# RE0 HD Remaster: reverse-engineering notes

Build: Steam app 339340, buildid 17178773, `re0hd.exe` 32-bit, image base 0x400000, **no ASLR** (addresses below are absolute). SteamStub-wrapped: `.text` is encrypted on disk and decrypted in memory before the game's own code runs. Adapter hooks must wait for decryption. Found with `tools/re0/probe.py` and `tools/re0/disasm.py` (runtime reads only).

## MT Framework type info
- Every class vtable slot 4 is `getDTI()` = `mov eax, <dti>; ret`. The DTI's field +4 is the class-name string.

## Globals (pointer to singleton)
| address | singleton |
|---|---|
| 0xdcbf3c | sPlayer* |
| 0xdcc0d0 | sGameChara* |
| 0xe2d7c8 | sGamePad* |

## sPlayer
| offset | meaning |
|---|---|
| +0x2c | controlled character (uPlayerBase*) |
| +0x3c | partner character (uPlayerBase*) |

The V key (partner switch) swaps +0x2c/+0x3c. sGameChara mirrors them at +0x148c/+0x149c.

## uPlayerBase (uPlayerBilly / uPlayerRebecca), about 0x6AD0 bytes
| offset | meaning |
|---|---|
| +0x40 | position vec3 (+pad) |
| +0x50 | rotation quaternion xyzw |
| +0x60 | scale vec3 |
| +0x70 | world matrix 4x4 (current); +0xB0 previous |
| +0x498, +0x4a0 + n*0x140 | motion state (static study 2026-10-19, "Pose puppet" below): +0x498 `mMotionCalcFlag`, `mMotion[8]` layers of 0x140 bytes; per layer +4 u16 motion number (bank in bits 8..11, 0xffff = none; layer 0 = +0x4a4), +0x40 `mInterFrame` (blend length in frames: the earlier "+0x4e0 float 8.0 idle / 6.0 walking" was this, not a frame), +0x4c **current frame** (layer 0 = +0x4ec, advanced each update), +0x54 frame max (+0x4f4), +0x5c speed (+0x4fc). +0xff8 `cAnimControl`, +0x6a70 byte "keep the motion's own displacement" |
| +0x67b8 | think (brain) object: cPlayerThink (vtable 0xccbbe8) when controlled, cPlayerSubThink (vtable 0xcc99b8) when partner. V swaps them. Setter at 0x508b1e |

## Think (brain) interface
- Slot 20 (+0x50) = which pad to read. cPlayerThink 0x4f9f10 → `sGamePad::getPad(0)`; cPlayerSubThink 0x4f4be0 → `getPad(1)` (the partner's arrow-key pad).
- `sGamePad::getPad(i)` at 0x600630 (thiscall, 1 stack arg, i < 2) returns the pointer at `sGamePad + 0xc24 + i*8`, or 0 while input is blocked.
- Player code calls think slots +0x1c, +0x20, +0x28, +0x38 on the character's think. +0x38 is a target-eligibility filter (loop of ≤ 20), not input.

## Think lifecycle (confirmed)
- `sPlayer::setControlled(player)` 0x4ecda0: `alloc(0xd0, 0x10)` (cdecl 0x4f81e0), `cPlayerThink` ctor 0x4f8010 (thiscall, returns this), `player->setThink(t)` 0x50f980 (thiscall, ret 4, destroys the old think), `sPlayer+0x2c = player`.
- `sPlayer::setPartner(player)` 0x4ece00: same flow with `cPlayerSubThink` (alloc 0x4f3760 size 0x1a0, ctor 0x4f3540), `sPlayer+0x3c = player`.
- V recreates both thinks; it does not swap objects.
- cPlayerThink DTI 0xdcebac. DTI vtable slot 1 = newInstance.

## Per-frame (tracer, 60 fps)
- `uPlayerBase::move` 0x513190 (thiscall, no args), vtable slot 41 (Billy reaches it through a wrapper at 0x51b683). It calls think slots 5, 6, 16, 21, 25 once per frame.
- cPlayerThink calls its getPad (slot 20) about 12x/frame, e.g. 0x4fa420: `pad = think->getPad(); return pad->vfunc[9]() == 1`.

## Pad object (vtable 0xce55e0, 36 slots)
- Slots 5..35 are action queries forwarding to two inner devices at +4 and +8 (keyboard/joypad), OR'ed. Most return bool.
- Slot 14 returns a struct via hidden out-pointer (`ret 4`), probably an analog vector.
- Slots 8/9/14/15 first check `sGamePad+0xbcd` and `0x5fe490(this+8)`.
- **Network pad plan:** an object with a copied vtable whose slots 5..35 return the remote player's recorded answers. The partner's cPlayerThink gets it via its getPad (slot 20).

## Co-op control model (decided 2026-09-29, revised)
- Fixed ownership: the host always owns Rebecca and the first peer always owns Billy, whoever is focused. Each machine always keeps its own character focused (camera_parity, in Team and Split up alike; V/Y do nothing with a peer): the party mode only decides door travel.
- Control rule (`character_owner::controlOf`, pure part in `control_rule.h`): no peer is vanilla; an unknown owner is vanilla on the host and Locked on a guest; both characters stay with their owners (Local or Remote) in both party modes; a Remote character is Locked while its owner reports another room than the one loaded here (its input belongs to that room). TEAM = following and doors together; LEAVE_BEHIND = independent play (nobody follows). The camera is per machine in both. Damage, inventory and state sync follow ownership only (`isRemoteOwned`/`isLocalOwned`).
- PLAYER_STATE (44 bytes) describes the sender's owned character (position, hp, characterId), whatever the camera is on; `focusedCharacterId` (the camera character) is diagnostic only.
- Menus: menu_mirror focuses the own character for the menu and camera_parity keeps it afterwards.
- Party commands are adapter-level because the game reads V/E from the focused character's think, whose input on a non-owner's machine is the remote pad. `command_input` polls KC_change (V) and KC_trace (E) from `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CAPCOM\RESIDENT EVIL 0 HD REMASTER\config.ini` from the game's own input (see "Command input source" below). V/Y are only hidden from the game (SWITCH_REQUEST 0x0103 is gone; each player keeps their own camera). E toggles the party mode: PARTY_REQUEST 0x0109 to the host, which flips and broadcasts PARTY_MODE 0x010A (reliable, also every 2 s); toasts "Team" / "Leave behind: <character> waits". The vanilla E toggles sPlayer +0x40 (u8, 1 = partner follows); party_mode now owns that byte while a peer is connected.
- Vanilla camera model: both clients keep the same controlled character (sPlayer+0x2c) and show the same room. The owner of each character drives it with their pad; the other client replays that input.
- If the partner is in another room it idles as in vanilla. Split-room simulation is deferred (too much data).
- The V switch changes the active character. Only the owner ever drives a character.
- One PC cannot run two RE0 instances: SteamStub ends the second one. Testing uses the echo peer, and real tests need two Steam accounts that each own RE0.

## Proven in-game (2026-09-29)
- Giving the partner a cPlayerThink (alloc + ctor + setThink) is stable, and the partner then obeys the pad exactly like the controlled character (identical 90° turn, both walk). **Co-op strategy: the remote player drives the partner via input replay; state sync only corrects drift.**

- **Network input replay works end to end** (echo test, 1 s delay): the partner reproduces the sender's turns and walks with native animation; drift snaps are rare.
- `getPad` returns a dummy pad (`sGamePad+0xc34`, vtable 0xce58b8) while input is blocked; only vtable 0xce55e0 objects are real pads.
- The joypad's stick query (slot 14) writes its out-vector with `movaps`, so the buffer must be 16-byte aligned.

## Enemies (found 2026-09-29, hardware write breakpoint via tools/re0/watch_write.py)
- Classes uEnemyNN, object about 0x6cc0 bytes, same transform layout as players (+0x40 pos, +0x50 quat).
- **HP: i32 at +0x1030** (fresh uEnemy11/12/16 = 94, handgun hit = 16). Dead enemies hold -1, re-written every frame by their update (0x526c7d → 0x42105d → 0x41eb60 → 0x424a2a).
- `setHP(this, i32)` 0x529310 (thiscall, ret 4). It is called by per-class code.
- **Damage entry: enemy vtable slot 35 (+0x8c)**, `damage(attacker, HitPoint*, HitInfo*)` thiscall ret 0xC. uEnemy11 implements it at 0x4200c0. The second argument is a pointer to the world-space hit point (3 floats), not a distance (corrected 2026-10-07, see "Hit point" below).
- Hit resolver 0x5313b0 (ret 0x18) builds `HitInfo { i32 rangeTier; i32 attackType; i32 a; i32 b; void* attacker; u8 flag }` and calls slot 35. rangeTier 0..3 comes from distance thresholds. attackType 0x1b takes a special path through sPlayer (0x4eca10). Caller chain: 0x7d4d20 → 0x542710 → 0x52f520 → 0x5313b0.
- **sEnemy\*** global at 0xdcdc78. Its enemy pool is at sEnemy+0x4b0: 16-byte entries `{vtable 0xcbd01c, next, prev, uEnemy*}`: seven per-class tables of 20 back to back, 140 slots (`game::kEnemyPoolSlots`; +0x4a8 = 0x25 = 37 is not the pool size). **Pool slot index = enemy network id** within a machine; across machines enemies bind by spawn record.
- The 38 enemy class vtables (slot 35 = damage; 18 distinct implementations): 0xcbdcd8, 0xcc4f28, 0xcc50a0, 0xcc5218, 0xcc5390, 0xcc5508, 0xcc5680, 0xcc57f8, 0xcc5970, 0xcc3fe8, 0xcbf0d0, 0xcbf5f8, 0xcbf9a0, 0xcbfbc8, 0xcbfe58, 0xcc01a8, 0xcc0458, 0xcc08c0, 0xcc5ae8, 0xcc0cb0, 0xcc13d8, 0xcc1618, 0xcc18e8, 0xcc5c60, 0xcc1ce8, 0xcc1e60, 0xcc1fd8, 0xcc25d8, 0xcc2b38, 0xcc2f70, 0xcc3248, 0xcc3710, 0xcc5dd8, 0xcc5f50, 0xcc60c8, 0xcc6240, 0xcc63b8, 0xcc6530.
- Co-op plan: the guest sends {enemy id, attackType, a, b, rangeTier, hit point, attacker character}. The host calls slot 35 with its own object for that character. Still open: a stable enemy id (index in sEnemy's list), and enemy state replication to the guest.
- Player damage: player HP is i32 at +0x1030 (Rebecca 150, Billy 161 seen), set with the same setHP 0x529310. `uPlayerBase::takeDamage(a, b)` 0x50fb30 (thiscall, 2 stack args, ret 8) is called from the player's own update, takes the amount from sEnemy 0x416de0 and floors HP at 1. Enemies also call it from their own update (0x76f9e8 → 0x5127c5).
- **Player hits (damage and death):** the earlier reading that every hit goes through vtable slot 38 0x512080 as `processHits` is wrong (corrected 2026-10-19): 0x512080 is a reset / re-entry (it calls `Player_Reset` 0x516440, changeState(0, 0, 1), think slot 6 and the ground snap; `setWeaponType` calls it) and nothing hooks it. Enemy hits and grabs enter through slot 34 0x512570 ("Pose puppet" below). Normal hits call takeDamage. The lethal hit calls think slot 6 → 0x4fcea0 (cPlayerThink slot 28) → 0x4fd010, which does setHP(0). The adapter gates setHP and onDeath for a remote-owned character (`player_damage`), and PLAYER_STATE carries the owner's HP.
- Ownership gate (2026-09-29): damage also reaches players through queued damage in their own update (0x513570 → 0x4fd160 → takeDamage) and the lethal hit (think->changeState 0x4fb2a0 → cPlayerThink slot 28 `onDeath(player)` 0x4fcea0 → killPlayer 0x4fd010 → setHP 0). So the adapter gates **setHP** (remote-owned players ignore non-authoritative writes) and **onDeath** (skipped for remote-owned characters; the owner sends PLAYER_DIED 0x0120 and the other machine replays onDeath). The partner's cPlayerThink is allocated at 0x1a0 and zero-filled.
- Crash 2026-09-29: heap corruption (0xc0000374) when the echo-driven partner Billy "died"; root cause not confirmed.

## Rooms, doors, menus, saves (2026-09-29)
- **Door entry point:** every door calls `sRoomControl::changeRoom(room, entry, flags)` at 0x610c60 (thiscall on sRoomControl 0xdcbeb4, ret 0xC; the only caller is the door thunk 0x552720, flags 0x20001, room/entry from the door object +0x20/+0x24). It calls 0x61e2c0 on sSceneInfo (0xdcbf40), which carries the partner (sPlayer +0x3c) along when sPlayer +0x40 (follow) is set and partner +0xff4 equals the scene's current room record (sSceneInfo-side +0x20; record +8 = room id). The carry repositions the partner (writes at 0x61edcb / 0x61b321) and does not change partner +0xc or +0xff4 (+0xff4 is the same object in both rooms). Writing +0xff4/position by hand leaves the game half-updated (E refused, crash on the partner's next door).
- **Story flags:** sFlagManager (global 0xdcc014, size 0x144, vtable 0xcd9184, DTI 0xdcff60) holds the script flags as 0x47 dwords at +0x20. Script opcodes (table of 12-byte rows {handler, signature, name} from 0xcd57d8; corrected 2026-10-07, the rows were read one field off before, see "Room script events"): FlagSet 0x577790 calls set(index, value) 0x59bd50; FlagSetMB 0x577830 calls set(index, count, value) 0x59c980 (thiscall, ret 0xC); FlagSetF 0x5778d0 goes through 0x59c960; SetLocalFlag 0x577f50 uses the singleton at 0xdcbebc. flag_sync diffs the 0x47 words (FLAG_DIFF 0x010C).

All script flag families share that bitset. Each setter adds its operand to a base: story 0x000 (FlagSet), enemy 0x312 (SetEnemyFlag 0x577970 through 0x59c320), effect 0x412 (SetEffectFlag 0x577c00), act 0x612 (SetActFlag 0x577ac0), display 0x712 (SetDisplayFlag 0x571710), bgm 0x812 (SetBGMFlag 0x577dc0). Bit n is `(sFlagManager + 0x20)[n >> 5] >> (n & 31)` (getter 0x59b8d0). The generic setter 0x59bd50 (set(index, value), thiscall, ret 8) writes the bit, then runs per-family side effects: enemy bits 0x312..0x411 (spawn/clear through 0x61a2b0, 0x4109f0, 0x411ec0, 0x619e30), effect bits 0x412..0x611 (0x560720/0x560740 when set, 0x55d9e0/0x55da00 when cleared), display bits 0x712..0x811 (0x59b6a0). Act and bgm bits are plain bit writes there; the BGM wrapper SetBGMFlag 0x577dc0 calls the BGM refresh 0x643490 when the value changes. Per-flag table with the rooms that set and read each one: docs/re/RE0_FLAGS.md; the classifier is adapters/re0/src/flag_family.h.
- **Room phases:** sRoomControl (0xdcbeb4) +0xb8 is the phase manager (vtable 0xce6bdc): +0x8 count 0x18, +0xc array of phase objects, +0x14 current, +0x18 requested (-1 none), request(phase) 0x60a340 (thiscall, ret 4; sRoomControl::requestPhase 0x610e00 adds 0xb8). Index: 0 Init, 1 Main, 2 Message, 3 MessageImm, 4 DoorLoad, 5 SubScreen, 6 Save, 7 UpCut, 8 Option, 9 Change, 10 EventDemo, 11 Map, 12 Movie, 13 Event, 14 Dead, 15 Opening, 16 StaffRoll, 17 Ranking, 18 WeskerTitle, 19 WeskerRanking, 20 OmakeTitle, 21 OmakeResult, 22 PlayDemo, 23 Exit (read from the live array, 2026-09-29). Class vtables: cRoomPhaseMain 0xce6b90, Dead 0xce68a8, EventDemo 0xce6a20, Movie 0xce6ea0.
- **Zapping (V):** the vanilla V requests room phase Change (9) through sRoomControl::requestPhase 0x610e00 from the player think (0x4fed48 when the target is the partner, 0x50395e); the Change phase does the swap and loads the partner's room when apart. camera_parity swaps directly in the same room and requests Change when the partner is in another room (untested in game).
- **Save manager / slots:** singleton 0xdcc018 (+0x20 request object; dispatch 0x6133d0 on op +4: 1 0x612ad0, 2 0x612c40, 3/4 0x613080 save, 5 0x612e30 load, 6 0x612d20). Save request (slot, 0) thiscall ret 8: op 3 0x6134c0 (via 0x613cb0), op 4 0x613500 (save by copy, via 0x613cc0); both come from the save GUI 0x5d1d00 (slot = cursor, then 0x15 for the system data), the options menu and 0x5d13b0. Load request (slot) ret 4: op 5 0x613390 (via 0x613c40, called from the load list 0x5d18f0 with its cursor index and from the title 0x5eb150 with 0x15). Verified 2026-10-21 in the decomp (0x6136d0 copies the live game into the slot, 0x6127d0 is its mirror) and in the image bytes (each request stores its op, 3 / 4 / 5, at +4). Slots 0..19 are player saves; slot 21 (0x15) is the system data read at boot ("Load successful."). From boot, Enter on every screen reaches the game through the notice, title (Load Game default), load list and Continue.
- **Game over:** Main -> Dead (14) phase; uGUIGameOver vtable 0xcdb2f0, sGameOver 0xcdb288. Setting HP to 0 does not kill; death only comes through the damage path. Writing the phase manager's request field directly crashed the game (use requestPhase). The co-op flow does not read the game over menu: the guest waits muted and continues (Enter) once the host is in game again, assumed to be the default choice (unverified).
- **Save sharing:** the host's typewriter save goes to the Steam cloud through the remote-storage proxy; a successful write sends SAVE_CHANGED to the launcher, which re-sends data0.bin (2.3 MB) to the guests. A guest whose game is running gets it in `coop\session\staging` (the launcher's receiver writes `.part`, then moves it in; it promotes the folder only after the game exits), so `save_redirect` reads and writes the staged copy first (`save_redirect_rule::source`): a continue after a game over loads the host's newest save. Before 2026-10-25 the staged copy was not read and a guest kept the save from its launch until a restart.
- **Submenu open:** sSubMenu::open 0x5d9030 (thiscall, no args) resets the menu and sets state +0x2c = 0; it runs from 0x60bef0 and shows the focused character's inventory. States then step 1..6 while open, 7/0xb/0xc on the way out, 0x0d closed.
- **door_sync:** hooks sDoorLoad::start 0x552b50 (thiscall (room, entry, a3, a4, flag), ret 0x14; stores +0x20/+0x24/+0x34/+0x38, resets the phase +0x44 and switches sRoomControl to the door phase; callers are the door script request 0x552ae0 and two other script paths). The owner of the focused character runs it and sends DOOR_CHANGE 0x010B {room, entry, a3, a4, flag, u8 character}; the other machine suppresses its own starts and runs the received one on the game thread once no door is active, so it plays the door and loads the room normally. Calling changeRoom 0x610c60 directly skips the door animation and leaves the room half-loaded (room reads 0xffff until a camera cut). F8: doors sent/run/blocked.
- **Only the focused character can act on doors:** door (and other interaction) scripts use the condition 0x564070 (cdecl bool(void*, ctx*)): controlled player +0x16e4 (trigger zone; also kept for the partner, -1 outside any zone) == ctx +8, getPad(0) action pressed (pad vtable +0x48), sPlayer 0x4ec1e0(7) == 0. The door script then calls sDoorLoad::request 0x552ae0 (from script opcode 0x579010); sDoorLoad (vtable 0xcd3120, update 0x552300) counts down +0x2c and calls the thunk 0x552720 -> changeRoom. door_sync runs the check with sPlayer +0x2c temporarily set to the local partner when the peer owns the focused character; when it passes, focus moves to the local character first, and DOOR_CHANGE carries that character id so the peer focuses it too.
- **In current room:** player +0xff4 is the loaded record and the hide mask +0xfc8 has no reason 2 ("room not loaded"). +0xc bit 0x4000 is the draw bit (set when +0xfc8 & ~1 is 0), not room membership: it is also clear for a character in the room on the other floor (reason 3) or hidden by a script or parent (reason 1); see "An undrawn puppet froze" under the state-mode fixes.
- **Current stage/room:** sGameInfo (global 0xdcbe9c) +0x2a80 stage, +0x2a84 room; the record at +0x2a88 keeps the previous room in its first dword. uScrDisp (recreated per room) also has mStageNo +0x30, mRoomNo +0x34, mReqRoom +0x44 (from MT property registration at 0x63a950).
- **Door transition:** sDoorLoad (global 0xdcbeb8) +0x44 (i32): 0 → 1 → 2 → 4 during the transition, 5 idle afterwards; after a fresh save load the idle value is 0xFFFFFFFF (-1), so a door is running only while the phase is in 0..4 (`door_phase.h`). F8 panel lines: door phase (raw), room (hex).
- **Inventory / pause menu:** sSubMenu (global 0xdcebd0) +0x2c: 0x0d closed; other values while a submenu or the pause screen runs.
- sGamePause (0xdcbea8) +0x24 is only a focus-loss marker; setting it does not pause the game.
- **Adapter door/menu behaviour:** `door_travel` latches the door start (game tick and net thread, level-triggered) with whether the partner had the in-room flag. The partner is not moved by the adapter: writing partner +0xff4 (room object) and position makes it visible but leaves the game's room membership half-updated (E command refused, crash when the partner later walks through a door). Instead party_mode mirrors TEAM/LEAVE_BEHIND into the game's own follow flag, sPlayer +0x40 (u8, 1 = partner follows; the vanilla E toggles it), so the vanilla door logic carries a following partner. No partner AI is given at door start; partner_think skips applying while the door runs and for 30 frames after (polled every tick from `game_state::doorActive`). Arrival sends ROOM_STATE {u16 room, u8 partnerInRoom, u8 pad} and forces one position snap. PLAYER_STATE carries `room` in the former reserved bytes; corrections only apply when the rooms match. Remote-owned characters are corrected continuously after their own move: drift under 3 is ignored, up to 60 moves 0.25 of the way (position and rotation) toward the owner's newest state extrapolated by velocity (capped 100 ms), beyond 60 snaps. `menu_mirror` sends MENU_STATE {u8 open}.
- **Save:** the full data0.bin image is assembled in memory and written by 0x6133d0 → 0x612ad0 → 0x8b3280 → 0x8b3940 → ISteamRemoteStorage::FileWrite("data0.bin", buffer, 0x23a8f0). The step that serializes the live game into a slot is 0x6136d0 (op 3 step 1; op 4 copies a slot with 0x613930 first); the dispatch 0x6133d0 is reached from sSaveManager::v6 0x613c50.
- Other statics: sRoomControl 0xdcbeb4, sLoading 0xdcf688. MT property names ("mRoomNo", "mPause"…) point at registration code that gives exact offsets.
- **D3D9 device:** hook d3d9!Direct3DCreate9 inline before the decryption wait; the game creates its device within about 1 s of start. (0xdc0790 is not a reliable device pointer.)

## Unit update dispatcher and world freeze (2026-09-29, static disassembly)
- `sUnit::updateAll` **0x727b50**: thiscall, no args, plain `ret` (vtable 0xd1bfd8 slot 6, also referenced by vtable 0xcf3bf8). Once per frame, called from 0x67ce00 (`call [eax+0x18]`) via 0x72373c. Wrapped in a scope object (0xa3b4e0/0xa3b510), loops groups `i < [0xe2d434]+0x620` calling slot 9.
- Slot 9 **0x7279e0**: thiscall, 1 stack arg (group index), `ret 4`. This is the function containing return address 0x727b0e. Per unit in the group's list (+0x2c, next at +0x14): state 1 runs unit slot 5 (init) and becomes 2; state 2 with flag 0x400 calls unit slot 8 (+0x20), the move chain (player slot 8 0x526b30 leads to `uPlayerBase::move` 0x513190 through slot 41 0x51b680). Units flagged in the group's +0x28 bit 0 are queued on a job list at [0xe2d904]+0xac instead. Slot 10 (0x727bb0, run per frame by slot 11 0x727ce0) is a second phase: after init it calls unit slots 8 and 10 once, then unit slot 9 (+0x24) each frame.
- Neither function touches a pad, input or a render call: they only walk unit lists. The engine itself early-outs both when `[0xe2d904]+0x60 == 0 && byte [0xe2d904]+0x64 != 0` (its own pause), so skipping the whole call is a state the engine already supports.
- Adapter freeze: `menu_mirror` hooks 0x727b50 (once per frame, no args) and returns without calling the original while a peer's menu is open and ours is closed. Rendering, input polling and the net thread keep running. Side effects: `game_tick` callbacks (run from the controlled player's move) do not fire while frozen, so the freeze state is evaluated in the detour itself and MENU_STATE is sent from the net thread. The second phase (unit slot 9, e.g. animation/motion) is not hooked and keeps running, so frozen characters may still animate.
- The vanilla screens do not stop calling 0x727b50: they set sUnit stop reasons, which make slot 9 skip whole groups (section "Live menus"). Door state values are still seen only at the ends of a transition when the callback stops firing.

## Items (2026-09-29)
- sItem* 0xdcbf44 holds inventories as fixed per-character blocks (unchanged by the V switch): **Rebecca at +0x24, Billy at +0x64**, 0x40 bytes each = 6 slots of {u32 itemId, u32 count} plus 0x10 of extras (third extra dword = the equipped slot index). 0xb4 marks the second half of a 2-slot item. Seen ids: 0x68 Hunting Gun, 4 Handgun, 0x20 Handgun Bullets, 2 Knife, 0x2d herb.
- sItem slot writers: set 0x4dae04/0x4dae8b, remove 0x4db2b9, add on pickup 0x4dc269. The drop (Leave) runs through the player's update: 0x513570 → 0x500db2 → 0x50e1c4 → 0x5d763e → 0x4dc750.
- sItemPut* 0xdce0a8 = the dropped floor items. Layout (static, from the ctor 0x4de150, dtor 0x4de1f0, DTI 0xdce9bc, vtable 0xcc7bfc, getDTI = slot 4 0x4df910): the object holds **28 records of 0x24 bytes at +0x20** (ctor loops 0x1c+1 times calling the record ctor 0x4de0c0; initial record = all zero except the dword at +0x14 = -1). A cleared record seen at runtime: {u32 room?, ..., u32 itemId, u32 count, u16/u16 position-ish, i16 -1, i16 -1, u8}. Field meaning is NOT confirmed statically, and there is no used flag found: the adapter treats the 1008-byte array as plain data and compares it whole. The names mUseItemCount/mUseItemHead/mEmptyItemCount/mEmptyItemHead belong to a different class (property list at 0x55cb40, xref of "mpStageEffectManager"), not sItemPut; sItemPut's own slot 3 (0x608180) is generic. Level-placed items live elsewhere (0x4dfa00 reads a stage item table, keyed by 0x71/0x73 kinds; pickup flags not found yet).
- Item sync (adapter): INVENTORY 0x0106 `{u8 characterId, u8 block[0x40]}` reliable, sent by the owner of a character when its sItem block changed and stayed unchanged for 10 frames, and every 5 s; the receiver overwrites the block only if it treats that character as remote-owned. Floor items are synced by event, see below.

- **sItemPut record layout (runtime-checked 2026-09-29):** 28 records of 0x24 bytes at object +0x20: {u32 key, u32 0, u32 itemId, u32 count, **u32 pointer to the spawned uItem object**, u32 0, u32 0xff, u32 0, u32 0}; an empty record has -1 at +0x14. Because of the live pointer, whole-record overwrites are unsafe. Whole-record overwrites are unsafe, so floor sync replays the game's own put/remove instead.

- **Floor put/remove (static analysis 2026-09-29, untested in game):** `sItemPut::put` 0x4de500, thiscall, ret 0xC, `this` = sItemPut* (0xdce0a8; the caller gets it as `0x404fe0(0x480460(&tmp))`, a weak-pointer accessor chain). Args: (1) `ItemDesc*` {u32 itemId, u32 count, u32 0}; (2) **position** vec3, copied to the new uItem at +0x40; (3) **Euler rotation** vec3, passed (y minus a constant) to 0x719f20, which builds the unit's rotation. The Leave caller (0x5e4b84 in 0x5e1d10) builds arg 3 with 0x67fcc0 = matrix to Euler angles (`this` = a 4x4 built from the player's facing at 0x5e4a63, output pointer returned), so it depends only on the dropper's facing; the adapter reproduces it by forwarding the rotation the local put received. put also reads `sPlayer->controlled + 0xff4` (must be non-null) and returns the uItem. `sItemPut::remove` 0x4de730, `ret 4`, one arg (the uItem*); it loads `this` from [0xdcbf40] itself, so ecx is ignored. Adapter (`floor_items_sync`): both are MinHooked. A local call (not while applying a network event) runs the original and then sends FLOOR_PUT 0x0107 `{u16 room, u16 0, u32 itemId, u32 count, f32 pos[3], f32 rot[3]}` or FLOOR_TAKE 0x0108 `{u16 room, u16 0, u32 itemId, f32 pos[3]}` (reliable; the take reads itemId and position from the uItem before the original frees it). Calls made inside a remote-owned character's move scope are suppressed and not broadcast. The receiver (game thread) applies an event when `room` equals our current room and the room is ready (no door running, 30 frames after door arrival, item table and controlled character present); otherwise it goes to a per-room pending queue (`floor_pending`, cap 32 per room, oldest dropped), where a TAKE cancels the nearest matching pending PUT (same itemId within 50 units) instead of being stored. When a room becomes ready its pending events apply in arrival order through the same apply code (reentrancy flag, no re-broadcast). RE0 keeps no plain in-memory table of other rooms' floor items (only the current room's sItemPut; other rooms are presumably in the save image), hence the adapter-side queue. Not covered: a late joiner sees nothing already on the floor, and the queue is lost on restart. F8 panel: floor pending (gauge), pend appl/drop. TAKE finds the local record with the same itemId whose uItem (+0x40) is within 50 units and removes it; no match counts as a take miss. Risks: put may also run when a room loads persisted items (would duplicate for a peer already there); the suppressed remote-move case assumes Leave/pickup run inside the character's move.

- **Pickup action (corrected 2026-09-29):** step 0x500d00 (thiscall, state object, 1 arg player, ret 4; state +0x10 phase 0 start / 1 take / 2 done, +0x18 = the player). The take reads `[player + 0x67a0] + 4` (0x4ded40 is just that accessor), and player+0x67a0 is the interaction target, cleared when the item is removed, with no null check (crash at 0x500da3). The adapter's pickup_guard ends the action (phase 2) when the target is null.

- **Room membership (confirmed in-game 2026-09-29):** player +0xff4 = pointer to the room object the character belongs to. Each frame the player update (0x529990..0x529a1f) sets "hidden reason" bits in +0xfc8 through 0x6534f0(bit, set); reason bit 2 = 0x61b0b0([0xdcbf40], room obj, 1), i.e. "room not loaded". +0xc bit 0x4000 (active/in room) is on only when +0xfc8 & ~1 == 0. **Setting a partner's +0xff4 to the focused character's value and writing its position (+0x40) brings it into the current room** (tested: reasons 0x4 -> 0, the partner appears).

## Keyboard (config.ini)
W/S/A/D move, Space run/attack, Shift aim, F action, **V switch character**, E partner follow toggle, arrows move the partner, Q map, C status. **Space cancels menus**; Esc opens pause (Quit Game is on that menu).

## Co-op design direction
Each client controls its own character. On each machine, the remote player is the partner. The partner is driven by network state; replacing its think vs mirroring its state is decided after in-process tracing.

## Party command path (revised 2026-09-29)
E/V are polled by `command_input::onNetTick` on the net thread (~5 ms), not from a game_tick callback and not from the render path: the net loop keeps running through menus, doors and the world freeze, so the raw-key edge detector never stalls (a hook-driven detector missed edges whenever move() stopped). A press logs `command: <name> pressed (keyboard|controller)`; it becomes a request in gameplay with a peer connected. Host and guest presses share one request path (`party_mode::queueToggle`; a guest's PARTY_REQUEST calls the same function); the host decides on the game thread and logs applied or ignored with the reason. Panel: last command and last decision with age. `key_config` reads the first `KC_change`/`KC_trace` line in any section whose value is a known `KB_` name.

## Separate rooms study (2026-09-30, unmodified game, solo)
Goal: both players in different rooms at once (each machine loads its own player's room). Findings so far:
- Leaving the partner behind (E = stay, controlled character takes a door) clears the partner's in-room bit
  (+0xc 0x4000); the game keeps simulating only the loaded room.
- Each character has an `sCollision::SbcInfo` at +0x1870 (vtable 0xcc3fb4): +0x1880 stage, +0x1884 room of the
  collision set it uses. The left-behind partner keeps its old room there (2, 3 in the test).
- **SbcInfo is not the partner's room record.** Rewriting the partner's SbcInfo room and position, then reloading
  rooms, changed nothing: on return the game placed the partner back in its real room at a position of its own
  (241, 600, 2852) and set the in-room bit. A separate partner room/position record exists.
- Current-room record: sGameInfo +0x2a7c {entry, stage, room, previous room}; +0x2a90..+0x2a9c gets a copy once
  the characters split (0xffff while together). Candidate for the partner's room: sGameInfo +0x1e5c (3 in both
  snapshots, the room the partner stayed in); needs a third sample with the partner in a different room.
- Controls in this setup are camera-relative (D moves right on screen), not tank controls.

What separate rooms needs, in order:
1. The partner room/position record (write it when the remote player changes rooms, so a reunion places them
   correctly through the game's own logic).
2. Camera parity off while apart: each machine focuses its own player's character.
3. door_sync only between players in the same room (already the TEAM rule) and no door forcing while apart.
4. Enemy authority per room: whoever is in a room simulates its enemies; host authority only when together.

**Scene records (static analysis of the door carry 0x61e2c0, confirmed in game 2026-09-30).** sSceneInfo (0xdcbf40)
keeps up to 4 room records (pointers at +0x2dc70); +0x20 is the loaded one. A character belongs to the record at its
+0xff4; a room a character was left in stays as a dormant record (that is the "hidden" partner room: record +8 = scene
id, the same id doors pass, e.g. 0x59, 0x61, 0x24; +0x9aa4 = entry spots, 0x18 bytes per (entry * 3 + mode)). The carry
moves a character with: [0xdcc010]->0x411570(-1, player +0xff0) (unit registry, detach); 0x61e0f0(scene, flags)
on sSceneInfo (find the room's record, or load it dormant; flags = [0xdd1d44] | 4); 0x61ed50(player, entry, mode) on
the record (entry spot, mode 0 = through the door, 2 = following partner); 0x619e30(record, player) on sSceneInfo (leave
and enter callbacks, +0xff4); 0x411570(scene, handle) again; 0x61dde0(record) releases an empty dormant record.
`scene::move` runs exactly that for one character. Tested with the fake session: Billy sent from the loaded 0x59 to
0x24 vanished with no screen change; sent back he stood at the door's spot (241, 600, 2852, where the game itself puts
him); moved away (0x59 released) and back (0x59 loaded fresh, same slot reused), then Rebecca walked into 0x59 through
its door and found him there. A rejoining guest took Billy through his door into 0x61 and Rebecca was moved into
dormant 0x24; the fake host's Rebecca then walked into 0x61 and appeared at the door. The in-room flag (+0xc 0x4000)
of a character moved into a dormant record can stay set (it only updates while the character's room runs), so "in the
loaded room" also compares +0xff4 with the loaded record.

So split rooms needs no replay: a peer's door moves the peer's character in place (split_rooms.cpp, always on), the
save and later doors see the engine's own state. LEAVE_BEHIND is independent play (nobody follows; the own camera on each machine is
unconditional since 2026-10-06). Enemies: the machine that was in a room first keeps simulating it when the other walks in (ROOM_STATE carries
the claim; host on a tie). **A dormant record never spawns the room (2026-10-06, first two-PC session).** `0x61e0f0` returns an existing record of the scene at once (and with flag 2 makes a second one), so a record `scene::move` made dormant for the peer's door is reused by a later local door into that room: no room load runs, the room has no enemies or floor items (log: `enemy_state: slot 0 has no local enemy`). Cause inferred from the log and the disassembly, not yet seen live. `split_rooms::beforeLocalDoor` therefore moves a peer character that sits alone in the target's dormant record into the loaded room (which frees the record), the door loads the room fresh like a never-visited one, and `onArrival` moves the character back to its door entry. Enemy state then applies slot by slot as before. Retest: guest enters a room first while split, host walks in, enemies and items exist on the host.
Cutscenes that move the peer's character hand the result to its owner (event_place).

**The game's own switch (V)** is a multi-step action in the player think (0x4fec64..: states 3/4/5): in the same room it
swaps directly (no room phase), apart it requests Change from 0x4fed48 / 0x50395e. Refusing requestPhase(Change) misses
the first path, so the switch is cut at its input: the DirectInput keyboard never reports the KC_change key during co-op
(virtual_keys), which also keeps it out of the pad frames the peer replays. A gamepad's switch button is undone by
camera_parity's focus keeping (always, with a peer).

**Zap right after a load asserts (2026-10-01).** Requesting the Change phase 0.1 s after a save reached Main (the
own character dormant in another room) crashed in the engine's room-record assert (strings 0xcb57ac/0xcb57d0, via
0x618c99); the same zap 2 s or more later works. `character_owner::switchTo` refuses the zap (NotSettled) until the game
has been in plain gameplay for 2 s; swaps in the room stay immediate.

**Apart includes the own character.** A save can split the two (slot 2: Rebecca in scene 0x04, Billy dormant in 0x0a).
A guest loading it sees the host's room while its own Billy is elsewhere, so "apart" is also true when this player's own
character is outside the loaded room: the camera goes to it (zap, after the settle) and its room loads. Verified with
the fake host: the guest landed on Billy in 0x0a 2.1 s after the load; the join flow with that save also caught up there.

**Peer doors wait for plain gameplay.** door_sync ran a peer door during a local status screen: the door's character
took the camera and the door itself never started (lost). Doors, placements and the party commands now all wait for
`game_state::playing` (Main, no door, no menu). In the status screen the command keys are the game's again.

**Boot-time load:** on the title screen the game issues its own load request (0x613390) for slot 0 and for the system data (0x15), with no room phase. Redirecting
the slot 0 load into the empty co-op slot made the next load assert in the scene id check (crash at 0x401f78 via 0x610d8a). Until 2026-10-21 this was written up as a save and the hooks were swapped (see the guest saves note below); the co-op slot redirect now sits on the real save requests and only acts in the Save room phase.

**Pickups run only on the owner's machine (2026-10-06).** The replayed press of a remote-owned character started the pickup interaction on the other machine too, so the take prompt opened on both and the sequence played twice. `pickup_guard` now ends a remote-owned character's pickup action at its first step. Only the confirmed result crosses: FLOOR_TAKE comes from `sItemPut::remove` (the item left the floor, taken or used from the prompt; declining never calls it), the added item or used herb/ammo through INVENTORY and state sync. Not yet seen live.

**Enemy puppets (2026-10-06, first two-PC session).** The non-owner used to let its enemies run their own AI between 10 Hz snapshots and snapped them back when drift passed 80 (log: snaps every 1-4 s, drift 80-245). Enemy vtable slot 41 (0xa4) is the per-frame update for all 38 vtables (uEnemy classes share 0x420f70; it ends with the AI state dispatch `table[state]` at [enemy+0x67a4]); plain thiscall, no arguments. `enemy_update_hook` patches it, and `enemy_state::puppetSkipsUpdate` skips it on the non-owner while the owner reports the enemy alive, the snapshot is under 500 ms old and no HP drop came in the last 700 ms (so hit reactions and death still play through the game). Pose is blended every tick toward the position extrapolated from the last two snapshots and snapped only past 300 units; snapshots go out at 20 Hz. HP, death and hits stay owner-authoritative (HIT_REQUEST to the owner, HP back in the snapshot). Animation is not sent: a puppet holds its last pose while skipped and is carried by the pose blend. Unverified in game; if a skipped enemy looks frozen, widen the reaction window or run the update and only pin the pose.

**Enemy animation (2026-10-06).** `sUnit` runs units in phases (0x727b50 calls its slot 9 per phase), and the per-class update at slot 41 is only one of them, so skipping it should not stop the animator (phase 9, motion; the same reason a menu freeze left characters animating). Not proven live. The motion block is uModel +0x4a0 (property mMotion, DTI-headed record 0xd1d0dc; read live on a player and an enemy: motion 0x1b / 4, total frames at +0x4f4 = 132 / 180, speed 1.0 at +0x4e8). +0x4a4 u16 = mMotionNo (setter 0x73ef50 sets the dirty bit and copies +0x4e0 to +0x4e4), +0x4e0 f32 = `mInterFrame`, the blend length in frames (corrected 2026-10-19: the current frame is +0x4ec, not +0x4e0; the reading of +0x4e4 as the previous frame is unverified). ENEMY_STATE entries carry the motion number and frame (44 bytes); a puppet switches motion through the game setter when the number differs and rewrites the frame when it is more than 4 frames from the owner's extrapolated one (30 frames/s assumed). The samples were taken while nothing moved, so the frame field at +0x4e0 is inferred from the setter, not seen advancing: check with tools/re0/motion_probe.py on a walking enemy.

**Pickups, take 2 (2026-10-06).** Ending a remote-owned character's pickup action at its first step (f0fcca5) left the character frozen mid-motion on the other machine and did not stop the full-inventory path. The replayed press itself is now the thing removed: `net_pad`'s cloned pad answers 0 on query slot 18 (+0x48, "action pressed", the query doors use; items are assumed to use it too, not proven), so a remote-owned character never starts any interaction here, whichever branch (take, use, decline, full inventory). `pickup_guard` is back to only the missing-target guard. PLAYER_STATE now carries the owned character's motion number and frame (52 bytes); `state_correction::followMotion` re-times a drifted frame at once and switches the motion when it differs for 400 ms, so a remote character stuck in an animation follows the owner out of it. Equipped weapons: nothing in the adapter equips a weapon on a character; INVENTORY carries the block (third extra dword = equipped slot) but no call refreshes the held weapon object/model, so only what the replayed pad produces appears. Not yet found: the engine function that re-equips after the menu (sWeapon/uWeapon/IGameWeapon strings at 0xcd135c, 0xcd17a0, 0xcc9b28).

**Equip trace, host side (2026-10-06, tools/re0/equip_trace.py, handgun to shotgun on the controlled Rebecca).** Changes that belong to the equip: sItem +0x5c (Rebecca block +0x38, the equipped slot) 4 to 2; the character's motion number +0x4a4 0x209 to 0x309 with total frames +0x4f4 180 to 172 (the weapon class is the high byte of the motion number); controlled +0x0fe8 and +0x6950 3 to 5; controlled +0x6838 and +0x6840 (and the same pointer at +0x14 of the other character's uWeapon) pointing at a new uWeapon object 0x2de40060 while the old 0x2de58740 was destroyed: an equip creates a new uWeapon unit (size 0x67b0, DTI 0xdcf4dc, created through the DTI new at 0xcd1670, no direct caller) and destroys the old one. The other character's fields changed only by position noise. The engine function that performs the swap is not found yet; the inventory writers (0x4dc300 copy-in, 0x4dc370 copy-out of a block) do not touch the weapon. Next trace: the same equip with equip_trace.py running on the guest, where the host character is the partner copy; whatever does not change there is what an equip sync must reproduce.

**Equipped-slot writer (watch_write on sItem +0x5c, 2026-10-06, one equip, detached through the stop file).** `0x4db2f0` is `ItemBlock::setEquipped(slot)` (thiscall on a character's block, 1 stack arg, ret 4; -1 clears; it validates the slot with 0x4dd580 and stores it at block +0x3c, the instruction at 0x4db351). Hits: the inventory menu (caller 0x5e37de, the equip action in the menu code 0x5e360a..0x5e37d9, which then only updates the menu UI: 0x5e7240, 0x5eecc0, 0x5dc270, 0x5dc170) and two block copies (0x4dc300 copy-in from 0x5d7fcf / 0x5d7797). The equipped item id is read back by `0x4db380` (returns the id at the equipped slot or 0xffff), used all over the player think (0x4f75b0 weapon ready logic, 0x4f7e30). So setEquipped is data only: the uWeapon swap, the weapon resource and the motion class change are done elsewhere by code that reads the equipped id. Not found yet; the next watch is a write watch on the character object +0x6838 (the uWeapon pointer) during one equip, which gives the swapping function's stack. The player +0x6950 field (3 to 5 in the trace) is the player's action state, not a weapon class.

**Motion forcing and update skipping removed (2026-10-06, Build 139 crash and frozen enemies).** Host crash 0xc0000005 at 0x4fd370: the player think reads the low byte of the motion number (+0x4a4, via 0x50e0a0) as the key of a table lookup (0x4f9700, tables 0xccb788 / 0xccb7e0) and dereferences the result without a null check. `state_correction::followMotion` wrote the peer's motion number into the remote-owned character with the setter 0x73ef50 while its own state machine was in another state, so the key was not in the table and the lookup returned null. The motion number belongs to the character's own state machine: never write it from outside. The enemy side had the same risk and its sync was also unproven (the +0x4e0 field, read as the frame, was never seen advancing: it is `mInterFrame`, the blend length; the frame is +0x4ec); the guest's enemies were frozen because slot 41 is the whole per-class update (AI dispatch, animation and physics together), not just the AI. Removed: PLAYER_STATE motion fields, `followMotion`, the ENEMY_STATE motion fields, `enemy_update_hook` and `update_thunk` (slot 41), `model_motion`, `motion_rule`. Enemy puppets now run their own update (they animate) and their pose is blended toward the owner's every tick from 20 Hz snapshots. Still open: suppressing only the AI decisions of a puppet; each class has its own slot-41 function with its own dispatch (`table[state]` at [enemy+0x67a4] for the 0x420f70 family), so it needs a per-class hook on the dispatch, or restoring the AI-chosen state after the update. Enemy animation parity is open too. Reading the motion block (tools/re0/motion_probe.py) is safe; the layout notes above stay valid.

**Enemy AI dispatch, static findings (2026-10-06).** Every enemy class overrides vtable slot 41 (the per-frame update); 12 of the 18 distinct update functions end in a dispatch `table[state]` through a per-class table of function pointers, indexed by the shared base field **+0x67a4 (AI state index)**: 0x420f70 family (15 vtables) table 0xd7ab60 (5 states), others 0xd7bfcc, 0xd7cef0, 0xd7d588, 0xd7dad0, 0xd7ea08, 0xd7fbf8 / 0xd7fb60, 0xd80248, 0xd80b30, 0xd817a8, 0xd82408, 0xd82bd0, 0xd843fc, 0xd85338, 0xd8607c / 0xd86090; 6 classes (0x443610, 0x46cff0, 0x48af40, 0x4b24f0, 0x9e2ac0 stub, 0x4d7a90 with several) dispatch differently. In the 0x420f70 family the table entries are thunks `mov eax,[ecx]; jmp [eax+K]` into virtual slots 65..69 of the same object: the state handlers are virtual methods, and they mostly watch conditions (scene id at [record+8] via 0x526490 = [enemy+0xff4], HP via 0x522df0, 0x4cb950 flag tests) and request the next state through virtual slot 63 (+0xfc, `setState`-like, 4 arguments in the overriding classes: (1, newState, 0, 0)). The state writers (+0x67a4) are spread over 55 sites in the class code. So the AI is a state machine whose transitions are virtual calls: a puppet can be made to hold the owner's state by (a) sending +0x67a4 and skipping the transition-checking handlers, plus (b) running the class's own transition (slot 63) when the owner's state changes so the animation starts the way the game starts it. Neither is safe to write blind (an unmatched motion crashed the host today); it needs a live sample of state, motion and frame on a moving enemy: `tools/re0/enemy_state_probe.py` (read-only, prints every change of +0x67a4, motion number and frame). No enemies were loaded during my 10-minute probe.

**Weapon swap found and synced (2026-10-06, second watch on player +0x6838).** The weak pointer at player +0x6838 (the held weapon unit) is set by `0x529320` from `0x509c20` (attach the weapon unit), called by `0x50d870` = `player->setWeaponType(type)` (thiscall, ret 4: stores the type in +0x6950, creates the weapon unit through the factory 0x51fa20(…, 0x74, record, type), attaches it, re-applies the aim, and for a knife calls 0x50d6e0). The menu-close routine at 0x5d7740..0x5d77e3 runs, for the controlled player and the partner: `block = 0x50dc70(player); type = 0x4db2f0(block, block[+0x3c]); 0x50d870(player, type); if slot != -1 { 0x50d670(player); 0x516630(player) }`. +0x6950 is therefore the weapon type (3 handgun, 5 shotgun in the trace), +0x0fe8 its copy (0x526500). The adapter ran none of it for a remote-owned character, only copying the inventory block, so the partner kept the old weapon. `equip_refresh` replays that sequence when a received block changes the equipped slot of a remote-owned character (both directions, as each machine treats the peer's character as remote), after the resource step below (running the sequence alone T-posed the character).

**Enemy probe reading (host, 60 s, tools/re0/out/enemy_probe.txt).** AI state (+0x67a4) stayed 1 for the 0xcc5508 and 0xcc4f28 families while the motion number (+0x4a4) changed constantly (0x05f, 0x005, 0x02f, 0x037, 0x016, 0x01a, 0x00c): the motion number tracks the animation, not the AI state, so holding the state index would not match it. At each motion change the value at +0x4e0 (`mInterFrame`, the blend length; the frame is +0x4ec: corrected 2026-10-19) was a small number (0, 2, 4, 9, 16) and the total (+0x4f4) the motion length, which was read as a current-frame field; the probe only prints on a change, so it never shows the frame advancing, and the later idle samples (enemies not in motion, frame 0 constant) do not either. Still to verify: sample one walking enemy at 10 Hz and print the frame every sample. Animation design to follow: request the motion through the class's own transition or motion-request call, never a raw write of +0x4a4 (crashed the player think, see above).

**Enemy animation match (2026-10-06).** Superseded 2026-10-07 ("One decision maker per enemy" below); kept for the findings. Vtable slot 63 (+0xfc) of the enemy classes is `setAction(state, id, a, b)`: it stores the four ints at +0x67a4 / +0x67a8 / +0x67ac / +0x67b0, and the class's state handlers (slots 65..) start the motion for that record. Live probe (host, three enemies): records such as (1,2,1,0) with motion 0x004, (1,3,1,0) with 0x00c, (1,5,2,1) with 0x037, (1,8,0,1) with 0x016, (1,8,1,1) with 0x01a; a lying enemy shows (3,3,0,0) with 0x01b. The record is therefore what the AI chooses and the motion follows it; the state word alone (1) does not. The value at +0x4e0 stayed 0.00 / 16.00 on enemies that were moving across 1 s, so it is not a running frame (it is `mInterFrame`, the blend length; the running frame is +0x4ec: corrected 2026-10-19), which this design did not need. Implementation: the owner sends the record; a puppet whose record differs from it for 200 ms while the owner's has been stable for 120 ms is told the owner's record through its own setAction (the call its AI makes, never a raw write of +0x4a4), at most once per 400 ms. Only the five slot-63 implementations with four arguments (0x4cc670, 0x44d820, 0x4650f0, 0x480c20, 0x4bbc70) are called; 0x48ac80 (ret 8) and 0x4b17f0 (ret 4) are left alone, a wrong argument count would unbalance the stack. Not verified live: watch the F8 "enemy action requests" counter and the guest enemies' animation.

**Door entry spots (2026-10-06).** A record keeps three spots per entry (+0x9aa4 + (entry * 3 + mode) * 0x18, {x, y, z, facing}); read live in scene 0x37: mode 0 the door spot, mode 1 50 to 70 units beside it, mode 2 50 units behind it, except that at some entries mode 2 equals mode 0 (entry 1 of 0x37). The engine's own carry puts a following partner on mode 2. `scene::move` always used mode 0, so a peer's character placed into a room where the other character already stood (the peer arrived second through the same door, or the equip/room-free park of `split_rooms::beforeLocalDoor` returned it) landed on the same spot and the collision threw one of them. `spot_rule::modeFor` now picks mode 2 when the other character is already in the target record, mode 1 when mode 2 is the door spot, else mode 0. Both machines place only the REMOTE character this way; the local one is placed by its own game and state_correction then pulls the remote copy to its owner's real position, so one side (the owner) decides where a character stands. The game's TEAM carry through a door was already on mode 2. No collision query was added: the spots are the game's own, authored for two characters.

**Owner AI replication on the non-owner (2026-10-06).** Superseded 2026-10-07 ("One decision maker per enemy" below); kept for the findings. (1) The setAction slot (63) is patched on every enemy vtable with a generated thunk (`slot_thunk`, N = 4, 2 or 1 stack arguments by implementation: 0x4cc670 x32 classes, 0x44d820, 0x4650f0, 0x480c20, 0x4bbc70 take four; 0x48ac80 two, 0x4b17f0 one). On a puppet that is alive and has an owner record the enemy's own call is dropped; everything else (the owner machine, an enemy with no record yet, a dead one) calls the original. Our own application bypasses the thunk by calling the original directly, so no flag or thread guard is needed. Handlers that write the record directly are undone by the update wrapper (next paragraph); the 100 ms cooldown stops a flip-flop from re-applying every frame. (2) The owner's record is applied as soon as it differs from the local one, no waiting. (3) Target: see the next paragraph. Unverified live.

**Direct state writes and the target (2026-10-06, static, image.bin; `tools/re0/field_refs.py`, `slot_funcs.py`).** (1) Writers of the record word +0x67a4: 83 `mov [reg+0x67a4], imm/reg` sites in the class code (state 1 and 2 mostly; the rest of the 160 references compare), none of them through setAction, so the setAction thunk cannot see them. Slot 41 (the update, plain thiscall, no arguments; the sUnit callers push nothing) runs them all, so `enemy_action::onUpdate` wraps it: on a living puppet the four record words are saved before the update and written back after it. Our own setAction application happens outside the update (game tick), so it is untouched. Why a restore and not only a skipped think call: in the 0x420f70 family (15 vtables) the update dispatches `table[state]` (0xd7ab60) to virtual slots 65..69: state 0 (slot 65, 0x41e260) sets the enemy up, state 1 (66, 0x41ea80) is the action executor (per-action begin/update tables indexed by +0x67a8, then 0x4cc6a0), state 2 (67, 0x41db20) is the **think**: a chain of transition checks (0x422fe0, 0x423680, 0x423590, 0x423e90, 0x424340, ...) that end in setAction, then a per-action table call; it only sets flag bits (+0x6844, +0x684c) and moves nothing. That one function is shared by all 15 vtables and is hooked by MinHook (`enemy_action` `onThink`): on a living puppet it does not run. The executor and the per-action functions mix animation and movement with their own `state = 2` writes (action finished), so they cannot be skipped (a skipped whole update froze guest enemies, Build 139); the restore undoes those writes. The other 12 update implementations dispatch through their own tables with decisions and execution interleaved the same way; each would need its own analysis, so they get only the restore. (2) Target: the 15 vtables whose update is 0x420f70 call 0x421b20 from it (only caller, 0x421019). It takes sPlayer +0x2c (controlled, getter 0x4ec780) and +0x3c (partner, getter 0x96cd30), stores the distances to them in the enemy at +0x67cc / +0x67d0 (3.4e38 when that player is in another scene record), and picks the target with `comiss`: the partner only when strictly nearer; flags 6 and 2 (0x4cb4d0) force the controlled one. Result: **+0x6b80 = target uPlayerBase\*, +0x6b84 = target y minus own y, +0x6b88 = distance**. Because each machine's controlled player is its own character, a tie or a flag picks Billy on one screen and Rebecca on the other. The owner sends the character id (ENEMY_STATE entry byte 1, 0xFF none); `enemy_target` hooks 0x421b20 and, on a puppet, overwrites the three fields with the local object of that character. Other writers of +0x6b80: 0x43a016..0x43a182 (the own-class selector 0x439e90 of vtable 0xcbf0d0, called from its update at 0x43933d, plain thiscall; it writes the target object and +0x6b84 as the flat x/z distance (0x521bd0) and leaves +0x6b88 as a range, so its hook rewrites those two; its later range logic still uses the locally chosen target), 0x4c02e1 clears it. Both selectors are hooked. Damage to a character is still decided by the machine that owns it (player_damage), so a wrong target only ever looked wrong. Unverified live.

**Hit point, not distance: the enemy_net crash (2026-10-07, static, image.bin).** The second argument of the enemy damage slot 35 is a pointer to the world-space hit point (3 floats), not a float distance: 0x4200c0 copies [arg2], [arg2+4], [arg2+8] into the enemy at +0x6aa0..+0x6aa8 and compares the hit height (+0x6aa4) with the enemy's own (0x420157..0x420227, the head-or-body decision); 0x438b00 copies it to +0x6b20, 0x528ef0 pushes [arg2] and [arg2+4]. The hit resolver 0x5313b0 passes its own third argument there; its fourth is the real float distance, used only for rangeTier. The adapter had typed it `float distance` and sent the pointer's bits in HIT_REQUEST / HIT_APPLIED, so the receiver ran the game's damage function with an address from the peer's process: unmapped, an access violation inside `enemy_net`'s drain, which game_tick caught and disabled the callback for good (every occurrence, 2026-09-29 to 2026-10-07, came seconds after the first shots in room 0x25); mapped, a garbage hit point, so head hits (crits) differed between the screens. HitPayload now carries the 3 floats (32 bytes) and the receiver passes a local copy's address. The payload also carries the sender's loaded room, and a hit queued across a room load is dropped: pool slots are reused by the next room's enemies. game_tick now logs a caught exception with its code, address (game VA and module+offset) and the address an access violation touched, writes coop\caught-<date>-<time>.dmp for the first one, toasts "Sync stopped: <name>", and a resync (F9, resync_now.txt or a lasting room desync) re-arms the callback once the snapshot is sent (host) or applied (guest). resync_now.txt was never taken because its name was written `L"\resync_now.txt"` (a carriage return, not a backslash). Unverified live.

**Command input source (2026-10-07).** The commands used to read the keyboard with GetAsyncKeyState and accept a press only while `GetForegroundWindow` belonged to the game. On the guest's PC every E / LT press that night logged `foreground=0` and was ignored, while the guest kept playing (a door the guest opened 4 s after an ignored press). The game opens its keyboard foreground-only (SetCooperativeLevel flags 0x16 = NOWINKEY | FOREGROUND | NONEXCLUSIVE at 0x807691; the mouse 0xa = BACKGROUND | NONEXCLUSIVE at 0x808057) but reads XInput with or without the focus, so a keyboard player cannot play without the focus and the guest was most likely on a controller with another window focused (inferred from the log, not confirmed with the guest): the game took the controller's input while the commands refused its LT. Fix: the commands read the input the game reads. Keys come from the game's own DirectInput keyboard read (`virtual_keys` records each read's real state before hiding; a failed read, which is what a foreground-only device returns without the focus, counts as all up), so a key typed into another window still never counts, and the controller is accepted whenever the game reads it. The log line names the source.

**Other update implementations, local flags, kill persistence (2026-10-06, static).** (1) The 83 direct `mov [reg+0x67a4], x` sites are all in the other classes' code (0x44d9f7 ... 0x4e80a0): the 15 base-family vtables contain none (only compares), so for them the setAction thunk plus the skipped think already leave the owner's record as the only writer; the update restore is only a fallback for the rest. `tools/re0/think_scan.py` profiles every state-dispatch table entry of the 15 other update tables (setAction calls reached within two direct calls, direct state writes, position writes). Result: no class has a standalone decision handler like 0x41db20. Their state handlers reach setAction only from state 0 (a once-only setup) and, in 6 classes, state 5 (a one-call wrapper); state 2 is the death handler there (class 0x4392a0: setHP(-1) then a per-action table), and the direct writes (`state = 1`, `= 2`, mostly pairs in one function) sit in per-action begin/update/end functions that also animate and move, and in the state handlers of the class with update 0x4c21e0 (0x4bbd80 .. 0x4bcf40, its own state machine). Nothing to skip there without freezing the enemy; the restore stays for them. (2) The "local flags" word (singleton 0xdcbebc = sEventScript, vtable 0xcd7ac0, bit word at +0x58) is read and written only by the script opcodes (set 0x577f50, test 0x578010; no other code touches +0x58), is not part of sFlagManager and therefore not in the save. It orders scripts inside the machine that runs them; scripts run where the trigger fired and where the room is loaded, so each machine sets what its own scripts need. Not synced: nothing in the code shows a script on one machine waiting for a bit set by the peer's script, and a diff of a word that room loads reset would clobber a split-room peer. If a live room is ever seen stuck waiting, this is the place to look. (3) Killed enemies: an enemy class reads the flag `0x612 + [enemy+0x67c4]` (its spawn id) in sFlagManager at its first update (about 70 reader sites: 0x42be0d, 0x447bae, ...; if the bit is set and the id is non-zero it calls 0x528ee0, which takes it out of play), and the bit is set only by script opcodes (the flag setters 0x59c980, 0x59ba50 have callers only in the 0x57xxxx opcode handlers). The flags are inside the 0x47 words that flag_sync diffs, so a kill recorded by the owner's room script reaches the peer, and a room loaded later on either machine spawns the enemy already gone. No adapter change needed.

**Enemy sync v2 (2026-10-07; review and evidence in docs/re/RE0_ENEMY_SYNC_REVIEW.md).** Build 158 had two or more writers on every piece of an enemy's state (pose blend vs its own movement, four writers of the action record, snapshot HP vs replayed damage), which matched the live reports (jitter, stuck animations, one-screen hits). Rebuilt as: both machines run their enemies natively, the room's enemy owner decides hits and deaths and the other machine replays them exactly, the owner's decisions are cued once per change, positions get a gentle error correction, and a door barrier starts both rooms together. Details per part below.

**Hits replay the owner's outcome (2026-10-07, static).** The damage function is not deterministic: its first call, slot 76 (0x4cbdb0, shared by 37 classes), rolls the game's one random generator (xorshift128 0x684d90, state four words at 0xe2ccb0) through randRange 0x6600c0 (0..99) against a per-weapon chance from sEnemy 0x417270 and sets the critical flag +0x6a0c bit 0x1000000; class 0x438b00 rolls it via 0x660080 (random float). So every machine that ran the damage function rolled its own crit (guest log 2026-10-07 00:48:04 `slot 1 hp -68 -> -1`: the guest's replay dealt a critical's damage the host never dealt), and the head/body test (+0x6aa4 against the enemy's own height) depended on each machine's enemy pose. Now the owner records the enemy's HP and the random state, runs the damage function, and sends both with the hit point relative to the enemy (HitPayload 52 bytes). The receiver sets that HP, writes the random state, runs the same function around its own enemy and writes its own random state back. Whoever receives HIT_REQUEST applies it as owner and whoever receives HIT_APPLIED replays it (Build 158 dropped a request on a machine that did not believe it owned the room, and an applied hit on one that did, so every hit was lost on one side while the two disagreed at a room entry). Unverified live: the random generator is assumed to be used from the game thread only (all 154 call sites were not checked).

**One decision maker per enemy (2026-10-07).** Removed: the setAction refusal and the slot-41 record restore (both slot thunks, `slot_thunk`), the think skip, the 50 % per-tick pose blend toward a receive-time extrapolation, the 100 ms record re-apply, and (from the first version of this branch) the per-tick error correction, the setAction cue and the snapshot HP backstop. Each wrote state the enemy's own code also writes. The base family (15 vtables, update 0x420f70: uEnemy10..1a, 50, 51, 5a, 5b, 6a, 6b) decides only in its think step 0x41db20 (state 2; HP > 0: transition checks 0x422fe0, 0x423680, 0x423590, 0x423e90, then the crit-flag branch 0x4240c0 or the per-action think call from table 0xd7ac38; HP <= 0: death checks 0x424340, 0x424780, 0x424680, 0x425580, 0x425230, table 0xd7aca8), and every choice ends in setAction 0x4cc670, which only stores the four words (ret 0x10; shared by 32 vtables). So the owner's think runs as is and reports each record it chose (ENEMY_DECISION 0x011A, with the pose and the class; a decision for another class is dropped); on the follower the think runs with its own setAction calls dropped (only for the enemy thinking) and the owner's newest decision is stored at that point through the original setAction; the pose is set to the owner's decision pose first when more than 40 units off. Execution, movement, animation and reactions stay native. A follower that ran 15 ticks (500 ms) without an owner snapshot thinks for itself again (ticks, so a world held on both sides by a menu is not silence). The other 23 vtables decide inside their action code (think_scan.py found no decision step). 18 of them have an action executor (state-1 handler, vtable slot 66) that runs the class's per-action tables by action id (+0x67a8), like the base family's 0x41ea80: 0x4d6530 (uEnemy2a), 0x437800 (2b), 0x4423a0 (2c), 0x44d080 (2f), 0x451aa0 (30), 0x45a370 (31), 0x45da30 (34), 0x474250 (37, 38), 0x480320 (39), 0x486810 (3a, 3e), 0x490b10 (3c), 0x49c390 (40, 41, 42), 0x4a8940 (43), 0x4bad30 (46); 9 of them end in the commit 0x4cc6a0 (record copied to +0x67b4), the others tail-jump into their second table. The action code inside decides the next action by direct writes, so each executor is MinHooked (14 functions, one detour each): a record whose state or id changed across one call is an action boundary; the owner reports it, and at the follower's own boundary the owner's newest action of the same state replaces the local choice from its first step (third word 0: the probe saw it count steps within one action), through the class's own setAction (slot 63: 0x4cc670, or the wrappers 0x44d820, 0x4650f0, 0x480c20, 0x4bbc70 that also reset class sub-state; 0x4b17f0 is a jmp to 0x4cc670, four arguments, not one as an earlier table said), after the call, so the next frame starts it as a new action. uEnemy35, 45, 47 have no executor (slot 66 is the stub 0x9e2ac0), uEnemy44's update is that stub, and uEnemy3bRebecca's slot 63 0x48ac80 is not a setAction (it writes one byte through its argument): those 5 run natively. Damage no player dealt is applied by the owner only and its HP after travels as HIT_APPLIED with attacker 0xFF; every replay is checked against the owner's HP after. Class names from the DTI (vtable slot 4 returns the DTI, name at +4). Open, check live: whether a follower enemy waiting in state 2 for the owner's decision (one round trip) shows, and the drift of the 23 unsynced vtables.

**Door barrier (2026-10-07).** Load times differ by seconds between two PCs (2026-10-07 00:25: guest arrived 3 s before the host), and the first machine to finish a TEAM door ran the room's enemies alone until the other arrived. The room phase is DoorLoad from a door's start until the door finishes (log: `phase: Main -> DoorLoad` at the start, `DoorLoad -> Main` 8.6 s later, arrival 50 ms after), and the game does not tick in between. sDoorLoad's last phase (5, update 0x552300 at 0x5525ce) counts down its fade timer +0x2c, waits for the fade flag and for 0x551c70 (thiscall, no arguments: the door model's motion finished; only caller 0x552629), then finishes: 0x552e10 stops the door sound, 0x610ea0 on sRoomControl sets its +0x3c done flag (the room phase goes back to Main), and the door marks itself done (+0x43 = 1, +0x48 = -1). `room_gate` hooks 0x551c70: for a door both machines play (`door_sync::sharedDoor`: a local door sent while together in TEAM, or the peer's door run here), the first time it would pass the machine reports its door ready (ROOM_STATE door flags) and, while the peer's same door is not ready, answers false; the door stays on its last frame and the room does not start. Released when the peer's door is ready (or it is in the room), it turns away, the link drops, or after 5 s; "Waiting for partner" only past 2 s. A door one player takes alone, or a partner heading into the same room on its own, never waits. ROOM_STATE's door target and flags are reported while the room phase is DoorLoad (door_phase calls phase 5 idle, which is why it is not used here). Open, check live: what is on screen during a hold (the door's last frame, or black after its fade).

**Enemy creation seeded (2026-10-07, static).** Every enemy is built by sEnemy's create 0x410bb0 (thiscall (a, record index, b), ret 0xc; called from the room setup 0x4109d0 <- 0x61b2f0 and from script opcodes through 0x4109f0), from the spawn record this + 0x30 + index * 0x5c (index < 0x4e8; +0x4 kind, +0x10 spawn id, the kill flag is 0x612 + it), and ends in the class's spawn init, vtable slot 36 (`call [edx+0x90]` at 0x410e67; 18 implementations, 0x420ce0 for the base family). 0x420ce0 copies the spawn id to +0x67c4 and the home position, then: record +0x40 == 0xff (spawned before) takes HP from record +0x3c and the variant from record +0x50; otherwise HP comes from a table (0x4ca860) and the variant +0x6ab0 = rand % 3 (generator 0x684d90 on 0xe2ccb0), and record +0x40 is set to 0xff. +0x6ab0 is read during play (0x419450, 0x41a1a2, 0x41e86f, the update 0x420efb, 0x421b00 next to the target selector), so the two machines' first spawn of a zombie could behave differently from the start. `enemy_spawn` hooks the create: it runs with the random state set from the scene and the record's index, kind and spawn id (`spawn_seed.h`) and the game's own state is put back after it, so every class's spawn rolls match on both machines and nothing else's randomness changes. Always on in co-op (a room created before the partner joined matches too). The variant of a first spawn is then a fixed function of the record, not random per playthrough. Assumed: the create runs on the game thread (its callers are the room setup and the script opcodes). Every create also clears the new enemy's pool slot of what its previous enemy left (a waiting owner decision, the follow state): script spawns replayed by event sync can reuse a freed slot in the same room.

**Owner reactions (2026-10-07).** HIT_APPLIED also carries whether the owner's damage function changed the enemy's record (state or action id) and the record right after it (80 bytes). The follower applies that reaction after the outcome when its enemy does not show the same action: through the class's own setAction, from the action's first step (a replayed hit normally sets the same reaction itself; damage no player dealt is not replayed, so this is its flinch, fall or death on the follower). A reaction that a class sets only on a later frame from flags the damage function raised (0x4200c0 sets flag bits through 0x4cbd80 / 0x4cbd90) is not seen here; for the base family it reaches the follower as the owner's next think decision.

**QoL pass (2026-10-06, nothing run live: no second PC; pure rules unit tested, wire shapes checked with `fake_session.py`).**
- *Guest saves.* The save requests 0x6134c0 and 0x613500 are refused on a guest (`session_slot::guestSession` = the game plays the session copy, so it stays true after the host left) with the panel banner "Only the host can save the game.", `session_rule::saveRoute`. The ink ribbon is spent in cRoomPhaseSave::v7 0x60ba30 only when the save GUI ends with result 2 (saved), after the request, so a refusal costs no ribbon. A false return does not cancel the GUI: the save GUI 0x5d1d00 state 7 retries the request every frame, so a guest at a typewriter stays on the saving screen with the toast (the refusal log line is written once per episode). A cancel route (end the GUI with result 1) is not built; it needs a live probe of the GUI. Hook swap fixed 2026-10-21: the hooks had the save and load requests the wrong way round (0x6134c0/0x613500 were treated as loads and 0x613390 as the save), so the co-op slot redirect, this refusal and the state-mode save gate never ran on a real save and a guest's save was turned into the host's slot instead.
- *Host leaves.* `state_sync` sees the host's slot drop out of the peer list while the link is up: `host_exit::request` toasts "Host left, returning to main menu." and, at the next moment of plain gameplay with a controlled character (`game_state::playing()`), writes the title flag 0xdcc131 = 1 and asks room phase Exit (the way the game over leaves its world; the phase field is never written directly, that crashes the game), which opens the title at its main menu. The partner tag goes away with the host (no partner, nothing shown), saves stay refused. Nothing played on without the host would reach the host's save.
- *Host's game leaves play.* Quitting to the title or closing the game keeps the host's launcher in the session, so no peer drops. A guest in play watches the host's LOBBY_PRESENCE (`lobby::hostInGame`: fresh and In game) and, after `host_exit_rule::kHostAwayMs` (5 s) without it (title, closed, crashed: presence stale after 6 s), leaves the same way (`host_exit: the host's game left play`).
- *Partner tag.* Bottom left of the HUD (section "Co-op HUD"), shown with a peer. Condition is hp against the highest hp the partner reported (floor 150): above 50 percent Fine, above 25 Caution, else Danger; the game's own ECG thresholds were not read. The detail line says `Room 0x24` when the partner's loaded scene (its PLAYER_STATE `room`) is not this one (same room comes from ROOM_STATE) and `In menu` while it is in a menu. Whether the vanilla map (Q) shows the partner's room is unchecked.
- *Resync.* F9 in gameplay or `coop\resync_now.txt` (either machine) or a room desync that lasts 10 s (3 tries, 10 s apart): a guest re-requests the join snapshot and applies it like the first one (flags, both inventories, door travel, the host's character placed); a host sends RESYNC_REQUEST 0x0114 and the guest does the same. The guest's own state keeps being sent (it is live); the snapshot overwrites the guest's copy of its own inventory with the host's last view (up to 5 s old).
- *Game over.* The virtual Enter at the Dead phase no longer needs `auto_join=1`; the Continue cursor is the game's default there. Boot, title and load list still need it. The keyboard is muted while waiting for the host to be in game (handed back after 60 s, or at once when the host leaves).
- *Menu hold.* MENU_STATE carries the room phase and the screen kind (section "Live menus"). A peer's reading screen (Message, MessageImm) or cutscene (EventDemo, Movie) holds the world until it closes; the pause menu, the save screen and the Option phase (and the inventory and map with live menus off) for 20 s, after which the world runs and the partner tag shows "In menu". A live menu never holds. A held screen stays paused on the peer's own machine, so positions catch up through state correction when it closes.
- *Floor items for a late joiner.* Every machine journals its floor changes since its last accepted save or load (local puts and takes, received events, coalesced like `floor_pending`, cap 32 per room). The host sends the journal after the join snapshot as FLOOR_SNAPSHOT 0x0115 (cap 256 events); the guest queues them like peer events, puts marked onlyIfAbsent (skipped when an item of that id is within 50 units, so a save that already holds the item, or a resync, does not duplicate). A take with no matching put (an item the save holds) is kept and replayed. Limits: two identical items dropped within 50 units collapse into one on the joiner; the journal is lost when the host's game restarts.

**Level-placed items (static, decrypted image, 2026-10-06).** Map items are not a separate table: the room script's item placement loop (a script opcode, loop 0x581a50..0x581b7c, `put` call at 0x581b53) calls `sItemPut::put` for every map item each time the room loads, so they are sItemPut records like dropped items (the only other `put` caller is the player's drop, 0x5e4b84). Picking up goes through the item-get menu (0x5d85a3..0x5d85c6 removes the uItem through its weak pointer with `sItemPut::remove`), so FLOOR_TAKE already carries every pickup. The bug was on the put side: the script's puts were broadcast as FLOOR_PUT too, so a peer already in the room (or its pending queue, at its next load) got a second copy of every map item on top of its own script's. `floor_items_sync::putDetour` now skips announcing and journaling a put whose return address is 0x581b58 (`game::kScriptItemPutReturn`). A pickup whose partner is elsewhere waits as a pending take and removes the script's copy once the room has settled. The 0x4dfa00 "stage item table" of the earlier notes is a resource file name formatter (sprintf of model paths), not a table. Unverified live.

**Cutscenes (static, 2026-10-06), superseded by "Room script events" below.** Cutscenes are ops of room script threads (EventPhaseIn 0x56cbe0 and EventPhaseOut 0x56cd10 request phase 13 Event, PlayMovie 0x56e180 phase 12, Demo_Start 0x571890, Demo_EntryPos 0x5717f0, DmyDemoStart 0x56eb20, EventProc 0x56ace0, UpCut* for the close-up cuts; the addresses in the first version of this paragraph were each the next row's). Triggers are condition functions evaluated against the controlled player, so the machine whose character is not in the trigger zone never starts the cutscene; there is no single start function with an event id, the sync point is the thread start.

**Weapon sets before the equip step (2026-10-07, T-pose on every switch to a new weapon).** Build 156: a character T-posed on the peer's screen when its owner equipped a weapon. `setWeaponType` 0x50d870 attaches the player's motion lists for the weapon through 0x517360, which builds the weapon's names (0x531870: `arc\game\we\weXXh\weXXh`, `model\...`, `motion\we\...`) and fetches each with sResource getResource (0xe2d740 vtable +0x30 = 0x74d5e0) and flag **0x80 = look up only**: a resource that is not resident comes back null, the motion slot is set to null and the character stands in its bind pose; the weapon unit created next (factory 0x51fa20, kind 0x74) finds no set either. The menu close (state machine 0x5d8040, sSubMenu) loads first: for each character 0x5d7fa0 calls setEquipped (0x4db2f0) and, when the type differs from +0x6950, releases the old set with sGameChara (0xdcc0d0) 0x520850(0x74, old) (thiscall, ret 8); 0x5203d0(0x74, new, [player+0xff4]) (ret 0xc) requests the new one; state 0xc polls 0x520240 (true when every request in the map at sGameChara +0x5c0 and the pool at +0x5c8 is loaded; the poll itself, through 0x414e90, asks sResource for each missing file with flag 2, so polling drives the load) and only then does the close run the equip step 0x5d7720. b57b722 ran only the equip step, so the owner's screen was right (its menu loaded the arc) and the peer's copy T-posed unless that machine already had the weapon's arc. `equip_refresh` now runs the same release and request when a received slot change is first applicable (plain gameplay, character in the loaded room), polls 0x520240 every tick, then runs the equip step; a newer slot that arrives while loading releases our earlier request (`equip_rule::planLoad`, unit tested). Each equip is logged: `equip: <who> weapon <old> -> <new> (<local|remote>, synced) after <ms> ms, weapon set loaded|not needed`, and a change the game made itself (the local menu, a room load) as `(…, game), weapon sets loaded=<0|1>`.
