TEAM: the host's door gives `door_follow: serial N sent` on the host and `door_follow: serial N accepted` on the guest, each machine plays its own door, the guest's log shows `standing behind`, no carry and no `room_gate` line; both press a door in the same second: `rejected (busy)` and `partner rejected (busy)` on both, each in its own target, nobody swapped; a split save loads with each character in its room and the self-heal line stays absent in normal play.

## State mode: transport and diagnostics (phase 17 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

Launcher: a profile key `freshTypes` (re0: `0x0140` POSE_STATE, `0x0160` ENEMY_POSE, `0x0173` PROP_MOVE) puts those message types on their own lane. `partner_mode=input` sends none of them, so input mode is unchanged on the wire; DS2 and SR2 have no key and send as before. Adapter: SLOW_FRAME timers, state mode only.

- **Fresh lane.** `Session.OnAdapterFrame` hands an unreliable frame of a fresh type to `FreshQueue` instead of sending it; the end of the same `Session.Pump` flushes the queue on Steam channel 1 (`TransportChannel.Fresh`). The queue holds one frame per (peer, type): a newer one replaces the unsent one. Reliable frames, launcher-owned frames and every other type are sent at once on channel 0 as before. A burst reaches one pump when the adapter stalls and its TCP backlog arrives together, or when the launcher loop is late (10 ms sleep), so the effect is small on a smooth link and shows only after a hitch. `SteamTransport.Pump` receives channels 0 and 1; the peer budget, slot mapping and the adapter see no difference. `LocalTransport` has one lane and ignores the channel (the queue still coalesces).
- **Why a second channel.** The frames of channel 1 do not queue behind reliable traffic of channel 0 (FILE_CHUNK save transfers, MOTION_EVENT bursts) on the sender, so a late pose is not delayed by a reliable retransmit (RE4P 3.1, 7.4: its own channel per player state, newest wins). Whether Steam's per-channel handling helps on the real link is not measured (the plan marks this phase "measured first"; no game was run): the live gate below decides whether `freshTypes` stays in `games/re0.json`.
- **SLOW_FRAME** (`slow_frame_rule.h`, `slow_frame.cpp`; RE4P 3.5). A frame is the time between two ticks of the controlled player's `uPlayerBase::move` (0x513190, once per frame per player; the hook is the one `game_tick` has used since the first release). `game_tick` wraps each callback, move scope and post-move step in `slow_frame::Scope`; the microseconds go to a column chosen by the registered name (prefix table in `slow_frame_rule.h`: puppet, pose_, state_correction, post_move = puppet; enemy_ = enemies; weapon_fx = fx; event_, door_, room_, split_rooms, flag_ = events; net_, join_sync, resync = net; the rest, move scopes without a name and the time outside the callbacks = other, the game's own work is in the frame time but in no column). A frame of 50 ms or more is logged, at most once per 5 s. The scope is free until `slow_frame::enable` (from `state_mode::enableAll`), so input mode takes no timestamps. Loading screens and menus that stop the player's move end in one long interval and log one line; the numbers tell them apart (all columns near 0).
- **Trace lines.** Launcher log: `Fresh channel: 3 types`, `Fresh channel: N older frames replaced`. `adapter.log`: `SLOW_FRAME 83ms: net 1 puppet 4 enemies 2 events 0 fx 0 other 1`.
- **Tests.** `tools/launcher_tests` (`FreshQueueTests`: newest replaces, other type and other peer untouched, reliable never held, order, profile parsing), `slow_frame_test` (50 ms threshold, 5 s limit, sums reset, name mapping).
- **Live gate (two PCs, Wi-Fi, state mode).** Both launcher logs show `Fresh channel: 3 types`; F8 `delay` stays near 100 ms and the puppet shows no more `timeline reset` lines per minute than the phase 3 baseline; a hitch (alt-tab the game for a second) is followed by `Fresh channel: N older frames replaced` in the launcher log of the machine that hitched; during a game stall `adapter.log` has one `SLOW_FRAME` line naming the column that owns the time. DS2 session unaffected: its launcher log has no `Fresh channel` line.

## State mode: hit effects and part bursts (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

- **Why.** Blood, sparks and the hit sound of a hit are made by the enemy's own damage function (vtable slot 35; `uEnemy10::damage` 0x4200c0 calls `uEnemyBase::spawnHitEffectByAttackType` 0x4c8b80, thiscall (HitPoint*, zone, attack type) ret 0xc, and the class's playSe, which ends in `playSeAtPosition` 0x644740, (float* pos, bank, id, u8) ret 0x10 on the caller's ECX: it passes ECX on as `this` of the sound request 0x644a00 (which locks this + 0xf04), so it is effectively a thiscall on the sound manager. The first detour was stdcall and let the compiler reuse ECX: Build 197 crashed when a zombie played sound 0x132f right after a room load (EnterCriticalSection on 0x40 + 0xf04, crash-20261025-160350). The detour now passes ECX through and the replays use the sound manager of the game's last own call). STATE_ENEMIES.md said the shooter's hit path 0x52f520 spawns them; that is only the wall spark when nothing was hit. Only the enemy's simulator runs the damage function, so a shooter on the other machine saw no blood and heard nothing, and the partner's hits looked unconfirmed (live 2026-10-25).
- **HIT_FX 0x0162** (simulator to all, reliable, 196 bytes): {senderMs, seq, roomEpoch, scene, spawnRecord, effects, sounds, pad, 4 x {HitPoint, zone, attack type}, 4 x {pos[3], bank, id, flag, pad[3]}}. `enemy_damage_hook::runDamage` (every damage this machine applies: its own hits on rooms it runs, HIT_REQUEST from the peer, damage no player dealt) calls `hit_fx::beginHit(enemy)` / `endHit()`; the two MinHooks record the calls made in between (the effect only for that enemy), and a hit that made none sends nothing.
- **PART_BURST 0x016a** (simulator to all, reliable, 24 bytes): the base family's part loss 0x4256a0, thiscall (part, arg) ret 8: part 2 is the head of the critical kill (`uEnemy10::thinkCriticalKill` 0x4240c0 step 0), part 4 a limb (0x422cb0). It runs in the enemy's think, which only the simulator runs, so the other machine kept the head. The hook sends it when this machine simulates the enemy; a call it makes itself for a received burst is not sent back.
- **Receiver.** Both are queued on the sender's stream clock (`timed_queue`) and shown at the render delay, the instant the enemy's reaction shows; the enemy is found by spawn record in the loaded room under the table's epoch, and the same functions are called on the local copy.
- **ENEMY_SOUND 0x016b** (simulator to all, unreliable, 208 bytes: header + up to 8 {spawnRecord, flag, pos[3], bank, id}). An enemy's own code (its think, attacks, holds: the class `playSe`, e.g. `uEnemy10::playSe` 0x4213f0) never runs on a copy shown from a stream, so its groans and cries were silent there. `enemy_observer`'s unit move detour brackets the move of each enemy this machine simulates (`hit_fx::beginMove` / `endMove`); the `playSeAtPosition` calls whose return address lies in the enemy classes' code (0x415000..0x4e0000: all 77 such call sites; the 16 others are the player's) are collected and sent once a tick. Sounds of animation events run on the copy by themselves and are not collected.
- **PART_MASK 0x016c** (simulator to all, reliable, 24 bytes: header + the 2 words at +0x110). The model part mask (one bit per mesh part; `0x4c8730` swaps a part for its stump) of every enemy this machine simulates is sent on first sight (a part may already be gone when this machine takes the room over) and whenever it changes; the other machine writes it into its copy. This keeps a lost head or limb missing for every class; the zombies' burst blood comes with PART_BURST. The meaning of +0x110 is read from 0x4c8730 only, the word count (2) is an assumption.
- **ENEMY_EFFECT 0x016d** (simulator to all, reliable, 336 bytes: header + up to 8 {spawnRecord, index, attach, joint, flags, extra, pos[3], rot[3]}). The effects an enemy's own update makes (a death's blood, an attack's or a hold's effects) are `sEffect::create` 0x55a6d0 calls from the classes' code (113 call sites between 0x415000 and 0x4e0000), with the enemy as owner and its effect set (+0x6790). The one `sEffect::create` detour (`weapon_fx_capture`) hands every call to `hit_fx::effectCreated`, which keeps those made on the game thread during the move of an enemy simulated here, from enemy code, owned by that enemy, outside a damage function (HIT_FX has those) and outside the burst (PART_BURST has those). Attachment: none, the enemy, or Billy / Rebecca by vtable; anything else is not sent. The receiver calls `sEffect::create` with its own copy, its set and its own first flags word.
- **Only the zombie family loses parts (static, 2026-10-25).** The part mask toggle 0x4c8730 has five callers, all base-family code: the burst 0x4256a0 and 0x41ae10, 0x425da0, 0x426b20, 0x428ec0, which only swap the mesh pairs 0x14/0x15 and 0x1e/0x1f back and forth during an action (no burst, no spray). No other class loses a part, so there is no other burst to mirror; PART_MASK carries the swaps.
- **HIT_FX is needed** (HIT_REQUEST replays a hit through the enemy's own handler on the simulator only; the shooter's own copy never runs the damage function, so it saw no blood). RE1-Coop relays its blood the same way (hooks of its hit spray and grab/bite effect, "v32 blood to the joiner"). HIT_FX records only the calls for the enemy being damaged, the filter RE1-Coop applies.
- **Not covered.** The critical roll itself stays on the simulator; animation-event effects and sounds run on the copy by themselves.
- **Live check.** Either machine shoots an enemy the other runs: blood and the hit sound on both screens; F8 `hit fx sent/shown` count on the two machines. A headshot kill on a zombie: the head bursts on both, log `hit_fx: slot N record R lost part 2, sent` on the simulator and `lost part 2 (from its simulator)` on the other. A zombie's groan or a grab's cry is heard on the machine that does not run the room. If an enemy loses or shows a wrong mesh part on the other machine, PART_MASK's +0x110 assumption is wrong (turn it off by removing the PART_MASK route).

## State mode: contact and lease (phase 9 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

- **A grabber killed mid-hold (2026-10-25 live).** The holder ended the lease as soon as the enemy's HP hit 0 (`lease: token 1 ended (enemy dead)`) and the enemy went back under the observer's CharPause scope before its own dying update released the victim: Rebecca stayed bound until the game was closed. `contact_rule::finished` now ends a dead enemy's lease only once the victim is out of its paired state, or after `kDeadReleaseMs` (3 s, logged `enemy dead N ms and the victim still held`).
- **Enemies named by spawn record (2026-10-25).** ENEMY_LEASE (now 72 bytes), ENEMY_LEASE_END and PLAYER_HIT carry `spawnRecord`; the receiver replaces the sender's pool slot with its own through `enemy_observer::toLocalSlot` on the game thread, and a leased pose is accepted for the slot the stream bound by record (`acceptPose(entry, localSlot, ...)`). Live, the uEnemy2c grab (token 3) named the authority's slot 80; the holder resolved slot 80 locally, started nothing visible, the authority never took the holder's poses and the lease lapsed `silent, enemy not resumed`. A grant whose record has no enemy here is declined, an end or a hit for one is dropped and logged.

Closes the "known gap until phase 9" of "State mode: enemy observers": the non-authority player now takes enemy damage and grabs. With `partner_mode=input` none of it is enabled (`state_mode::enableAll` registers it) and the only edits to existing modules are state-mode branches or a rename: the four paths that asked `enemy_observer::observes` (decision, alignment, hit replay) ask `replicates`, still behind `state_mode::on()`.

- **One funnel, routed.** Every enemy damage and grab on a player ends in vtable slot 34, 0x512570 (`PlayerDamageEntry`; thiscall, `this` = victim, stack: attacker, hit position pointer, kind, level, attacker again; ret 0x14). It indexes the reaction table 0xd8d6f8 by kind (row = pair {begin, end} of 16-byte entries, 0x1d rows, rows 0x4, 0x5, 0x9, 0x11, 0x16, 0x17 empty) and level (entry = begin + level * 16, asserting and reading null at or past `end`; `contact_game::entryState` refuses those cases), then runs `changeState(2, entry state, 0)` on the victim, the paired snap 0x50cf00 and the reaction motion. Read in the decrypted image through REA (ghidra provider, the image wrapped as a PE): the entry Ev ev_e4c7367aeac10c11115df59c85f6e1c9c1d79db7324c9eb96281d84b5aded07c, the table bytes ev_9fac7692a632bf4a3bc75dd65cae3cbded11009de651df7b4a811d2faa436e6f (kind 0 = 0xccd118..0xccd188 with states 6, 9, 2, 1, 19, 9, 19). The base family's grab start 0x41a000 calls the contact (0x419950) and then `setAction(1, 0xe, 0, 0)` in the same function (decomp `funcs/00417d00.c`), so the record read after the enemy's update is the hold action.
- **Router** (`player_contact::damageEntryDetour`, a puppet as victim only; everything else passes as before). It acts when the attacker is an Observer-class pool enemy created through the spawn hook (`enemy_observer_capture::entryFor`) that this machine simulates (`enemy_observer::simulatorOf(slot).here`: the room authority, or the holder of a lease). Legacy and Native classes run on both machines and keep the refusal. The reaction state classifies the contact (`contact_rule::classify`): 0..5 flinch, knock-back, knock-down and 17..19 death are plain; 6..16 (held, bitten, carried, pulled, attached, thrown, and the unnamed 11) are paired. `contact_rule::route`: plain is a PLAYER_HIT; paired with no lease on the enemy is a lease when the enemy lives and the victim stands in the loaded room (otherwise nothing is sent: a victim put into a hold without a holder would wait for an enemy that never comes); a repeat on the leased victim is dropped; a second victim of a leased enemy gets a PLAYER_HIT only (class 30; probe G1). The call itself still answers 0.
- **PLAYER_HIT 0x0165** (36 bytes, reliable; `contact_net`). Sender: the enemy's simulator. Receiver: the victim's owner, checking room, the table's epoch, that the sender is the enemy's simulator as it sees it (`simulatorOf`) and that the victim is a character it owns, then ordering hits on a `timed_queue` against `puppet::clockFor(sender)` so the hit lands at the sender's time plus the render delay, when its observer copy visibly reaches the character. When due it checks the class and spawn of the local enemy and that the victim is alive and in the room and calls the original 0x512570 (victim, local enemy, point rebuilt around the local enemy, kind, level, local enemy) through `player_contact::applyToLocal` (SEH guarded). The victim's own think then runs every reaction, `takeDamage` or the death state natively; HP and death keep travelling by PLAYER_STATE / PLAYER_DIED.
- **Lease** (`enemy_lease`, wire `enemy_lease_wire.h`, table `enemy_lease_rule.h`). A paired attack is bound to the victim's own think (the group-2 hold state), the struggle 0x4c8ed0 / 0x4caae0 reads the pad only for the controlled character, the grab command +0x69cc is written by the enemy and read by the victim, and the paired snap needs the enemy next to the victim, so the enemy's simulation moves to the victim's machine for the attack. Nothing hooks 0x4c8ed0, 0x4caae0 or 0x517340; there is no GRAB_CMD or STRUGGLE.
  - *Grant* (authority, ENEMY_LEASE 0x0163, 68 bytes): the router only queues the contact; on the next game tick, with the enemy's update over, `grant` reads the enemy (`grantable`: alive and in an action, the record fits the wire), makes a token (`nextToken`, low 16 bits never 0), sends token, epoch, clock, room, slot, class, spawn id, victim, kind, level, HP, record, pose and hit point, and records an Away lease. The enemy is then shown from the holder's stream: `enemy_observer` gives the slot the scope (CharPause path), the holder's clock (`puppet::clockFor(holder)`) and accepts its poses and motions for that slot only from that holder. The authority stops streaming it (`enemy_lease_rule::streams`).
  - *Start* (holder): the grant is queued; it is checked (room, epoch, sender is the room's authority, victim owned here, no other lease on the slot, class and spawn of the local enemy, victim in the room) and started when the authority's clock plus the render delay comes: the local enemy is placed at the grant pose and HP, the original 0x512570 binds the victim (the paired snap runs against the real local enemy), the slot becomes Held (no scope, `replicates` true: the decision, alignment and hit-replay paths leave it alone) and the granted record starts at step 0 through the class's own `setAction` (`enemy_decision::startAction`). Anything that fails answers ENEMY_LEASE_END Declined; a grant not started within 1500 ms of arriving is declined the same way.
  - *Stream* (holder): the enemy's ENEMY_POSE / ENEMY_MOTION go out as for a simulator (`enemy_observer_capture`), but only for held slots, each pose flagged leased with the low 16 bits of the token in the former pad word (`PoseEntry.leaseToken`: non-zero exactly when the leased flag is set). The authority takes a pose only for an Away lease from that holder with that token, with an HP not above the lease's (and only ever lower) and a displacement of at most 1000 + 12000 * dt units from the last accepted pose (dt = local time since it, capped at 2.5 s).
  - *Hits on a leased enemy*: `enemy_observer::simulatorOf` answers the holder for an Away lease and this machine for a Held one, so a local hit goes to the holder as HIT_REQUEST (payload unchanged, `forwarded` as in phase 8) and the holder runs it as the owner; the zombie's damage function writes grab command 3 (released) on the holder, where the victim reads it.
  - *End* (holder, ENEMY_LEASE_END 0x0164, 48 bytes): `contact_rule::finished` each tick: the enemy died (EnemyDead), the victim died (VictimDied: HP 0 or a death state), or, 300 ms after the start, the record left the granted action and the victim left the group-2 hold states (Released); the enemy gone from its slot is Left. The message carries the reason, HP, record and pose. The authority removes the lease and resumes the enemy at once: from the holder's final state when the hold ran (`carriesState`), otherwise from the state it last showed (`enemy_observer::resume`: pose and HP put back, then the phase 8 hand-over rule: an action restarts at step 0 through the class's setAction, a think state or a corpse is left to the class). The holder's enemy becomes a shown enemy again on the next frame. The authority also ends a lease the holder never answers with a first pose within 1500 ms (Expired) or that went silent for 2500 ms (Silent), and tells the holder (ENEMY_LEASE_END from the authority drops a pending or started lease there). A role restart of the room (peer left, room change, authority change) drops every lease; the enemies this machine had given away carry on from the state they were last shown in when the room is the same.
- **Order of the ticks.** `contact_net` and `enemy_lease` register their game tick callbacks before `enemy_observer`'s, so a lease granted or started in a frame scopes or frees its enemy in the same frame (otherwise the authority's enemy would run one more native frame and could contact the puppet again; a repeat on a busy enemy is dropped anyway).
- **Not built / limits.** No STRUGGLE, no GRAB_CMD, no hook on 0x4c8ed0 / 0x4caae0 / 0x517340 (plan 1.4). A HIT_REQUEST for a leased enemy that arrives at the holder before it started the lease (about 150 to 250 ms after the grab) is forwarded once to the authority and dropped there ("forwarded twice"). A second victim on another machine gets a plain hit only. PLAYER_HIT omits the enemy's pose (the plan listed it): the receiver's observer copy is already shown at the sender's time, and the hit point is relative to the enemy.
- **Deviations from the plan's byte counts**: ENEMY_LEASE 68 (plan 72), ENEMY_LEASE_END 48 (44), PLAYER_HIT 36 (48, no enemy pose, a hit sequence number added for ordering and duplicates). The wire carries the room epoch in every message; the `PoseEntry` pad word became `leaseToken` (wire size unchanged).
- **Trace lines (`adapter.log`).** `contact: slot %u (%s) on %s kind %d level %d -> state %d (plain|paired)`; `contact: hit applied to %s from local slot %u`; `contact: hit for slot %u from slot %u dropped (not the simulator, room or victim)`; `lease: token %u slot %u to slot %d (%s)` (authority); `lease: slot %u not granted (hp %d, record %d,%d)`; `lease: token %u started here (%s held)` (holder); `lease: token %u declined (%s)`; `lease: token %u ended (%s) hp %d` (holder, with the reasons released, enemy dead, victim died, enemy gone, declined, expired, silent) and `lease: token %u ended (%s) hp %d` / `ended (%s), enemy not resumed` on the authority, `ended (%s by the authority)` on a holder; `lease: pose for slot %u refused (hp or displacement | wrong token | not the holder)`; `enemy_observer: %u enemies given away resumed`. F8: `contact hits sent/applied/dropped`, `leases granted/started/ended/refused poses`.
- **Live gate (two PCs, host authority; probes G1, G2, G3).** (1) A zombie grabs the guest's Billy: host log `contact: slot N (uEnemy10) on Billy kind 0 level 0 -> state 6 (paired)`, `lease: token T slot N to slot 1`; guest log `lease: token T started here (uEnemy10 held)`, the guest sees the paired grab and escapes by mashing (`ended (released)` on the guest, `ended (released) hp H` on the host, the zombie resumes on the host); the host sees the same hold on its zombie and the Billy puppet. (2) Grabbed again and the host shoots the zombie mid-hold: host `hit for slot N sent to slot 1`, guest `hit slot N applied` and `ended (enemy dead)`, release on both. (3) A bite costs the guest HP once (HP by PLAYER_STATE on both). (4) A bat or Cerberus plain hit: guest `contact: hit applied to Billy from local slot N`, host `-> state S (plain)`. (5) G2: the guest pulls the network cable for 2 s as a zombie grabs: host `ended (expired)` or `(silent)`, the zombie resumes without looping. (6) G1: a class 30 grabbing both: the second victim gets `hit applied` only. (7) G3: time from the host's `contact:` line to the guest's `started here` on a slow link, 10 grabs. `partner_mode=input`: none of these lines, F8 `state refused` 0.

## State mode: scripts and cutscenes (phase 11 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

With `partner_mode=state` the remote-owned character is a puppet whose body comes from its owner's stream, so a room script must not write it, and a cutscene is played natively on both machines with one skip for both. `partner_mode=input` keeps every op running on both machines exactly as shipped (`OpRole::Body` is a Run there; `event_body` and `demo_skip` are not installed).

- **Verified in the decrypted image through REA** (a PE wrapper with the same bytes at base 0x400000, Ghidra provider, analysis scratch only). `uEventDemoScheduler::update` 0x54ac30 (fastcall, this in ECX, state at +0x58, jump table of states 0..7) takes its skip in state 5 when the active pad's trigger word `[pad + [pad+0xc38]*0x2cc + 0x1a0]` has bit 8, or the pad's `+0xb70` mask hits outside the title screen, or the keyboard word `[0xe2e148]+0x160 & 0x8050000`, or the mouse byte `[0xe2e16c]+0x40 & 2` (0x54b496 .. 0x54b4f9), or the demo's own end time has come. The branch at 0x54b50b reads the fade duration `[[0xdcbe98] + 0x64]` (0x548700), calls the fade start 0x5aa410 (thiscall, float, ret 4) on the object at `[[0xdcbea4] + 0x20]` and stores state 6 at +0x58 (Evidence ev_c1e5035cd80f66db1f78270e4cb22c3a075644b56b7f17eed55f6d73f429466c). `demo_skip` runs exactly that branch for a skip the peer sent; no input is synthesised.
- **Body ops.** `event_rule::roleOf` returns `OpRole::Body` for SubMove 148, CharPoint 149, CharPointY 150, CharAddY 151, ChPosSet 152, PlayerClimb 153, PlayerClimb_Pos 154, PlayerEventStart 155, Player_AnimSet 167 / 168, Player_Reset 169 / 170, Player_Anim_SetSpeed 171, NeckRotation 173, PlayerAddChild 174, GameOver 192, CorrectPos 209 and PlayerAnimPause 231. Their handlers read the subject word as the first operand (the third for CharPoint) and compare it with 0x20000 (`sPlayer +0x2c`, getter 0x4ec780) and 0x30000 (`+0x3c`, getter 0x96cd30); ChPosSet and CharAddY treat any other word as a unit ordinal (0x40ff00 on the unit registry with the thread's script key, then 0x51fed0), SubMove always moves the partner (0x96cd30) and PlayerEventStart calls 0x4ec700, which starts the event mode of both characters and tolerates a 0 slot. Demo_EntryPos 226, CorrectPos2 276, AfterDemo 233 / 236 / 270 and the pause families (158 .. 164, 277) stay Run.
- **Decision.** `event_sync::runAsSubject` applies the perspective swap, then in state mode asks `event_body::plan`. A subject that is a puppet (`puppet::active`) skips the op: the pc moves past it (`stepOverBody`, the tail the handlers have) and the thread goes on; the owner of that character runs the op in its own thread. PlayerEventStart with one puppet runs with that slot of sPlayer reading 0 for the call (restored after). A skipped op is counted (F8 `event body ops skipped`) and logged. EVENT_START and EVENT_STEPS are unchanged; the follower still never runs ahead of the leader (`beforeOp` treats Body as Run).
- **SubMove for an owner without the thread.** SubMove brings the partner into the loaded room. If the partner's owner does not run the thread (it never got the start, or is in another room) a skip would leave it where it is, so the op runs on the puppet and `event_place::reportMoved` sends the result to the owner as CHARACTER_PLACE (the owner places its own character, as for any local event). The same mark / report brackets a climb of type 7 (a step up beside the partner), which places the partner on the first entry spot as a side effect that the owner's own run of the op, skipped there as Body, never makes.
- **Puppet through events.** `puppet::suspended` no longer includes the Event, EventDemo and Movie phases: the owner's character runs branch A under the scripts' pause, its samples keep going out, and the thread's Body ops skip the puppet, so the stream is its only writer. Doors, another room and a dormant record still hold it. A demo plays in its own room record, where the puppet is not in the loaded room.
- **Shared skip.** `demo_skip` hooks the scheduler's update. DEMO_SKIP_REQUEST 0x0153 (follower to leader) and DEMO_SKIP 0x0154 (leader to follower) are reliable, 6 bytes {u16 demo id, u16 thread serial, u8 flags (event_sync's serial flag), u8 reserved}. In state 5: a waiting skip for the running demo and thread from the right side takes the skip branch (and the leader then tells the follower); otherwise a press (the engine's own inputs, read by `skipInputDown`) on the follower sends the request and on the leader sends the skip. The pressing machine's own engine takes its skip in the same frame. A skip that arrives before the demo reaches state 5 waits 10 s (`demo_skip_rule::kPendingMs`) and then lapses, so it cannot hit a later demo. The tracker is set by the Demo_Start op (idempotent: the op runs again every frame while the demo plays) and ends when the scheduler leaves state 5 or the peer is gone.
- **Partner conditions are each machine's own** (corrected 2026-10-21, see "Partner-zone triggers" below; they were Shared here). Script waits (`wait_movie`, `wait_cancel`) need nothing: the follower takes the leader's branch.
- **Counters and lines.** F8 `event body ops skipped`, `demo skips sent/applied`. Log lines: `event_sync: op %s skipped as Body (%s is a puppet)`, `event_sync: op %s runs on the puppet of %s, its owner does not run the thread`, `event_place: a script op moved %s to scene 0x%02x, sent to its owner`, `puppet: %s driven through %s phase`, `demo_skip: demo %u of serial %u led|followed here`, `demo_skip: request|skip %u sent`, `demo_skip: skip %u applied`, `demo_skip: skip %u not applied, the demo's fade is not there`.
- **State mode switch.** `state_mode::enableAll` enables `demo_skip` (two frame router entries, policy Any, and the scheduler hook); the module count in `state_mode: on, %u modules enabled` is 12 with phase 10's, 14 with phase 9's contact net and enemy lease merged too.
- **Not checked statically.** Whether a skipped GameOver on a puppet matters for the Dead phase (the handler also sets `+0x36 |= 6` on the thread; probe D1); and that the scheduler misses one run of its per-frame step 0x54a990 on the frame a received skip is taken.
- **Live gate (two PCs).** The guest climbs the pl16 ladder: the host log shows `event_sync: op PlayerClimb_Pos skipped as Body (<the guest's character> is a puppet)`, the puppet climbs on the host from the stream and ends at the same spot. The tr0a lift with the guest at the panel. A cutscene skipped once by the follower and once by the leader: both end within one network delay, idle on both after, one `demo_skip: request|skip N sent` and one `skip N applied` line where expected. An event that moves the guest's character (tr0f reunion): `event_place: a script op moved ...`. A cutscene that stows and restores the weapon (probe W6): no bind pose on the puppet.

## State mode: props (phase 13 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

With `partner_mode=state` a pushed block (uObstacle) is shown on the other machine from the pusher's stream, and the box's zone trigger fires on the pusher. `partner_mode=input` keeps the shipped path (the remote pad pushes the box natively): nothing is registered or hooked, `classify` gets `obstaclesByPusher = false`, and the refusal log text for shared triggers is unchanged.

- **Verified in the decrypted image through REA** (ghidra provider, the image wrapped as a PE at base 0x400000; in this session plain `0x` addresses resolved and the `.text:0x` form did not). `uObstacle::move` 0x4e7830 begins `56 8B F1 E8 C8 EC 03 00` (ev_bdbb07f00fc31e3fdec81ff79711a5df70f2ebeb34ad14caa400e52e2512105b), takes `this` in ECX with no stack arguments, reads and writes the position at `this + 0x40 / 0x44 / 0x48` (fourth word +0x4c), then calls vtable slots 31 (0x5286c0, animation), 18 (0x76fa10) and 19 (0x76fa30) and the matrix update 0x4e72a0 (ev_ea94fb3ceef8b885f1612bd722546041505812a9eb907d94f01fa6d2826d9c19); its only reference is the uObstacle vtable word 0xcc81e4 = vtable 0xcc8140 slot 41 (ev_8ae9799b66761061c38ccfe3be854ba2de6d37019de28afefd06e6064c28e37e), so the hook sees every call. The pushed obstacle is `*(player + 0x6a00)` (getter 0x50e290, ev_e2753c2ac0f88b7c785356d41883764c0fcc5b8148f4b503677592669b0e5739). The push handler 0x501180 is in the think state table at 0xd8ac64 (ev_ae1f0defd24f02831791e6c70272db5c49bf1515e18f00281c5347495b517f40; table bytes ev_6b7f1df437cd1ee19747a5b6dec9779fba2a0f02feec6a63e128e1911914698b: pairs of {enter, update} words from 0xd8ac18, state 8 = {0x727270, 0x500000} climb, state 9 = {0x506820, 0x501180} push). The decomp of 0x501180 shows it writing the obstacle's +0x40 / +0x44 / +0x48 (and +0x4c = 0) from the player's position every frame of phases 2..4 and nothing else of the obstacle; it is entered by `changeState(0, 9, 0)` (0x4fb2a0) at 0x504770 and 0x4eb870 after the push-direction check. The obstacle table behind a room script's p2 0..4 (0xcd5398, ev_a2727f9fb0be00b90c2ad4e4a894ca3b37a6bebf99e905e18e65bb96f0298125) holds the rows {0x70, 6, 0}, {0x70, 7, 1}, {0x70, 7, 0}, {0x70, 5, 0}, {0x70, 0xc, 0} = {unit class, kind, n-th of that kind}; the trigger conditions 0x564200 / 0x564410 resolve them with the unit registry lookup 0x410070 (thiscall on [0xdcc010], stack scene, class, kind, n-th, ret 0x10; ev_4b82904b2f65d988ee510d36bb2aa883929503b4815f241583468dc62fc21eda) and the object lookup 0x51fed0 (ev_d1be5758d8fc9f11ae6818c0ad0727496a3f84784def91762c40bc8ebc98768b). The REA server was shared: its session subject was another builder's copy of the same image (same sha256), left open.
- **Identity.** A box is (scene id, unit): the scene id is the loaded room record's +8 and the unit is the table row, the same word the room script's trigger names, so the trigger and the stream agree without a translation. A pushed obstacle that is not one of the five rows of its room is not replicated (the push still works natively). The unit lookup is `scene::unitIndex` / `scene::unitObject`, shared with `event_body`.
- **Lease.** PROP_LEASE 0x0172 (12 bytes: u32 serial, u16 scene, u8 unit, u8 op Request / Grant / Deny / Release, u8 holder, 3 reserved; the plan sketched 8 bytes, the holder byte lets the guest tell a grant to itself from the host's own). The local character in think group 0 state 9 with a box in hand asks on its first frame: the host decides through `LeaseTable::request` at once and sends Grant to the guest when it holds the box itself; a guest sends Request and the host answers Grant or Deny (naming the real holder). The first asker holds the box, the holder asking again keeps its serial, a lease with no sign of life for 4 s lapses (a holder sends a keep-alive each second). The push itself is never held: the engine moves the box natively on the pusher's machine; a denied pusher (both pushed within one round trip) sends nothing, and on that machine the holder's stream wins at the box's update. Nobody linked means nobody to arbitrate and the push is granted.
- **Move.** PROP_MOVE 0x0173 (24 bytes: sender clock ms, scene, kind 0x70, unit, pos, yaw), unreliable at 10 Hz while the box moved at least half a unit (or turned 0.01 rad), a keep-alive per second otherwise, plus one reliable move with the exact final pose when the push ends. Receivers take a move only from the lease's holder and only when it is newer (wrap-safe), for the loaded room, with the peer's `StreamClock` ready. The yaw is `2 * atan2(q.y, q.w)` of the box's quaternion at +0x50 (x y z w); it is written back only when the two yaws differ, so it is inert unless a box's rotation differs between machines (convention unverified: pushing only translates the box).
- **Showing it.** `prop_rule::Track` keeps the holder's poses behind the shared peer clock and shows them the clock's render delay late, interpolated, nothing extrapolated. The pose is written in a MinHook on 0x4e7830 right before the original runs (position, fourth word 0, rotation only when the yaw differs), so the box's own update (animation slot, collision shape slots 18 / 19, matrices) sees it; never after. The targets (objects of boxes with a stream) are found once per game tick.
- **Commit.** At the end of a push the host commits: its own push at once, a guest's when its Release arrives (the pose is the last accepted move, which the reliable final one is). PROP_COMMIT 0x0174 (28 bytes: serial, scene, kind, unit, pos, yaw, committer, 3 reserved; HostOnly). A lapsed remote lease with a pose is committed the same way. Every machine drops the lease and notes the commit in its journal; the machine that did not push adds the committed pose as the last sample of the track, shown when its time comes and then retired. A commit for a room that is not the loaded one is only journaled: a room that loads fresh starts from the flags' layout (`sObstacle` 0x4e4dc0, called from every player move and from the reset 0x512080, applies a layout only when the flag-derived index changes: rooms 0x2a flag 0x87, 0x2d flag 0x8d, 0x8e 0x107 / 0xfa, 0x86 0x11a / 0xf5, 0x98 / 0xa0 0xf0, 0x08 0x52), so a load of a scene clears that scene's journal and the streams. The persistent result stays the story flags'.
- **Zone trigger.** Conditions 4 / 5 with p2 0..4 were Shared (fired by the room authority). In state mode `event_rule::classify(type, p2, true)` gives them `TriggerKind::Obstacle`, and `mayFire` lets the box's owner fire: `prop_sync::triggerOwner` is the lease holder, else the box's last committer; with neither (a box nobody pushed) the room authority fires as before, and a player alone in the room fires everything. The other machine's scan still sees the box in the zone and is refused at the trigger start (`event_sync: scene 0x%02x trigger %u (type 0x%02x) is the box pusher's to fire, not fired here`); the thread then runs on both machines through the normal EVENT_START / EVENT_STEPS.
- **Journal for joiners.** `prop_sync::commits()` returns the newest commit per box (bounded at 32); JOIN_EXTRAS (phase 16) sends it.
- **Counters and lines.** F8 `prop moves sent/applied/refused`, `prop commits`. Log lines: `prop: scene 0x%02x unit %u lease requested|granted|denied|released|expired|timed out slot %d`, `prop: unit %u committed (%.0f, %.0f, %.0f) serial %u`, `prop: refused ...` for malformed or misdirected messages.
- **State mode switch.** `state_mode::enableAll` enables `prop_sync` (three router entries, the obstacle hook, the tick); the module count in `state_mode: on, %u modules enabled` is 15 with phase 9's modules merged, 16 with phase 15's boss hand-over.
- **Not checked statically.** That the box's collision follows a position written before 0x4e7830 (the slots it calls are the same collision and matrix updates the player's paused move uses; the push handler's own `nCollision::cObjectBase` call on the room's obstacle data, `record + 0x9900 != 0`, is not repeated on the receiver); whether an unsolved pushed box keeps its place across a room reload in the vanilla game (the commit is only applied to the loaded room); whether the box's rotation is ever touched by a push. Probe O1.
- **Live gate (two PCs).** Room 0x2d: Rebecca (either machine) pushes the box to its spot. Pusher log: `prop: scene 0x2d unit N lease granted slot S` (host) or `requested` then `granted` (guest), then `prop: unit N committed (...)`. The other machine: the box moves about 100 ms behind, F8 `prop moves ... applied` rises, `prop: unit N committed` appears once and the box rests where the pusher's does; `event_sync: fired scene 0x2d trigger ... (type 0x.., obstacle)` appears on the pusher only and `event_sync: ... is the box pusher's to fire` on the other; flag 0x8d reaches the peer (`flag_seq` lines) and both machines show the same layout after leaving and re-entering the room. Input mode: none of these lines, F8 prop counters stay 0.

## State mode: boss hand-over (phase 15 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

A boss fight is a few enemies that must change simulator together. Until now an authority change (the room's simulator left, dropped or died) went through the generic `enemy_observer` hand-over, enemy by enemy from the last streamed record. This phase adds one transaction for the boss members, BOSS_HANDOVER 0x0169 (`boss_wire.h`, `boss_handover_rule.h`, `boss_handover.cpp`), enabled by `state_mode::enableAll` last; with `partner_mode=input` nothing is registered and no message exists. No existing module changed except additive lines (game.h `kBossVtables`, debug counters, `state_mode.cpp`, CMake).

- **Members.** The boss classes are the Observer-policy bosses, `game::kBossVtables`: uEnemy31 (Proto-Tyrant, vtable 0xcbfe58), 37 and 38 (Stinger, 0xcc08c0 / 0xcc5ae8), 46 (Queen Leech, 0xcc3248). Marcus (43) and the second Queen Leech form (47) stay Legacy until phase 18, so `enemy_observer::snapshot` gives nothing for them and they are not members. REA, the decrypted image wrapped as a PE (ghidra provider): all four vtables have the unit move at slot 8 = 0x526b30, the motion update at slot 31 = 0x5286c0, their own update at slot 41 (0x45b600 for 31, 0x4757a0 for 37 and 38, 0x4bb4e0 for 46) and setAction at slot 63 (0x4cc670 for 31, 37 and 38; 0x4bbc70 for 46): ev_0461701fd9fe9a5fc362bb6097291b6478a6419b3cee630a87fa0b780ea58a7c (31), ev_ba7b32224289be64f9b77fb34af8e86b4f95ef34bf6548e90e8e1cba6a2f7fa8 (37), ev_438057ffa7ead1529615b3349a63036cb5dac481f8b8a7d63b30d098bf7d8ee5 (38), ev_567e5d416caabda5fea8f2dc1d9e4edcf6a07fe4a358015802ede7719b51f9ed (46). The freeze is the CharPause path of the unit move 0x526b30 (bit 0 of sGameInfo +0x50 runs only slots 31, 18, 19 and the root cancel and skips slot 41, jump at 0x526d4e): ev_cafed1aec6b1ebcfc0f99658e518eaf6dfe7e201cb93331d9905758a602452f6. A frozen boss stops attacking and moving but keeps animating and keeps its hit shapes, so HP still falls if it is shot.
- **Wire** (reliable, `state_net::send`, `frame_router` policy Any; the handler only decodes and queues, the game thread orders). Header 16 bytes {token, room epoch, scene, phase, count, alive mask, reason, pad}; a prepare is followed by up to 8 members of 40 bytes {slot, class, spawn id, HP, pad, position, rotation (smallest three), record +0x67a4}. Token: the issuer's slot + 1 in the top byte over a 24-bit counter, never 0. Phases: Prepare (old to new), Ready (new to old), Commit (old to new), Outcome (new simulator to the machine that left), Cancel (either way, with a reason: timeout, room, sender, member, busy, left).
- **Old authority.** On the first tick of its own door (`game_state::doorActive`) in a room it runs, with the peer's ROOM_STATE saying it stands in the room, it collects the living boss members (`snapshot`). A member in a lease (`enemy_lease::busy`) or no member at all means nothing starts (tried once per door). Otherwise it freezes every member (`enemy_observer::freeze`, re-asserted every tick while the transaction is open), sends Prepare and waits for Ready (5 s, or the peer leaving the room, cancels: the members start again from the snapshot record at step 0 through `startAt`). Ready is answered with Commit. After the commit it keeps the members frozen until the room changes hands: when its own room changes it is Away (the units are gone) and keeps the token to hear the Outcome; if the room does not change hands within 15 s, or the peer leaves the room, it cancels as above.
- **New authority.** Prepare is accepted from the room's holder only, for the loaded scene and the table's epoch, when no transaction is open and every member is the enemy standing in its slot here (same class and spawn id; one already dead here is accepted). Otherwise it answers Cancel with the reason. Nothing is changed on this machine until the room changes hands: the members stay observed enemies of the old authority's stream (whose frozen members keep streaming, so their HP keeps following damage taken during the freeze).
- **The flip.** The tick runs after `enemy_observer`'s, so when the table gives this machine a room the peer ran, the observer's generic hand-over has already carried the enemies on. With an offered snapshot (prepared or committed) the transaction applies it: each living member is put back at the snapshot pose with HP = the lower of the snapshot's and what the stream left (`resumeHp`) and started at step 0 through `enemy_observer::resume` (place, then the class's own setAction through `enemy_decision::startAction`); a member that died meanwhile is left dead; a member that is missing or is another enemy makes the whole hand-over Frozen. Frozen keeps the members that fit held (`plan.resume`, `freeze` re-asserted every tick; the bad slot's enemy is a bystander and is not touched), counts, logs and asks `resync::request("boss member missing")`; it ends with the room, releases (`startAt`) when the link is lost, or after `kFrozenReleaseMs` (5 s) resumes the members that fit from the snapshot (`releaseFrozen`) and runs on as `commit alone` would (review fix, "State mode: review fixes"). Without an offer (the authority vanished: a disconnect, or a door whose prepare never got through) the machine commits alone from the boss members it noted while the peer ran the room (4 times a second, last notes kept while the peer leaves): it checks them the same way, logs, and the fight carries on from the observer's own hand-over; a missing member there is logged, never frozen, because no peer is left to resync with.
- **Outcome.** From then on the new simulator watches the members' alive mask each tick and sends Outcome (reliable) whenever it changes, until it is 0 or the room changes; the machine that left logs it. The plan said the outcome also commits "kill flags 0x612 + spawn id" through the flag sequencer. Not built, because the premise is wrong: 0x612 + n is the Act flag family (the script op SetActFlag 0x577ac0 calls `FlagSet(operand + 0x612, value)`: ev_12367cab44b1849d37d66bd87487327dbf4b535be5438881dc982431fa86935c), and the enemy classes read it as the "may act" gate at their start (the uEnemy31 helper 0x457460 tests `FlagGet(spawnId + 0x612)` and only then starts: ev_1cc89c2fc478ec5d6ff3dabe1d90d69392e190d32ff82ae6a3ed92d91cf4a999); uEnemy30's death handler even clears it. There is no verified kill marker at 0x612. What the simulator's native code writes when a boss dies already reaches the other machine through `flag_sync` and `flag_sequencer`, so the outcome carries only the mask.
- **Order of the ticks.** `enemy_boss_handover` (the name puts it in the SLOW_FRAME enemies column) is registered after `enemy_observer`'s; the room authority's tick, which claims a dropped peer's room, runs before both.
- **Not built / limits.** One transaction at a time. A door is the only voluntary trigger (a boss room whose doors stay locked is only covered by the disconnect path). A TEAM door that the partner follows still freezes the old authority's members for the length of the door; the room then empties and the new authority's copy is dropped with the scene (`cancelled (room)`). The snapshot is applied at the flip, not at the commit (the commit only promises it), so a prepare whose commit never came still applies. `state_mode::enableAll` counts this module in its "N modules enabled" line (16 with phase 13's `prop_sync`); a failed enable leaves the count short and logs `adapter: state mode unavailable`.
- **Trace lines (`adapter.log`).** `boss: token %u prepare|ready|commit|applied|commit alone|handed over|outcome|released|ended (members %u, alive %02x)`; `boss: token %u cancelled (timeout|room|sender|member|busy|left)`; `boss: token %u frozen (member slot %u, alive %02x)`; `boss: token %u member slot %u is gone`; `boss: not started, a member is in a lease`; `boss: ready|commit|cancel|outcome for token %u ignored`. F8: `boss hand-overs/cancelled/frozen`.
- **Live gate (two PCs, `partner_mode=state`, a boss room: Proto-Tyrant 31 or Queen Leech 46).** (1) Both in the room, the authority machine fighting: that machine's player disconnects (or its game is closed): the other log shows `room_authority: scene 0x.. owner slot N epoch E (peer left)`, `enemy_observer: authority -> here ...` and `boss: token T commit alone (members M, alive MASK)`; the boss carries on from the same HP and pose, no stand-still, and `boss: token T outcome` lines follow as members die. (2) Voluntary, if a boss room lets the player out: the authority machine starts a door in LEAVE_BEHIND: its log `boss: token T prepare (members M, alive MASK)` and `commit`, the other's `ready` and `commit`; both see the boss stand still (frozen) until the door finishes, then the other machine shows `boss: token T applied (members M, alive MASK)` and fights it from the same HP; the machine that left shows `handed over` and later `outcome`. (3) Force a refusal (leave the room on the other machine mid-prepare): `cancelled (left)` on the authority and its boss moves again at once. `partner_mode=input`: none of these lines, F8 `state refused` 0.

## State mode: session (phase 16 of the state-replication plan; built and unit tested, not run live)

Join, resync, the host's save and a death, in `partner_mode=state`. With `partner_mode=input` nothing here is registered or sent: `join_sync` runs its shipped path (`caughtUp` is "snapshot applied and placed"), the save and death code are untouched, and `state_net` would refuse the two new types.

- **Handshake.** After a guest applied the host's JOIN_SNAPSHOT and its JOIN_EXTRAS it sends SESSION_READY 0x0155 (reliable, 12 bytes: phase Ready / Echo, scene, load serial, snapshot serial) at once, again on a room or load change and every second until the echo. The host (`session_ready_rule::judge`) accepts when the guest's own newest POSE_STATE (`puppet::latestPose`: room, load serial, think group and state, arrival) is fresh (2000 ms, `peer_sight::kFreshMs`) and names the same scene and load serial, and echoes the three values with phase Echo; a ready that names a snapshot serial the host never issued is refused too. Refusals: `unknown snapshot`, `no pose`, `stale pose`, `other room`, `other load`. The guest is caught up (`join_sync::caughtUp`) when an echo repeats its last ready; `echoed` stays true across a resync (a new handshake starts, the status stream is not held back) and resets when the game is loaded again or the link drops. The state block's policy for 0x0155 is Any (one id for both directions); the handler checks the role (the host takes a ready only from its peer, the guest an echo only from the host).
- **Why `placed()`.** `pose_capture` used `caughtUp` to start the pose stream; with `caughtUp` now meaning "echo received" and the echo needing the guest's poses, that would never start. `join_sync::placed()` (host, or guest applied and standing where the host last saw it) is the pose stream's gate; `caughtUp` still gates PLAYER_STATE, inventories and weapon effects.
- **No host-character placement.** In state mode `join_sync::finish` no longer calls `scene::move` for the host's character (`placeHostCharacter` is the unchanged input-mode code): the puppet follows ROOM_MOVE and the pose room self-heal.
- **JOIN_EXTRAS 0x0156** (reliable, host to guest, HostOnly, sent from the same game tick as the snapshot and the floor journal; `join_extras::send`). Header 16 bytes {snapshot serial (+1 per send, never 0), serial of the host's last flag commit, claim / commit / item counts, the host's world epoch (never 0)}, then up to 175 room claims (`room_authority::claims`, the AuthMessage of each room the host owns), 32 push block commits (`prop_sync::commits`, the PROP_COMMIT layout) and 256 items the host's ledger knows were taken since the last save or load (`item_ledger::consumed`, 20 bytes each). The decoder needs the exact size, counts inside the limits, and every entry valid as the message it stands for. It replaces the host's `resendClaims` in `answer`; the guest still resends its own claims when it finishes.
- **Applying it.** The guest holds the arrived message until its snapshot is applied (`join_extras::onSnapshotApplied`, called from `join_sync::apply`; a newer JOIN_SNAPSHOT drops the held message), then, from the `session_ready` game tick: claims go through the room authority table like CLAIM messages sent by the host (`applyClaims`), commits through `prop_sync::adoptCommits` (journal, and a box of the loaded room ends at its committed pose), taken items into the guest's item ledger (`adoptConsumed`) and the flag serial into the guest's order (`flag_sequencer::adoptSerial`: the snapshot held every commit up to it, so an older commit is a duplicate, the next is in order and a skip is a gap and asks for a resync). The item ledger now keeps the journal on every machine (`notePut` / `noteTaken` were host only) so the adopted takes stay true; a guest's pickup of a journaled item ends at once with `refused (taken) from the journal` instead of asking the host (the host's answer would be the same).
- **Save gate.** A host save with a partner (`session_rule::saveRoute` CoopSlot, state mode) waits until the partner stands in plain gameplay: its fresh pose shows think group 0 states 0..6 (`room_move_rule::freeMovement`), its ROOM_STATE names no door and this machine follows no script thread the partner leads (`event_sync::followsAny`). The request is refused (returns false) and logs `session_slot: save waits for plain gameplay (reason)`; F8 `saves waited`. The refusal is bounded (review fix, "State mode: review fixes"): the save screen asks again every frame and has no other exit, so after `save_gate_rule::kMaxWaitMs` (3 s) of refusals of one request it goes through and logs `session_slot: saved without the partner settled (reason)`; a partner with no pose (title, load list, loading) counts as plain. The saving machine's own character is not asked: the typewriter is a room script op that holds it (REA: ScriptOp_save_point 0x57a9d0 requests room phase 6 itself, ev_29d7bdea9f5e56179d6789c2cd8bc28df142d1274d76f467ce366661df572de8), so a check of its think state would fail at every typewriter. A script thread the partner leads in a room the host is not in is not seen; its think state usually is.
- **Death (probe D1, answered statically).** `cPlayerThink::onDeath` 0x4fcea0 only sets the think to group 3 state 0 phase 0 and calls slot 17 (hit attributes 0x100); it does not ask for the Dead phase (ev_84811effae6f7f70484b9f09c9f4bbcdb845c3f089eb4209508deb7ca1cb1c6f). The request is made by the death states' update functions: the callers of the game over request 0x5adca0 are 0x4826f0, 0x5036f0, 0x503840, 0x503a90, 0x56f180 (the GameOver script op), 0x57fb60 and 0x65d3e0 (ev_3e645980472d4d06c68235dc1b5718e9babc6a95f18bd31bbbc61f245122bb87), and 0x5036f0 (assembly, ev_a664d45d91bb9360ed797c49f9b2b59c7c1c2b96b4a749b16267b8c26e56ae51) calls it in its phase 1 after a timer, with ECX = the singleton at 0xdce11c and (player, 0) pushed, guarded by the current and the requested room phase not being Dead (0xe). A puppet's think never updates (its brain is held), so the survivor's machine would replay onDeath and never reach Dead. `player_damage::requestGameOver` therefore calls 0x5adca0 itself (thiscall on [0xdce11c], (dead character, cause 0), ret 8: bytes `c2 08 00` at 0x5ae0aa, entry `83 ec 08 56 8b f1 8b 0d b4 be dc 00` = ECX kept in ESI, ev_a0e60bca2812f70dce991e53506439cfb37347b3cdf83ab898b98962bd3cb656 and ev_c68db4b307fcebc6d0e442b335aa6071edfe2af8ae72edea722da8778507d0c5), SEH-guarded, right after the replay, in state mode only. The request returns at once when the Dead phase is current or requested, so a second call is harmless. It is immediate (the owner is still in the death animation when the survivor's game over starts); waiting for the owner's death state to reach the request would need the group 3 state table and was not built. Continue then follows the existing path (host loads its slot, the guest's `auto_join` confirms at game over).
- **Not changed.** `auto_join.cpp`: probe S2 (the load request 0x6134c0 reached without the menu's state) needs a live run, so the virtual Enter stays. The guest save refusal stays as built (probe S4).
- **Trace lines (`adapter.log`).** `join_extras: sent serial N (claims C, prop commits P, items taken I, flag commit F)` (host) and `join_extras: applied serial N (...)` (guest); `session_ready: sent (scene 0x%02x serial %u)` (guest, once a second until the echo), `session_ready: echo received (scene 0x%02x serial %u)` (guest), `session_ready: echoed (scene 0x%02x serial %u)` and `session_ready: refused (reason) scene 0x%02x serial %u` (host); `session_slot: save waits for plain gameplay (reason)`; `player_damage: game over requested for the dead partner`; `item_ledger: take scene .. item .. refused (taken) from the journal`. F8: `session ready sent/echoed/refused`, `saves waited`. `state_mode: on, 18 modules enabled` (two more: `join_extras`, `session_ready`).
- **Live gate (two PCs, `partner_mode=state`).** (1) The guest joins mid-game: host log `join_sync: snapshot sent`, `join_extras: sent serial 1`, later `session_ready: echoed`; guest log `join_extras: applied serial 1`, `session_ready: sent`, `session_ready: echo received` (after `join_sync: caught up in scene`, which now means placed); Billy and Rebecca stand in the right rooms on both. F9 on the guest: a new `join_extras` / `session_ready` pair with serial 2, nothing else changes. (2) A death: the guest's character dies: host log `player_damage: remote character N died` and `player_damage: game over requested for the dead partner`, both reach the Dead phase (`phase: Main -> Dead`), Continue loads both. (3) The host saves while the guest is in a door: the save is refused with `session_slot: save waits for plain gameplay (partner is in a door)` and goes through after the guest arrives. Probes D1 (this answers it as "no": check that both reach Dead), S2 and S4 still need the run.

## State mode: review fixes (static review before the first live run, `coop-scratch\re0\STATE_REVIEW.md` and `INPUT_REGRESSION.md`; built and unit tested, not run live)

One bullet per fixed finding, with the live gate that shows it.

- **Save gate is bounded (review 1).** `save_gate_rule::Patience` (pure, unit tested) counts how long the save screen's current request has been refused: asks less than 500 ms apart are one request, and after 3 s of refusals the request goes through (`session_slot::partnerSettled`). The `NoPose` verdict is gone: no pose means the partner's character is not in play and nothing is changing. Live gate: the host uses a typewriter while the guest sits at the title or loads in, or leads a long script thread: the save screen leaves "saving" after about 3 s, the log shows `session_slot: save waits for plain gameplay (...)` once and then `session_slot: saved without the partner settled (...)` (not for a no-pose partner, which is plain), F8 `saves waited` stays below about 200.
- **A Frozen boss hand-over ends and holds only what fits (review 2).** A held boss runs only slots 31, 18, 19 and the root cancel, never the death states (slot 41), so a Frozen stage that waited for the room to change could not end the fight. `boss_handover` now keeps `g_held` (the members `planFlip` says fit: `plan.resume`) and freezes or restarts only those, so the enemy standing in a mismatched member's pool slot is no longer frozen or restarted. `Machine::lapse` gives Frozen a release after `kFrozenReleaseMs` (5 s, long enough for the requested resync): `releaseFrozen` plans again, resumes the fitting members through `enemy_observer::resume`, logs `boss: token N member slot S is gone` for a missing one and runs on as Running (log `boss: token N released`). Live gate: a boss room where one member is missing on the new authority (kill it on the old machine before the door): log `boss: token N frozen (member slot S, alive MM)`, F8 `boss frozen` +1, and 5 s later `boss: token N member slot S is gone` and `released`; the remaining boss moves again and can die; no unrelated enemy stays stuck.
- **Move scopes are exception-guarded (review 3).** `game_tick` ran callbacks and post-moves under `__try` but entered and left the move scopes bare, and the puppet scope calls the engine (`AllocThink` 0x4f81e0, `PlayerThinkCtor` 0x4f8010, `SetThink` 0x50f980, `setPartner` 0x4ece00) from inside the unit walk: a fault there was a game crash, where a callback only gets "Sync stopped". Each scope now runs under `runMoveScopeGuarded` with its registered `name`: a fault is reported (`crash_dump::reportCaught`, minidump of the first), toasted `Sync stopped: puppet` and the scope is disabled (enter and exit) until the next resync re-arms it. A faulted enter leaves nothing to undo: the puppet sets `g_scopePlayer` and the pause bit last, and `exitMove` only compares `g_scopePlayer`. In input mode the only scope is `input_redirect`, which has no engine call; its behaviour without a fault is unchanged. Live gate: none expected; a `game_tick: 'puppet'` fault line with `Sync stopped: puppet` is the signal, and `adapter.log` then shows the address of the engine call that faulted.
- **No enemy was ever streamed (live, 2026-10-22, scene 0x39).** The host killed both enemies of scene 0x39 (its room, `enemies of scene 0x39 run here`); on the guest one stayed alive and killed Billy (01:05:45). Neither log has a single `enemy_observer: sending` or `slot N observed from slot` line in any session, and every create logs `enemy_spawn: created enemy ... is not in the pool yet` (143 on the host, 42 on the guest in this session). sEnemy's create 0x410bb0 does not touch the pool: the enemy enters it in its class's start, vtable slot 32 (0x420ab0 for the base family, calling the pool insert 0x416c40), on a later frame. So `noteOrigin` (and the slot's decision reset) ran at the create, found no slot and recorded nothing, `originOf` was always empty, `enemy_observer_capture::entryFor` refused every enemy, and each machine ran its own enemies. Fix: a create waits in a pending list and a game tick binds it once it has a slot (`enemy_spawn` `settlePending`; dropped with a log line after 600 ticks); `createOnDemand` counts pending creates as created. Live check: the authority logs `enemy_observer: sending N enemies` each second in a room with enemies, the other machine `enemy_observer: slot N (...) observed from slot ...` and `slot N terminal` when the owner kills one.
- **Still no enemy streamed with that fix (live, 2026-10-22 01:33).** Every create, the zombies of scene 0x39 included, ended `never took a pool slot` after 600 ticks. The "37-slot pool" at sEnemy +0x4b0 is in fact seven per-class tables of 20 weak handles {vtable 0xcbd01c, next, prev, uEnemy*} back to back to +0xd70 (sEnemy ctor 0x416560 builds seven with 0x416510; the dtor walks them back from +0xd70, +0xc30, +0xaf0); each class's start (vtable slot 32) find-or-adds the enemy with its own insert, thiscall (enemy) ret 4, index 20 when full: base family 0x416c40 (+0x4b0, start 0x420ab0), uEnemy2a 0x416a00 (+0x5f0), 31 0x416b20 (+0x730), 2b 0x4168e0 (+0x870), 2c 0x416970 (+0x9b0), 34 0x416bb0 (+0xaf0), 30 0x416a90 (+0xc30) (`game::kEnemyTableInserts`). The handles are linked into the weak-pointer list 0xe2dba8, so a destroyed enemy's entry is cleared. The adapter's 37 slots cover only the base family's table and the first 17 entries of uEnemy2a's; the other five classes' enemies are never addressable. sGameChara's create 0x51fa40 runs the start inside the create only when the create flags (0xdcf2e4) have bit 0 clear; the run shows it never did at the create. `enemy_spawn` now hooks the seven inserts and binds a pending create at its insert; with trace=1 each insert logs `enemy_spawn: table 0x... insert of <enemy> -> index N (slot S[, not a pool slot here][, created here])` and a create that never took a slot logs `created unit ... never took a pool slot` (objects such as kind 0x70 do that normally, so it is trace only). Not proven: why the zombies of 0x39 reached no slot below 37. The next run (trace=1) settles it: no insert line for them means their start ran elsewhere or not at all; `not a pool slot here` means their class's table lies past the 37 slots, and `kEnemyPoolSlots` must cover all seven tables (140, with the wire and the per-slot arrays).
- **Settled live, 2026-10-25 (Build 185, trace=1).** The host logged `table 0x870 insert of ... -> index 0 (slot 60, not a pool slot here)` (uEnemy2b, slots 60..64) and `table 0x9b0 ... (slot 80/81, not a pool slot here)` (uEnemy2c), and no insert below slot 37 at all: every enemy of the run was outside the adapter's 37 slots, nothing streamed, each machine ran the insects on its own (one screen showed one alive, the other two dead). Fix: `kEnemyPoolSlots` = 7 x 20 = 140 (with a static_assert that the tables are back to back), the create pacer sized from it.
- **An undrawn puppet froze, and the enemies hit it (live, 2026-10-25, Build 186, scene 0x32).** After the lift event (sh0e trigger 10, demo 31: Rebecca rides the lift from y 600 to y 900) the puppets stopped: the once-a-second `puppet:` line ends at 00:50:47.928 on the host and 00:50:58.535 on the peer and never returns until the death, while the owners' poses keep arriving and moving. `puppet::suspended` asked `game_state::inCurrentRoom`, which required +0xc bit 0x4000; that bit is the draw bit (0x6534f0 sets it only when the hide mask +0xfc8 & ~1 is 0), and uCharaBase slot 43 (0x529910, called each frame for every character by 0x520b40) sets reason 3 when the character's floor differs from the controlled one's (0x522030 -> 0x51f750: in scene 0x32 floor = y > 800). A puppet on the other floor (or hidden for another presentation reason) was therefore never driven, so it never moved back into view: a self-lock. The peer's frozen Rebecca stood on the lower floor; the simulator's uEnemy2c (slot 80) attacked her there (peer `contact: slot 80 (uEnemy2c) on Rebecca` x8), PLAYER_HIT reached the real Rebecca upstairs (host `contact: hit applied to Rebecca from local slot 80` x7, death), and the observer showed the enemy at the simulator's position on the lower floor, hidden by the same floor reason: the invisible enemy. The frozen puppet also fed the authority's shared trigger 17 (condition 14, either character in zone 8; `SetActFlag 0x5c`, the act flag the second uEnemy2c's drop waits for, 0x43f350): its zone (+0x16e0, written by the light probe inside `puppet::drive`) never reached 8, so neither machine fired it (host: `trigger 17 (type 0x0e) is the room authority's to fire`; peer: no line). In the Build 185 run, where Rebecca's machine was the authority, it fired (`fired scene 0x32 trigger 17 (type 0x0e, shared)`, `sent act flag 0x66e set`). Fix: `game_state::inCurrentRoom` = loaded record and no reason 2 (`room_move_rule::inLoadedRoom`, `kHiddenRoomNotLoaded`); the draw bit is no longer read (`game::kPlayerInCurrentRoomFlag` removed, `kPlayerHiddenReasonsOffset` 0xfc8). Every caller meant room membership (partner switch, door carry, contact, lease, equip, join), so a partner on the other floor no longer counts as elsewhere either (the switch no longer zaps). Test: `room_move_rule_test` `testInLoadedRoom` (other floor, script and camera reasons stay in the room; reason 2 or another record do not; it fails with the draw rule). Live gate: in sh0e, after the lift event, both logs keep the once-a-second `puppet:` line; the peer's Rebecca stands upstairs; entering zone 8 logs `fired scene 0x32 trigger 17 (type 0x0e, shared)` on the authority and `following ... trigger 17` on the other; the uEnemy2c that attacks Rebecca is seen on her screen. Built and unit tested, not run live.
- **JOIN_EXTRAS refused on F9 (live, 2026-10-22).** The host sent `items taken 4` and the guest logged `join_extras: refused a malformed message from slot 0`: `session_wire::decode` refused an item take whose `scene` is at or above the scene id limit 0xaf, but an item take names its room the way the item ledger keys it, stage << 8 | room (`take scene 0x0109`, `0x010b` in the host's ledger lines), so any take from stage 1 on made the whole message malformed and the guest kept none of the claims, commits, takes or flag serial. The check is gone (TAKE_REQUEST never had it); `join_extras_test` round-trips room codes 0x0109 and 0x010b.
- **Stale pool bookkeeping (review 4 and 5).** (4) `enemy_spawn::alreadyCreated` trusted `g_created`, which is only overwritten when a create lands in the same slot and never cleared when the enemy leaves; record numbers are global, so in a re-entered room an enemy the simulator script-spawns could be refused creation on demand for good (never shown, its hits on the observer dropped as unbound). A note now counts only while `enemy_registry::enemyAt(slot)` is still its enemy. (5) `enemy_observer::scopedSlot` matched a unit against last tick's pool copy; a unit the walk deleted and a later unit that took its address could get enemy fields (setHP, +0x67a4, +0x16ec, +0x6a0c) written into it for one frame. It now also requires the live pool slot to hold the unit (two pointer reads per matched unit move). Live gate: (4) leave a room where the other machine script-spawns an enemy and come back: the enemy appears on the observer (`enemy_spawn:` log shows no `not created on demand` line for its record); (5) no fault in unit moves right after an enemy is removed.
- **uEnemy3bRebecca has no setAction (review 6, input mode too).** Vtable slot 63 of 0xcc1618 is 0x48ac80 (`mov eax,[esp+4]; mov byte [eax+4],0; ret 8`; the decomp names it `uPlayerDemoBase::v63`, shared with the Rebecca demo actors), not the `ret 0x10` setAction the other 37 classes have. `enemy_decision::setAction` passed the four record words anyway, so a reaction replay wrote through word 0 (a small state number) and faulted; the `enemy_net` callback guard then stopped all enemy hit replay until a resync. `enemy_policy::EnemyClass::hasSetAction` is false for that class and `setAction` returns early for it. Live gate: a hit on the Rebecca enemy actor (the room where uEnemy3bRebecca stands) no longer logs `Sync stopped: enemy_net`.
- **Recorders ignore off-thread engine calls (review 7).** `weapon_fx_capture` documents that `sEffect::create` is also called off the game thread, and `cAnimControl::set` could be too; both feed vectors and counters only the game thread may touch (`g_batch` flushed by the post-move, the observers' `g_pending` and `g_eventSeq`), so an off-thread call for the local player could race the flush and corrupt the heap. `game_tick::tick` now stores the thread id and `game_tick::onGameThread()` answers it: `weapon_fx_capture::add` and `pose_game`'s `cAnimControl::set` observer dispatch return early on any other thread. Nothing recorded is lost on the game thread. Live gate: weapon effects and motions still reach the partner as before (F8 `fx out` and `puppet events in/out` keep counting); whether the engine ever makes these calls off-thread stays unknown, a dropped call leaves no trace.
- **Only busy threads block a door follow and the save gate (review 8, and 1c).** `event_sync::leadsAny` / `followsAny` answered "a thread exists": every started trigger thread (local ones too) is led, so a looping or long-waiting room thread made every TEAM door answer Busy ("Partner stayed") and shut the save gate for as long as it ran. Now both ask `event_rule::Activity`: each slot notes the role of the op at its pc at every dispatch (state mode only; a follower too: its pc stays on the op the leader is still running), and a thread is busy while it is at a LeaderOnly (message, door, item screen, equip, typewriter), Body or Switch op, or was within `kBusyHoldMs` (2 s) of one. Polling threads (Run and Wait ops) never count. The hold covers the short waits between a script's busy ops; a cutscene is also covered by the room phase and the partner's think state in the two judges. Live gate: a room with a long-running trigger thread (watch `event_sync: followed serial ...` lines that never end): TEAM doors there commit (`door_follow: serial N accepted`) and the typewriter no longer logs `partner is in a room script`; a room cutscene with messages still does.
- **A partial state mode is reported, not silent (review 9).** `state_mode::enableAll` kept enabling after a failure and only logged `adapter: state mode unavailable`; with `pose_capture` or the puppet down the remote character has no driver (no PAD_FRAME in state mode) while room_move and the enemy observers still run. It now walks a table (`kModules`), names every module that did not come up in the log, and toasts `State mode incomplete: <names>` for 10 s. `weapon_fx_capture::enable` removes the hooks it already installed when a later one fails. Not done: falling back to `input` at run time. The shipped modules decide their paths when they are enabled (`room_gate::install` hooks nothing in state mode, `input_record` is skipped, the state hooks and tick callbacks stay registered), so a late switch would run neither mode; the whole mode choice is one value both machines must share (see "partner_mode is shared through the lobby"). Live gate: none expected; a hook that fails to install is the case, the toast and the `state_mode: module` lines name it.
- **partner_mode is shared through the lobby (input review 1).** `partner_mode` was read from each machine's own adapter.ini and negotiated nowhere, so a host and a guest on different modes linked silently: an input peer got no PAD_FRAME from a state peer (the remote character stood still), the state peer's FLAG_PROPOSE, DOOR_FOLLOW, ROOM_MOVE and the rest were dropped, and ModHash only stops different DLLs. Two fixes. (1) Launcher: `re0.json` has a host option `partner_mode` (input, state; default input) and `GameOptions.ForAdapter` makes a guest's adapter.ini take the host's value of every host option the lobby carries (so the guest's `partner_mode` and `character` match the host's; a value the profile does not offer is ignored), unit tested in `tools/launcher_tests` (`GameOptionsTests`). A hand-edited `partner_mode` in adapter.ini is now overwritten at every session start by the launcher's choice (dropdown in the Options section). (2) Adapter: `state_net_rule::peerRunsOtherMode` (a state-block type seen by an input-mode machine, a PAD_FRAME seen by a state-mode one); `state_sync` logs `state_sync: the peer runs partner_mode=X (type 0x.... from slot N), this machine runs Y` and toasts `Partner runs partner_mode=X, this machine Y` once, for runs that bypass the launcher (`--no-launch`, a hand-written ini). In input mode a state-block frame is now dropped before `frame_router::dispatch` (it only searched an empty list and the handlers behind it filter on the PLAYER_STATE type, so nothing changes; this is also input review 3). Live gate: the guest's launcher log shows `Set ... partner_mode=state ...` copied from the host's lobby option; with the host on state and the guest's ini edited to input after the launch, the guest log shows the mismatch line and toast once.
- **The F8 panel shows state rows only in state mode (input review 6).** The panel had grown from 58 to 89 rows in input mode, 31 of them always 0 or blank. `debug_lines::build` now adds the state modules' rows (`addStateLines`) only when `Snapshot::stateMode` is set; `state_mode::configure` records it with `debug_stats::setStateMode`. In state mode the rows keep their labels, moved into one block before `disabled` and `error`. The input-mode panel is the shipped one plus the rows earlier input phases added.
- **An empty fresh queue costs nothing (input review 5).** The RE0 profile always builds a `FreshQueue` (`freshTypes`), and `Session.FlushFresh` calls `Drain()` on every pump; `Drain` now returns at once when nothing is held instead of allocating an empty list. Input-mode frames still never match `Accepts` and go out on channel 0 as before.
- **A faulted create on demand puts the game's random state back (review 2.3 nit).** `createSeeded` writes the spawn's seed into the game's random state and restores it after the create; a fault inside the create skipped the restore, leaving the state at the seed. `enemy_spawn::createGuarded` now reads the state before its `__try` and writes it back in the `__except`.

## State mode: sync review fixes (world epoch; static review `coop-scratch\roadmap\RE0_SYNC_REVIEW.md`, 2026-10-21; built and unit tested, not run live)

The review checked the state-mode paths against the 12 bug classes of `tools/fuzz/BUG-CLASSES.md`. Three findings were bugs (a concrete sequence breaks sync with no unusual timing) and are fixed here; the rest are risks (breaking sync needs a race, a dropped reliable frame or a stall) and stay as listed below.

- **World epoch (class 12, review section 3).** The host counts the loads of a player save it makes (`world_epoch::onLocalLoad`, called from `session_slot`'s load request hook for slots 0..19 only, not the system data the game reads at boot): `worldEpoch`, 1 at start, never 0 (`world_epoch_rule::next`). JOIN_EXTRAS carries it (`ExtrasHeader.worldEpoch`, the former reserved field; the decoder refuses 0). A guest adopts it when it applies the extras (`join_extras::applyArrived`), forgets it (0) when it loads itself, and stamps FLAG_PROPOSE (`ProposeHeader.worldEpoch`), TAKE_REQUEST (`TakeRequest.worldEpoch`, new, 28 bytes), FLOOR_PUT and FLOOR_TAKE (`worldEpoch`, the former reserved field) with it; the host stamps its own FLOOR_PUT / FLOOR_TAKE with its epoch. `world_epoch::acceptsFromGuest` drops a message whose stamp is not the host's epoch (host only, state mode only; log `world_epoch: dropped a %s from before the last load`). A host that reloads while its guest keeps playing leaves the guest with an old epoch: its proposals, puts and takes are dropped until its next take is answered stale, which asks for a resync (the normal flow, where the guest loads too, takes a new snapshot at once). A guest does not judge the host's messages: its load clears what it queued (below) and the snapshot journal covers what the host did between the two loads. A request from before a load names a spot in a room, not a world, so the ledger's key check cannot tell it from the same request in the reloaded world (RE0.md "type 12": it matters).
- **F1: the guest's own save load is no flag change.** `flag_sync::onLocalLoad` (state mode, every player-slot load) takes the flag tracker's baseline anew: `g_hasKnown` is cleared and `flag_load_hold_rule::LoadHold` holds the tracker still, nothing diffed, proposed, committed or applied, from the load request until the room load serial advances (the loaded room's record is in), at most `kMaxHoldFrames` (1200 ticks) when it never does; `flag_sequencer::onLocalLoad` forgets the order, the unanswered proposals and the queued proposals and commits. A guest also diffs only while `join_sync::caughtUp()` (the SESSION_READY echo), so a flag change before it has joined the host's world is not proposed; the snapshot's `applySnapshot` is its baseline then and ends the hold. The host's own load diff is no longer a commit that guests on the game-over screen run through the story setter. The world epoch makes the host drop a guest proposal stamped before its last load. Log check: after a death and Continue the host's `adapter.log` shows no `flag_seq: commit N (0 set, K cleared) from slot <guest>` near the guest's `scene: load serial` line.
- **F2: floor events queued for a world that is gone do not survive a load.** `floor_items_sync::clearJournal` is split: `onSave` (journal and, in state mode, the item ledger, as before) and `onLoad` (also the per-room `g_pending` queue, the `g_incoming` events not applied yet and `g_delayed`, the takes waiting out their render delay). FLOOR_PUT and FLOOR_TAKE carry the world epoch (above), so an event in flight across the host's load is dropped on arrival too, before it reaches `item_ledger::noteTaken` or the queue.
- **F3: a take request racing the host's load does not land in the new world.** The host answers a TAKE_REQUEST with another world epoch with `Answer::Stale` (3; `TimedOut` moved to 4, it never travels) and does not touch the ledger; a stale FLOOR_TAKE is ignored (F2). The guest ends the pickup like any refusal (`pickup_guard: take refused (stale)`) and asks for a resync (`item_ledger::onResult`: a host that reloaded while this guest did not leaves it with an old epoch, and the snapshot gives it the new one). `item_ledger::resetTake` also runs on every local load, so a hold from before the load does not outlive it.
- **Unit tests.** `world_epoch_rule_test`, `flag_load_hold_rule_test`, and the updated `flag_sequencer_test` (proposal epoch), `item_wire_test` (request epoch, Stale on the wire, TimedOut off it), `item_ledger_test` (a stale answer ends the hold), `join_extras_test` (epoch round trip, epoch 0 refused), `floor_pending_test` (a load drops queued events, a new take does not cancel an old put).
