- **Live gate (two PCs).** Probe: kill both players, Continue on the host, then on the guest: the host logs no `flag_seq: commit ... from slot <guest>` and no `world_epoch: dropped` after the guest's snapshot, and the guest's flags equal the host's. Probe: the guest drops an item in a room the host is not in, the party dies and reloads, the host walks into that room: no second item and no missing one. Not checked statically: whether RE0's Continue goes through the load request hooked at `session_slot` and whether the room load serial advances on it (the hold falls back to its cap if it does not); the evidence is `session_slot:` and `scene: load serial` lines around a Continue.

### Open risks (review findings F4 to F14; not fixed)

Each needs a race, a dropped reliable frame or a stall, so none is a bug in the review's sense. The review's fix is named with each.

- **F4 (classes 2, 11): the join snapshot overwrites commits the guest already applied, and `adoptSerial` can move the tracker backwards.** The snapshot holds the flags as of host serial S (the serial arrives in JOIN_EXTRAS); while it waits for the guest's own character, `flag_sync` keeps draining commits S+1 and later, then `applySnapshot` overwrites them, and a later commit shows as a gap and asks for a resync. After a reset the first commit of any serial becomes the baseline, so a queued commit numbered S or lower is applied again. Fix: `flagSerial` in JOIN_SNAPSHOT itself, hold `g_commits` from the snapshot's arrival until it is applied, drop commits numbered S or lower on apply, `adopt` never moves `last_` backwards, no baseline after `reset` until `adopt`.
- **F5 (class 3): a host commit overrides the guest's in-flight proposal, then the guest's own commit flips the bit back (1, 0, 1).** The host orders proposals first in, first out, so a bit an unanswered own proposal touches is always settled by that proposal's own commit; a correction of it never lasts. Fix: `GuestOrder::receive` does not apply bits the remaining unanswered own proposals touch (the code already computes them as `overrode`); needs F6's proposal sequence and answer timeout so a lost proposal cannot mask a bit forever.
- **F6 (class 2): lost reliable frames are not detected for proposals, authority STOPs or floor events.** The launcher drops game frames over the per-peer budget (`Session.cs`, `PeerBudget.cs`), reliable ones included. FLAG_PROPOSE has no sequence (`flag_sync` advances `g_known` even when the send fails), a lost room-authority STOP leaves the peer believing the room is owned (`resendClaims` sends only CLAIMs), FLOOR_PUT and FLOOR_TAKE are repaired only by a resync. Fix: number proposals per guest and echo the number in the commit; a periodic authority-table digest (bucketed, REPORT adoption 4) with a resend of each record on a mismatch; floor events in the same digest.
- **F7 (class 4): a rejoined guest does not know the epochs of unowned rooms.** JOIN_EXTRAS carries only the host's owned claims, so a fresh guest claims an unowned room at epoch 1, the host refuses it as stale and only logs it, and the guest simulates and streams at an epoch the host's observers refuse. Fix: reply to a claim refused as `StaleEpoch` with the current record (an unowned room: a STOP at the record's epoch), and carry every record (owner or none, plus epoch) in JOIN_EXTRAS.
- **F8 (class 5): a lease grant can start after the authority expired it.** `startDueGrants` starts a grant that is both lapsed and due, and an END for a grant that is still queued is ignored (ends are drained before grants start); a holder stalled for `kGrantExpiryMs` (1.5 s) simulates while the authority has resumed the enemy. Fix: a lapsed grant always declines; `applyEnd` for an unknown token also erases a queued grant with that token.
- **F9 (classes 8, 6): create-on-demand can bring back a removed enemy.** Removal and death go out on the reliable ENEMY_POSE, ordinary poses unreliably in a separate send; `refusalOf` does not check the batch `seq`, `createWanted` runs on each raw arrival and `alreadyCreated` counts only an enemy that still stands, so an alive pose overtaken by the reliable Removed creates a frozen ghost; a create that lands in another pool slot is never driven (the second half is fixed by "State mode: enemy identity by spawn record"). Fix: a tombstone per (scene, epoch, slot, spawnId) set on Terminal or Removed; drop pose entries whose batch `seq` is older than the slot's last reliable entry; destroy or leave uncreated a create that lands in another slot.
- **F10 (classes 3, 12): prop lease answers carry no request id, and expiry commits carry no load serial.** A Grant for a push that already timed out is taken as the answer to the next push of the same box; PROP_COMMIT carries only the scene, so a machine that left and re-entered the room gets the old pose applied to the new load. Fix: echo a request id in Request, Grant and Deny; put the load serial in PROP_MOVE and PROP_COMMIT (the world epoch of this section covers a save load, not a room re-entry, which is the load serial's job).
- **F11 (class 12): HIT_REQUEST across a leave and return of the same room.** A hit request matches on room id, slot and vtable only, so one queued across a leave and return hits the new load's enemy in that slot. Fix: `spawnId` and the room epoch in `HitPayload` (the `reserved[2]` bytes or a version bump).
- **F12: JOIN_SNAPSHOT has no serial, so two in flight can drop the extras.** The second snapshot resets the extras state, and `g_applied` blocks it when the first was already applied, so its claims, consumed items and flag serial are never applied (a second request 3 s after the first plus a slow link). Fix: the serial in JOIN_SNAPSHOT, extras matched to it by serial, a newer snapshot always replaces an older one.
- **F13 (class 11): a resync loop through the flag gap.** Covered by F4's "adopt never moves backwards"; the callback order (`flag_sync`, `join_sync`, `session_ready`) makes it a race across frames only.
- **F14 (class 1): a replayed journal take removes a second item of the same id.** The match is the nearest item within 50 units (`floor_pending::kMatchDistance`). Fix: a tighter radius for replayed takes (the ledger's 10 units) or skip them when the guest's journal already has that take.
- **Not covered by the review:** live behaviour (every verdict is from reading the code); `event_sync`, `door_follow`, `demo_skip`, `weapon_fx`, the puppet and pose stream internals and `inventory_sync`.

## Menus: the Co-op entry on the title screen (static research 2026-10-25, decomp export and the decrypted image bytes with capstone; built and unit tested, not run live)

Replaces the 2026-10-21 entry in the Options top menu (`options_coop`, item id 6 in uGUIOptionTop, help line through a `rGUIMessage::getString` 0x887090 hook): the Options screen is vanilla again. The title's row labels are not text (below), so the text hook cannot label the new row; it stays installed with no entries for later menu strings.

**Menus: text hook.** `rGUIMessage::getString` 0x887090: thiscall, ECX = the message table object, one stack argument (the id), `ret 4`; `mov edx,[esp+4]; cmp [ecx+0x88],edx; ja ok; mov eax,0xcb4810 (""); ret 4; ok: mov eax,[ecx+0x98]; mov eax,[eax+edx*4]; ret 4`. Callers look the table up as `[sGameGUI + 0x158 + slot*4]` (sGameGUI = [0xe2daa4]). `gui_text` hooks it, maps ECX back to its slot and asks `gui_text_rule::find(slot, id)`; new ids sit above the table's own count (0x1000 and up). The setters cache the pointer they got, so a string must outlive the screen (ours are static).

**The title's main menu.** `uGUITitle` runs one state machine on `+0x27c` from `uGUITitle::v8` 0x5ebad0 (fastcall, `push esi; mov esi, ecx; call 0x5efb50`, then `cmp eax, 0x3a; ja 0x5ebd15` at 0x5ebb22, jump table 0x5ebd18, so a state above 0x3a pops and returns). State 0x1f `stateMainMenuOpen` 0x5eb780 highlights the cursor row `+0x288` over the five row pairs (`+0x304`, inst iC_menu_01..05 / var vC_menu_01..05), places them at the Y table 0xce29dc (5 rows: 469, 513, 557, 601, 645) or 0xce29f0 (4 rows), sets the label frames 0..3 (and 4) of each row's `aCRF_category` child animation, picks vS_SWITCH 1 (first opening after the save check), 3 (five rows) or 9 (four), and goes to 0x20. State 0x20 `stateMainMenuInput` 0x5ed690 reads the menu trigger 0x5ef420, the wheel and the mouse hit test over `4 + (+0x27a == 1)` rows (`cmp byte [esi+0x27a], 1` at 0x5ed721) and on confirm switches on the row: 0 load 0x23, 1 new game 0x21, 2 options 0x26, 3 extras 0x29, 4 0x2b (`mov dword [esi+0x27c], 0x2b` at 0x5ed8eb, the only writer of 0x2b); cancel goes to Press Start (6). State 0x2b opens the Steam overlay store and 0x2c waits for it. `+0x27a` is written only as 0 (ctor 0x5ea58b, `queueResources` 0x5edc55): the fifth row is the console "Online Store", hidden on PC, but the layout (`ui\50_title\title` rGUI, sequences MENU_5 / MES_MENU_5 / MENU_5_START / MENU_5_OK) and every state (0x23 picks the five-row OK animation, `+0x284` = 4 instead of 10) handle it.

**Row labels are atlas cells.** The title rGUI has no font and no message object: each row is a `cGUIObjTexture` "category" whose frame selects a rectangle of `ui\50_title\tex\title_00_<lang>_win` (queued by `queueResources` as the third request, `title_00_jpn_win` localized by `sGUI::localizePath`; `uGameGUI::getRequestedResource` 0x5ef550 walks `+0x258` nodes {+8 resource, +0xc next}). The atlas is 512 x 512 DXT5, one level, the same grid in all eight languages (chS / chT use the English one); frame 4 is the "Online Store" cell, whose ink lies inside (0, 220)..(256, 264) in every language (widest French 243, lowest Italian 263; the Options descenders end at 219, the next label starts at 268). rTexture `+0xd4` is its `nDraw::Texture` (stored at 0x89641a in rTexture::v10 0x895dc0; vtable 0xd23364): `+0x10` buffer type (0 for a texture from a file, which 0x81fa60 creates with usage 0 in D3DPOOL_MANAGED, then fills by LockRect), `+0x20` width | height << 16, `+0x25` levels, `+0x30` D3DFORMAT, `+0x34` IDirect3DTexture9.

**What `title_coop` and `title_label` do.**
1. Hook `uGUITitle::v8` (bytes checked at the prologue, the dispatch bound, the row-count read and the 0x2b write). Before the game's move, on a frame in state 0x1f: `+0x27a = 1` (five rows), and on the first such frame of each opening `title_label::publish`.
2. `publish` (game thread) walks the request list to the atlas, checks the nDraw::Texture (vtable, type 0, 512 x 512, one level, DXT5), reads the language from the request's localized path (`+0x2c0`, char[0x40], `...title_00_<lang>_win`), AddRefs the D3D texture and queues both. `applyPending` (render thread, the overlay's EndScene) checks the texture itself (one level, DXT5, managed, 512 x 512), locks level 0, decodes the source rows and writes 64 x 11 DXT5 blocks into the fifth row's cell. The label is cut from the atlas's own letters (`title_label_rule::kRecipes`): letters are the column runs of fill texels (white and opaque above 160; the black outlines of neighbours touch, the fills never do), each cut with every texel within 3 px of its fill; C is letter 0 of Credits (row 7; Italian: Carica partita, row 1), o letter 4 and p letter 1 of Options (row 3); Japanese uses CREDIT and OPTION for "CO-OP". The letters keep their rows (baseline) and are placed one pitch apart from the C's own position, the pitch being the source's O-to-p distance (16.5 px Latin, 17.5 Japanese); the hyphen is a white bar as wide as the o's fill plus one, the o's stroke plus one thick, centred between the C's top and the o's baseline (German's own hyphen, 12 x 4 at rows 20..23, sits there), with the same 3 px outline. A source row with another letter count, a language without a recipe (German has no capital C; an unknown path) gets "Co-op" from the system font instead (MS Gothic, em 36, width 14, 2 px outline, ink from (6, 6)) and a log line. Offline check against the eight shipped atlases: English, French, Spanish, Italian, Japanese and chS / chT build from their letters, German falls back. No game file changes; the cell is rewritten on every opening (idempotent), which also covers a reloaded atlas.
3. State 0x2b at the start of a move (the fifth row was chosen the frame before): `native_page::openNext("coop")` and `+0x27c = 0x26`, the state the Options row's choice sets (stateMainMenuInput writes 0x26 at 0x5ed8c9 with nothing in between): 0x26 fades out (FadeStart 0.5 s), 0x27 opens the Options screen once the fade is done (`uOptionManager::open` 0x5cc350, the title arc `arc\ui\option`), 0x28 waits for its closed flag (`takeClosedFlag` 0x5cb320) and reopens the main menu (0x1f) with the cursor on Co-op. The Options screen shows the native Co-op page instead of its list ("Menus: the native Co-op page"). Without the page (sites not matching, a fault) the choice gives the main menu back (0x1f), never the store.
4. A fault in the step is logged (`caught-*.dmp`), switches the entry off for the session, sets `+0x27a = 0` and gives the menu back (0x1f).
5. Needs the overlay (it applies the label): with `overlay=0` neither the entry nor the page is installed.
6. The same hook drives the session load from the title (`title_load::titleBefore` before the game's step, `titleAfter` after it, "Forced save load") and tells the lobby where this game is (`lobby::setPlace`: Title, Page, Loading).

**Open (check live, docs/TESTPLAN_RE0_STATE.md "T15").** The label's look next to the game's own lettering; the first menu after boot (vS_SWITCH 1, chosen before the five-row value) showing the five-row panel; the 0x1f reopen after the page (the menu sequence restarts); mouse hover and click on the fifth row.

## Menus: the native Co-op page (static research 2026-10-25, capstone on the decrypted image and the decomp export, `coop-scratch\re0\NATIVE_MENUS.md`; built and unit tested, not run live)

The Co-op page is the Options screen's own PC Setup page (`uGUIOptionPcSetup`, layout `ui\11_option\option_pc`: 8 text rows with text values, the arrows, the cursor, the frame, the key guide and the fades) showing a data-driven menu instead of the PC settings. Nothing is created or freed: the page instance, its resources and its teardown are the game's.

**The router.** `uOptionManager::v8` 0x5cb5a0 (fastcall; prologue `83 ec 40 53 8b d9 56 8d 73 30` and the dispatch `48 83 f8 15 0f 87` at 0x5cba3d checked) runs the screen on `+0x54`. State 1 waits for the fade, sets the draw flag (0x800 at `+0xc`) of the key guide `+0x150`, the black backdrop `+0x154` (title only, may be null) and the frame `+0x158`, and falls through into state 2 in the same call (`OptionKeyGuide(1)` 0x5cc1b0 and `uGUIOptionTop::open` 0x5c8bc0, then 3). The pages are built in the call that sees the arc loaded (`+0x48` 1 -> 0; that call returns right after), so state 1 always runs in a later call with every page present. `native_page`'s pre-hook, when the title asked for a menu: at state 1 with `+0x48 == 0` and the fade done it does state 1's draw flags itself, `OptionKeyGuide(0)` (what every page gets), opens the PC Setup page `+0x174` the way `uGUIOptionPcSetup::open` 0x5c6fe0 does without capturing the video settings (cursor `+0x26c`, `+0x268` u16 = 0, state `+0x264` = 1) and parks the router at 0x7f (above 0x16: the dispatch's default case only unlocks). The Options list never opens. While parked, a router state other than 0x7f means the game moved the screen (the page is dropped, nothing written); the page's state 0 means it closed: the menu's Closed event, then `+0x54 = 0x15`, the game's close-all (draw off, `FUN_004e0730` on every page, Release the arc `+0x50`, closed flag `+0x49 = 1`), then the work the page's actions queued (`native_menu::afterClose`). The PC Setup labels need no restoring: 0x15 kills the page, and the next opening of the Options screen builds new pages.

**The page.** `uGUIOptionPcSetup::move` 0x5c6390 runs `+0x264`: 1 `play(1)`, draw on, `refreshAllRows` 0x5c6fb0, fade in 0x5aa2c0(0.5) -> 2; 2 waits for the fade -> 3; 3 tail-jumps to `stateInput` 0x5c6540; 4 waits for the fade and applies the settings only when `+0x268` is 1 -> 5; 5 `play(0)`, draw off -> 0. Two hooks replace the parts that know the PC settings while our menu is on that page instance: `refreshAllRows` (prologue `56 57 8b f9 e8`) writes our rows, `stateInput` (prologue `55 8b ec 83 e4 f0 83 ec 24`) is our input. Fields (onLoaded 0x5c6cd0): `+0x254` loaded, `+0x260` manager, `+0x294` atlas header (hidden), `+0x298` the arrows' null (Y = row x [0xcbfd98] = 48), `+0x29c` / `+0x2a0` left / right arrow with touch polygons `+0x328` / `+0x32c`, `+0x2a4` help line (cGUIInstMessageGame: our header "Co-op"), `+0x2a8[8]` row instances, `+0x2c8[8]` value instances, `+0x2e8[8]` label objects, `+0x308[8]` value objects, `+0x330[8]` row touch polygons. Text: `cGUIObjMessageGame::setString` 0x5f2e40 and `cGUIInstMessageGame::setString` 0x5f21c0 (thiscall, ret 4) keep the pointer (+0x16c / +0x18c), reset the cached message ids to 0x7fffffff / -1 and lay the glyphs out; one static buffer per object, written only right before its own setString and only when the text changed; printable ASCII, 47 bytes at most. Rows: `cGUIInstance::setVisible` 0x880d30, highlight `cGUIInstAnimation::setSequenceId` 0x880c70 (5 cursor, 1 normal, on row and value as 0x5c7020 does), arrows moved with `cGUIInstNull::setPositionY` 0x880640 and shown only on a choice that may change; a pressed arrow flashes 5 and goes back to 1 once `uGUI` 0x5ef650 says the sequence ended.

**Input** (as 0x5c6540 reads it): `MenuTrigger` 0x5ef420, repeat 0x5ef340, `addKeyGuideMouseInput(manager, &trigger, &repeat)` 0x5cc570 (the key guide's buttons); cancel = trigger & `[sGamePad+0xb70]` or `sSubMenu+0x25 == 1`; decide = trigger & `[sGamePad+0xb6c]`; repeat 0x10010 up, 0x40040 down, 0x80080 left, 0x20020 right; a left click (0x5ef9f0) at the cursor (0x5ef170, float[4]) hit-tested (`uGUI::hitTestObjectAt(inst, object, pos, 1)` 0x5ef730, ret 0x10) on the arrows, then on the visible rows. One event per frame in that order (`native_menu_rule::decode`): Cancel, Confirm (decide, or a click on the cursor row), Select (up, down, a click on another row), Change (left, right, an arrow); the defaults button (0x8000) is ignored. Sounds (`playUISe` 0x5eecc0): 0x802 move and change, 0x800 confirm, 0x801 cancel and a refused event. Closing does what the vanilla decide / cancel does (0x5c6be9): the sound, `+0x268 = 0` (nothing to apply), FadeStart 0.5 s, state 4.

**Menus as data** (`native_menu`): a menu is a `native_menu_rule::MenuDef` (id, the action id of its menu events, up to 8 rows of {id, kind Info / Choice / Action, action id}) and a view source (each row's label, value, visible, locked, and the header); C++ handlers are registered under action ids (`native_menu::registerAction`). Events Opened, Select, Change, Confirm, Cancel, Closed; a handler answers Stay, Close or Reject. A Link row (its action names a menu) shows that menu on the same page, and a menu with a `parent` returns to it on cancel: both are the page's own, `native_menu_rule::navigation` (the cursor lands on the row that linked when coming back). The page edge-detects cancel (the sub menu's close request may stay up for frames). The rules (unit tested): Info rows are never under the cursor, hidden rows are skipped, a locked row (host-only on a guest) is shown and selectable but its change or confirm is refused before any handler runs, an action nobody registered is refused and logged once, a faulting handler is off for the session. `afterClose` work runs once the page closed (the title is in charge again).

**The Co-op menu** (`coop_menu`, rules `coop_menu_rule`): Character (the host's choice; a guest reads it), Outfit (this player's, stepping through the launcher's list for its character), Save (host: None and the used slots, labelled like the launcher's picker without the area, `save_index_rule::compactLabel`, so it fits between the arrows; a guest reads the host's pick), Options and Start (host, once a slot is picked; a guest's row reads Waiting for host, then Loading or Waiting for save). Options opens a second registered menu, `coop.options`, on the same page (a Link row; cancel or its Back row returns to the Co-op page with the cursor on Options): Pause in Menus (host changes it, a guest reads it) and Back. The players are not rows any more: the host's character is drawn on the left and the guest's on the right while the page is open (section "Co-op HUD"). Partner mode is not changeable here: it stays an adapter.ini / launcher option. Character, outfit and world pause go to the launcher as OPTION_SET 0x0061 and come back in LOBBY_STATE 0x0062 (docs/CONTRACT.md), so the launcher's screens, the lobby and the other player all see the same choice; `lobby` applies the launcher's values that act in a running game: world pause at once (`live_menus::configure`), the host's character (`character_owner::setHostCharacter`) and this player's outfits (`appearance::setChoice`, put on at the next game start) only while no character is controlled. The save pick is the session's order (`session_slot::setOrder`), also sent as OPTION_SET `load_slot` so the hosting screen shows it (the launcher works out `load_hash` itself).

**Lobby messages** (RE0 game types, every partner mode: below the state-mode block 0x0140..0x017f). LOBBY_PRESENCE 0x0118 (each game to all, reliable, on a change of place and every 2 s; 8 bytes {u8 place 0 title / 1 page / 2 loading / 3 in game, u8 flags 1 host pick / 2 guest checked / 4 guest matches, u8 pick slot or 0xff, u8 0, u32 pick hash}): the host names its picked slot with its block's FNV-1a, a guest compares its own block of that slot. A presence older than 6 s reads Away. SESSION_LOAD 0x0117 (host to all, reliable, every 2 s while the host plays after a Start; 12 bytes {i32 slot 0..19, u32 slot hash, u32 start serial, never 0}): slot and hash follow the host's current session slot (a save moves it to the co-op slot), the serial stays, so a guest takes each start once (`lobby::takeStart`).

**Open (check live, docs/TESTPLAN_RE0_STATE.md "T17" and "T18").** The Options page and the way back (Esc, B and the Back row; a second Esc closes the page), the page's look (header in the help line, hidden atlas title), the arrows only on the choice rows, mouse clicks on rows and arrows, the texts' clipping in the value box (the longest is a save label), the Options screen and PC Setup still vanilla after the page, a player name in the Japanese and Chinese fonts.

## Forced save load (adapter half; static research 2026-10-25, `coop-scratch\re0\FORCE_LOAD.md`; built and unit tested, not run live)

The host picks the save slot in the launcher; whichever slot a player confirms in the game's own Load Game list, the session loads that one. The launcher writes two host options into every adapter.ini of the session: `load_slot` (0..19; empty or absent = off) and `load_hash` (hex FNV-1a 32 of that slot's 0x1c850 data bytes in the decrypted save; empty = trusted unchecked). `config.cpp` parses them with `save_load_rule::parse`: anything but a slot 0..19 or up to 8 hex digits switches the order off with one `config: load_slot=... ignored` line. The order is read only when `coop=1` (the hook is installed with the other co-op hooks, so a solo game never sees it).

**The hook.** `session_slot`'s load request hook on `cSaveManager::requestLoad` 0x613390 (thiscall on the request object `[sSaveManager + 0x20]`, one stack argument, `ret 4`), the lowest call that takes the slot: `sSaveManager::requestLoad` 0x613c40 is `mov ecx,[ecx+0x20]; jmp 0x613390` and has two callers, the load list (`SaveFlow::updateLoad` 0x5d18f0, which is also what game over -> title -> Continue goes through) and the title's boot read of the system data (`stateSaveLoadStart` 0x5eb150, slot 0x15). The prologue is checked before hooking (`game::kLoadRequestBytes`, 12 bytes `83 79 04 00 75 2e 83 79 08 00 75 28`, read from the decrypted image); a mismatch leaves the hook off and logs it. The request only stores the slot (`+0x10`) and starts op 5; the read and `restoreSlotToGame` run later in `stepLoad`, so replacing the argument is all it takes. The load list is left alone: the player picks any row and the redirect decides (the rows' summaries come from the boot read of all 20 slots, not from this request).

**The rule** (`save_load_rule::pick`, pure): only player slots 0..19 are redirected (slot 0x15 passes). A guest that knows the host's slot loads it (unchanged, `session_slot: load of slot M turned into the host's slot K`). With an order and no session slot yet (the first load of the session, either machine) the target is `load_slot`; once the session plays a slot (`session_slot` remembers every load and save: the order's slot, or the co-op slot 19 after the host's first save) the host's loads also return to it, so a game over after a co-op save reloads that save and not the launcher's older pick. A request that already names the target passes untouched. A redirect logs `save_load: slot M requested, loading slot N (redirect)`.

**The hash check.** Only on the first load (target = `load_slot`), with `load_hash` set. Static finding: the request object keeps the decrypted file blocks the boot read filled, `GAMEDATA<n>` of slot n at `+0x23a7c8 + n * 0x1c850` (registered by `str_SYSTEMDATA` 0x613640; `restoreSlotToGame` 0x6127d0 copies a block into the working slot at `+200 + n * 0x1c850`, `captureGameToSlot` copies the working slot back into the block on a save). At the redirect the hook hashes that block (49 reads of 2384 bytes, SEH-safe, `session_slot.cpp` `slotBlockHash`) and `save_load_rule::hashAccepts` compares it with `load_hash`. A block that cannot be read or differs: no redirect, the player's own slot loads, log `save_load: slot N hash differs, not redirected`. Limit: this is the boot-time read; the load that follows reads the file again, so a file replaced between the boot and the confirm is not seen. Open (T16): that the block bytes equal the launcher's decoded entry; if they do not, step b of T16 logs the hash line on an unchanged save.

**One record of the session's slot.** `session_slot::order()` is the only answer to "which slot does this session play": the launcher's `load_slot` / `load_hash` at start, the host's Save row on the Co-op page, a guest following the host's start (`setOrder`). A pick also becomes the slot the session plays (later loads return to it), so a Start after a game over is not redirected to an earlier co-op save. The Co-op page preselects the launcher's pick; the game's own Load Game still redirects as above.

**The session load from the title** (`title_load`, rules `title_load_rule`; static, not run live). The host's Start on the Co-op page closes the page (afterClose), and on the frame the Options screen closed (title 0x28 -> 0x1f, the screen already black) the title is parked (0x7f) before the main menu shows. A guest takes the host's newest SESSION_LOAD: its Co-op page closes for it, or at the main menu (state 0x20, once `uGUI::isSequenceFinished(0)` 0x883bc0, the fade and an idle save manager allow) the title is parked and FadeStart fades out. Then, each frame of the title's move, logged as `title_load:`: the fade ends; `sSaveManager +0x28 = 0` (what stateOpenLoadGame 0x5edeb0 writes) and `sSaveManager::requestLoad(slot)` 0x613c40 (through session_slot's hook; a refused request is asked again, 10 s at most); wait for `isBusy` 0x613c30; `result` 0x613c10 (not 0: asked again after 2 s, 5 tries); the slot's block hash (every load reads the whole file again and its parse refills the blocks, stepLoad 0x612e30); a guest whose hash is not the host's waits 2 s and loads again (a newer staged copy may have come), 60 s at most, and never starts another world; then Continue's commit (stateLoadGameWait 0x5eca90): `sGameInfo::setWeskerMode` 0x59d530 from the working slot's byte +8, start mode 0xa891f0 (1, or 2 for a cleared save: area byte +4 == 0x0c; the working slot is `+0xc8 + n * 0x1c850`, restoreSlotToGame's copy of the block) and the area switch `[0xe2d8c0]->vtbl[0x28](0xdcbfe4)`. The host then announces the start (SESSION_LOAD). A failure gives the main menu back (0x1f) with a toast; the game moving the title away ends the start. The existing join (snapshot, extras, ready) places the guest. auto_join leaves a title that follows the host's starts alone (`session_rule::joinMode`, `titleFollowsHost`) and presses nothing while a start runs.

**Not built:** a guest already in game leaving its world when the host reloads (FORCE_LOAD.md section 3: 0xdcc131 + requestPhase Exit), the host's own Load Game announcing a start (hook 0x5eca90).

**The launcher's pick starts by itself (2026-10-25).** `title_load::setLauncherPick(config.loadOrder)` at start-up; the first time the host's title is at the main menu input (0x20, sequence and fade done, save manager idle, no Co-op page), `title_load_rule::launcherPick` decides once per game run: no pick or a guest: nothing (a guest follows the host's SESSION_LOAD as it follows a Start); link not up yet: wait; a `load_hash` that no longer matches the slot's bytes: skipped with the panel banner "The session's save did not load."; otherwise the host's order starts exactly like the page's Start. A later main menu (game over, quit to title) is the player's. Log `title_load: the launcher's pick, slot N, starts`.

## Partner-zone triggers: each machine's own (live report 2026-10-21; built and unit tested, not run live)

- **Bug.** sh0e (scene 0x32, the training facility lift): Rebecca stood on the lift, Billy worked the panel and got "no one is on the lift". The panel is trigger 10 (type 2, zone 5): `ResFlag 0x92` (lift used), `ResFlag 0x91` (power, set by trigger 11's item 0x56), then gosub 0x479 `ResFlag 0x93`: 0 shows `mes 4` (nobody aboard), 1 shows `mes 5` and, for Billy (`PlayerCheck`), the ride (`TraceOff`, `ChPosSet 0x30000` onto the lift, `obj_move`, `FlagSet 0x92`, `CharChange2`). 0x93 is written only by trigger 12 (type 4, zone 6, p2 0x30000: `FlagSet 0x93, 1`) and trigger 13 (type 5, same zone and p2: `FlagSet 0x93, 0`). Conditions 0x564200 / 0x564410 resolve p2 0x30000 through 0x96cd30 (sPlayer +0x3c, the partner), then test the zone (0x568550).
- **Cause.** `event_rule::classify` filed p2 0x30000 as Shared, so only the room authority (Rebecca's machine, the room's claimer) evaluated it, and there "the partner" is Billy: trigger 13 fired every frame (Billy off the lift), its FlagSet 0x93 0 ran on Billy's machine as a follower and reached it through flag_sync. Billy's panel read 0x93 = 0. Host log 23:45:24 to 23:46:09: `fired scene 0x32 trigger 13 (type 0x05, shared) as Rebecca at pc 0x5fb` every 5 s (serials about 60 a second), and each `following scene 0x32 trigger 10 ... led by Billy` ends `21 ops run, 1 leader-only skipped`, which is exactly the 0x93 = 0 path ending in `mes 4` (the 0x91 = 0 path runs 15, the 0x93 = 1 path with "no" 29). The one `trigger 12 ... shared` at 23:46:00.794 with `story flag 0x93 set` is Billy stepping into zone 6 on Rebecca's screen.
- **Fix.** Conditions 4 / 5 naming the partner are `TriggerKind::Own`: every machine fires them for its own player (its partner is the other player's character, fed by the stream) and the thread is not tracked or sent. The story flags such triggers write (`event_rule::kPresenceFlags`: sh0e 0x93, pl0d 0x10d) are left out of flag_sync's diff, so each machine keeps its own value. Every script was read: the other partner-zone triggers set local flags (pl01, pl0b, pl0c, pl13, bt02; sEventScript +0x58, never synced) or damage the partner (sh06 trigger 11, `PlayerDamage 0x30000`, which on a puppet does nothing, while the partner's own trigger 10 hurts it on its owner; as Shared the follower ran it on the owner a second time).
- **Camera triggers and PlayerEquip (static audit, same day).** Condition 35 (0x566d70) tests this machine's camera cut (scene record +0x10) and was Shared, so only the room authority's camera counted. It is Local now; a camera trigger whose thread reaches `FlagSet 0x37` (`SubAreaCheck; OnCameraBit 0x30000, mask; gosub FlagSet 0x37 0/1`, 31 triggers in bt02, ch00, ch04, ch05, dm0a, pl01, sh09, sh09b, sh0e, sh1a, tr0c, tr0e, tr11) is Own, found at run time by walking the started thread's code (`event_rule::writesPresenceFlag`: jumps, branches, loops and gosubs, cached per trigger), and 0x37 joins the presence flags. Door threads also clear 0x37; they stay Local and the follower's copy clears it once, the camera triggers set it again the next frame. As Shared, the TraceOff in the dm0a, sh09, sh09b and sh1a camera threads set the host's party mode to Leave behind every frame the partner stood in those cuts; as Own it runs natively and party_mode keeps the follow flag. The other camera triggers (ch00 8 / 10, pl01 17, pl11 12, pl13 5, sh00 27, sh00b 13, sh12 20 / 21, sh17 9, tr08 19) fire on the machine whose camera shows the cut and the peer follows. PlayerEquip (op 157, 0x56c3d0: subject word 0x20000 / 0x30000, then setEquipped(-1)) was LeaderOnly, so `PlayerEquip 0x30000` (tr08 trigger 11, pl18) unequipped the leader's puppet and the owner kept its weapon; it is a Body op now and runs on the target's owner (input mode: on both).
- **Lift ride left Rebecca unable to act (live, 2026-10-22, build with 72c839c).** On Rebecca's machine (the follower of Billy's trigger 10) the lift demo started about 7 s after the leader's, and the stall cap ended `Demo_Start` (op 227 at 0x4ee) 3 s after the leader finished it, then `CamChangeNS` (0x544) 34 ms after it began. Demo_Start 0x571890 sets sGameInfo +0x50 bits 2, 8 and 0x800 in its first call and clears the ones it set in its last; cut early, bit 8 (EventTrigPause's bit) stayed set, and cRoomPhaseMain (0x608ad0) runs the trigger scan only while that bit is clear. No trigger fired on Rebecca's machine afterwards (not even triggers 13 / 25, which had fired every frame): no door, no examine. Fix: cutscene ops (`event_rule::holdsPause`: Demo_Start, PlayMovie 0x56e180, DmyDemoStart 0x56eb20, which hold the same bits) are never cut, and the stall is counted from the later of the leader's finish and the op's start here. The resync key (F9) now also clears the cutscene pause bits on the machine that presses it, in plain gameplay with no busy script thread and none at a cutscene op (`event_sync: resync key: event pause bits 0x.. cleared`). The follower's 7 s late start (menu_mirror held its world 00:20:07 to 00:20:14 during the leader's demo) is not addressed here.
- **Live check.** sh0e with Rebecca on the lift and Billy at the panel: Billy gets the yes/no (`mes 5`), the ride plays on both, and both logs show `event_sync: fired scene 0x32 trigger 12 (type 0x04, own) for <character>'s partner, kept here` and no `flag_sync: sent story flag 0x93`. Rebecca at the panel still gets her refusal. pl0d's fence with the partner in zone 5.

## Flag setter checks (2026-10-12 and 2026-10-13)

- **Setter path verified against the fake peer** (`coop-scratch/re0_peer/fake_flag_session.py`, not in the repo): effect 0x413, act 0x612, display 0x721 and bgm 0x84b each cleared and set back on the receiving side with no crash, with one BGM refresh per BGM change. Sent direction: a door and a room trigger set story 0x261 and 0x187 locally; both were sent and the fake peer received 0x187. Not checked: a real partner in the same room seeing the redraw.
- **Frida probe** (`tools/re0/flag_setter_probe.py`): every setter call had `this == readPointer(0xdcc014)` and the refresh object matched `[0xdd20a0]`. The game also calls the BGM refresh 0x643490 every frame itself (caller `re0hd.exe+0x246b73`), so filter that caller when probing.
- **Family ranges** in `flag_family.h` run from each base to the next base, which saves bits set past the last named flag. An enemy-family bit there must stay on the raw mirror (routing it through the setter would double-spawn next to enemy sync).
- **Decompiling:** REA's Windows Ghidra provider accepts PE files only, so the raw memory image `tools/re0/image.bin` is rejected (`target_format_unsupported`); use Ghidra's `analyzeHeadless` with `tools/re0/ghidra/DecompileAt.java` (CODEMAP has the command line; about 4.5 minutes per run, so decompile several addresses in one pass).

## Live menus (static research and build, 2026-10-24; unit tested, not run live)

Requirement: with a peer connected, the inventory (status screen, files, its options page, combine, examine), the item pickup prompt and the map stop the world on neither machine; only the pause menu pauses. Reference design: RE1-Coop's LiveMenus (study `coop-scratch/re1coop/REPORT.md`), clean-room.

**What freezes the world (decomp export, then the decrypted image bytes with capstone; REA was held by another agent's session on a different binary).**
- `sUnit::setStopReason` **0x652ce0** (thiscall on `[0xe2d434]`, (reason < 14, u8 on), ret 8; prologue `56 8B F1 80 7E 1C 00`, checked before use): sets or clears bit `reason` of the mask at sUnit `+0x33d8d0`, ORs the per-reason group masks of the table at **0xdb5ef0** (14 pairs {stopped groups, shown groups}) into `+0x33d8d4` / `+0x33d8d8`, and for each of 32 groups (`+0x28`, stride 0x18) sets bit 1 when the group is stopped (bit 2 cleared when shown). `0x652bc0` (reason) ret 4 tests the mask. Slot 9 0x7279e0 skips a group whose `+0x28` bit 1 is set.
- Reasons in use: 0 room load (set 0x60d488; cleared 0x60ebf3, 0x625493, 0x6269ba), 1 and 2 cutscene units (EventDemo), 3 from sGameInfo `+0x50` bit 15 (sGamePause::onFrame 0x601610), 4 and 5 game over and UpCut, **6 the pause menu** (sGamePause open 0x600ff0, close 0x600f10 / 0x601130; `sGamePause::isPaused` 0x6014b0 tests it; mask 0x9fefff7e, nearly everything), **7 and 8 the screens**: set by SubScreen enter 0x60bf20, the Main phase's openers in 0x608ad0 (inventory, item pickup, map, Change), Map enter 0x60a530, Save 0x60b8c0, DoorLoad 0x6078c0, Change 0x6070a0, OmakeResult; cleared by SubScreen v7 at the menu close (0x60c5aa, after `sSubMenu::isClosed` 0x5d7e70), Map v7 / v8 (0x60a6df, 0x60a5f0), Save / DoorLoad v8 0x60ba00, Change v8 0x6070e0 (7) and Change v7 0x60760f (8). Message uses 7 and 12, MessageImm 9, Movie 10. Reason 7 stops groups 0xbe00, reason 8 stops 0x13800000.
- The same enter paths also pause the background movie (`0x6264f0(1)` on `[0xdcf344]`) and clear move flag 0x400 of the room's cut loader unit (`0x605910(0)` on sRoomControl `+0x108`); both are presentation and stay as they are (the menu covers the screen; the close reselects the camera cut for the character's position).
- Room scripts run only from the Main, Event and Message phase updates and sUpCut (the four callers of `EventScriptUpdate` 0x5835a0), never from SubScreen or Map, so the trigger scan, script threads and event_sync's follower starts stay parked while a live menu is open.
- The item pickup prompt is the SubScreen phase (`sSubMenu +0x28` pending item in Main v7; a map item, category 7, goes to the Map phase). Closing the inventory runs `beginRoomLoadForCurrentScene` (SubScreen v7 state 2, reason 0 for its duration): a short vanilla stop after every close.
- Game over is requested from the controlled character's think (dead states 0x5036f0, 0x503840, 0x503a90 -> `sGameOver::request` 0x5adca0 -> requestPhase(14)); with the world running that could switch the phase under an open menu.

**What the adapter does** (`live_menus.cpp`, `live_menu_rule.h`; adapter.ini `world_pause=never`, the default, effective in `partner_mode=state` only and only while a peer is present).
- `live_menu_rule::screenOf`: the pause menu (reason 6) is Holding; SubScreen and Map are Live; any other open submenu or a `room_phase::pausesWorld` phase (Message, MessageImm, Save, Option, EventDemo, Movie) is Holding; else None.
- Local side: every frame, from menu_mirror's sUnit::updateAll detour and before the units update, a Live screen whose character is unhurt gets reasons 7 and 8 cleared through the engine's own setter (log `live_menus: SubScreen open, the world keeps running`). The engine's close clears them again, so set and clear stay balanced. Meanwhile `input_redirect` gives the local character the blocked pad and the neutral stick (route Blocked), so menu keys do not move it; enemies still reach and hit it.
- Hurt in a menu, chosen instead of closing the menu: `HurtWatch` compares the local character's HP (`+0x1030`) each frame; a drop sets reasons 7 and 8 again (log `live_menus: hurt in the menu, the world waits until it closes`), so the menu pauses like the vanilla one until it closes and the death path cannot run under the open menu. Closing was rejected: the GUI's own close (`uGUISubMenu +0x2ac = 3`, `+0x290 = 6`, then its close sequence and `sGameGUI::closeMessage`) runs from inside each sub-state, and nested states (item pickup, examine model, files, combine) tear down their own objects first; forcing it from outside skips that teardown.
- Remote side: MENU_STATE's `open` byte is the Screen (0 none, 1 holding, 2 live; an older build's 1 reads as holding). `menu_hold_rule` holds only for a Holding peer (reading and cutscenes until closed, the rest 20 s counted from when holding began) and only in the same room (`split_rooms::apart`); the status line still shows "in menu" for a Live peer. So the pause menu and the save screen (typewriter) freeze both machines in the same room, like RE1-Coop's Esc and typewriter rule; the save gate rules are unchanged.
- Without a peer, in input mode or with `world_pause=menus`, the inventory, map and messages are Holding as before.

**Message windows (2026-10-25; decomp export, then the decrypted image bytes with capstone; REA held another agent's RE1 binary; unit tested, not run live).**
- What pauses them: the room phase. The script message ops (`mes` 0x5780b0, `mesSel` 0x578320, `mes_common` 0x578580, `mesSE` 0x5787b0, `mes_commonSE` 0x578b20, `mes_blend` 0x570800, `mes_dialog` 0x5757a0, and `save_point` 0x57a9d0's question) show the window (`sGameGUI::showMessage` 0x5d4fa0, the event pad 0x5853a0) and request phase Message (2) unless the phase is UpCut; they pop it when `sGameGUI::isMessageBusy` 0x5d4ec0 says done. cRoomPhaseMessage (vtable 0xce6da4) enter v6 0x60a8a0 sets stop reasons 7 and 12, exit v8 0x60a8e0 clears them (Main 0x608ad0 also sets 7 and 12 the frame a Message is pending); its update v7 0x60a910 runs `EventScriptUpdate`. MessageImm (3) is pushed by Main itself for the "cannot change" notice (`showCommonMessage(0x1c)` when flag 0x36 is set, at the V press) and sets reason 9 (v6 0x60a9f0, v8 0x60aa20). Reason 12 stops groups 0x10800000, reason 9 0x1880be80 (table 0xdb5ef0). No other pause: sGameInfo +0x50 is untouched by the message ops.
- Item dialog versus event dialogue: the phase manager is a stack (push 0x60a340 stores the covered phase at `+0x1c[depth]`, depth `+0x3c`, 8 entries; pop 0x60a330). A message pushed over Main is the player's own: examine text, a locked door, the "cannot change" notice, the typewriter's question (all from local triggers, type 2 and friends, whose follower skips the message). One pushed over Event (EventPhaseIn 0x56cbe0) or shown while sGameInfo +0x50 holds a scripted event's bits (`game::kScriptEventBits`: CharPause 0x1, EventTrigPause 0x8, Demo 0x800, ControlPause 0x2000 (0x574a00), EventPhase 0x4000; PlayerPause 0x2 is left out, examine and door scripts set it) is event dialogue and keeps Holding (and holds the peer until it closes, `menu_hold_rule::unlimited`). The item pickup prompt and item descriptions are SubScreen screens and were live already; files read in the world go through the script's Sub ops, which request SubScreen (0x5668f0 region; inferred from the decomp, not traced).
- What the adapter does: `live_menu_rule::screenOf(View, live)` returns Live for SubScreen, Map, and Message / MessageImm over Main without event bits; `screenStops(phase)` names the reasons to clear (7 + 8, 7 + 12, or 9) and the hurt stop sets the same set back. MENU_STATE says Live, so the peer never holds for a player's own message.
- Pause in Menus option: the launcher's host option Pause in Menus (`world_pause` in launcher/games/re0.json: `never`, the default, or `menus`) is written into adapter.ini through `GameOptions.ForAdapter`, so a guest runs the host's value; it replaced the `live_menus` key. `menus` gives the vanilla pause for every menu and message, and peers hold as before.
- Shared Yes/No questions: nothing to build. event_sync's follower never shows the question: every message and selection op (`OpRole::LeaderOnly`, `event_rule.h`) is skipped and the pc goes to the leader's `to`, so the branch after the choice is the leader's answer, and the follower waits at that op until the leader finishes it. RE1-Coop's 6 s local fallback exists because RE1 shows the window on both machines; here there is no follower window to answer.
- A hit closes the open menu: not done, the freeze stays. The inventory's close runs from inside its own sub-states (above); a message closes only when the GUI reports it idle (`isMessageBusy`), after which the op pops the phase and releases the event pad, so closing it from outside would skip the script op's own exit or leave the choice unanswered.
- Esc pause and the typewriter: confirmed same-room only. Both are Holding (reason 6; the Save phase), and menu_mirror's detour holds only while `!split_rooms::apart()`, with the 20 s cap; the typewriter's question is a message over Main and so live, the save screen after it holds.

**Needs a live 2-PC check.** Open the inventory, files, map, an item pickup prompt, an examine message and a locked door's message next to an enemy: enemies and the partner keep moving on both PCs, the menu player's character stands still and takes hits, the first hit freezes only that player's world until the menu closes; Esc pauses both in the same room (20 s cap); the typewriter holds the partner; the close's room reload and camera cut after a knock-back; a herb used in the menu; an item taken by the partner while the pickup prompt is open (item ledger); the play-time counter and timed events while a menu is open; an event's dialogue (a story event with EventPhaseIn) still holds both; World pause = Menus pause on the host pauses both machines' menus as in the vanilla game; single player unchanged.


## State mode: world digest (plan item 1, 2026-10-24; built and unit tested, not run live)

A canary for every replicated store: each machine tells the other a small digest of its world once a second, and a store that stays different while both machines stand at the same point of the host's flag order is reported and repaired with the existing snapshot. It closes the detection half of sync review F6 (a lost FLAG_PROPOSE, STOP or floor event shows as a digest mismatch at equal serials). Design: `coop-scratch\re1coop\IMPL_1_2_6_7.md` "Item 1". State mode only; `partner_mode=input` sends and registers nothing.

- **Message.** WORLD_DIGEST 0x0178 (every machine to all, unreliable, 1 Hz, a launcher fresh type in `launcher/games/re0.json`), 32 bytes: u32 flagSerial, u32 flagCrc, u32 enemyFlagCrc, u32 actFlagCrc, u32 authorityHash, u16 reserved (0), u16 loadSerial, u8 pendingProposals, u8 flags (bit 0 caughtUp), u16 scene, u32 senderMs. The decoder takes the exact size and refuses an unknown flag bit. Router policy Any.
- **Contents.** The three CRCs are CRC-32 over the 0x47 flag words with the presence flags (`event_rule::kPresenceFlags`, each machine's own) cleared: the enemy family (0x312..0x411), the act family (0x612..0x711) and everything else, so the log names the family. `flagSerial` is the host's issued serial or the guest's last applied one (`flag_sequencer::appliedSerial`), `pendingProposals` the proposals in flight (`pendingCount`), `authorityHash` an FNV-1a over (scene, owner, epoch) of every owned room in scene order (`room_authority::digest`). The floor journal size was compared too until the 2026-10-25 live run: each machine's journal is its own catch-up list for a joining guest, so its size differs by design; the run logged `diverged (floor items)` every 18 s, and every resync it asked for sent a new snapshot that moved the partner again (the "position correction" after a save reload). Floor items are no scope now. The game tick reads them every 250 ms into a mutex-guarded copy; the net thread sends the copy.
- **Judging.** A pair is compared only when both machines are caught up, `flagSerial` is equal and neither has a proposal in flight (different serials are a commit in transit, never a divergence). Each scope (flags, enemy flags, act flags, authority, floor items) has its own timer: a mismatch lasting 3 s gives one Diverged, the scope is then quiet for 15 s so a resync can land, and one still different after that is reported again as a repeat; equality after a Diverged gives one Converged. An incomparable pair ends every running mismatch.
- **Repair.** Log `world_digest: diverged (<scope>) at serial N` and F8 `digest sent/diverged`. Authority: `room_authority::resendClaims()` first, `resync::request("world digest: authority")` on a repeat. Every other scope: `resync::request("world digest: <scope>")` (a guest asks for a snapshot, a host sends RESYNC_REQUEST). `world_digest: converged (<scope>)` follows when the stores agree again.
- **Tests.** `world_digest_test` (wire, CRC check value, presence flags never change a CRC, family edges, every canary rule including "different serials never diverge");
- **Live gate.** Two PCs, a long session with doors, keys and fights: no `world_digest: diverged` line and F8 `digest sent/diverged` shows the second number at 0. One PC: `run t7_flags` in both seats passes `T7.digest-equal` and `T7.digest-flip`. Real packet loss (the F6 drops, `--link` profiles): a diverged line, then a resync, then a converged line.

## Hang watchdog (plan item 6, 2026-10-24; built and unit tested, not run live)

A frozen game leaves no crash, so no dump. The render thread beats once per drawn frame and a watcher thread dumps the stalled thread, in every partner mode (diagnostics, no wire). Design: `coop-scratch\re1coop\IMPL_1_2_6_7.md` "Item 6".

- **Beat.** `crash_dump::heartbeat()` runs first in the EndScene detour (`d3d9_hook`), so loading screens and menus beat too: it stores the tick count and the caller's thread id. At the first beat after the game tick has run it logs `crash_dump: frame thread N, game tick thread M` once (`game_tick::gameThreadId`), which shows whether the frame is drawn on the game thread.
- **Rule.** `hang_rule::HangWatch` (pure): one Dump per stall at `kHangMs` 20000 of stall time; the time only accumulates while the game window is foreground (`window_focus::gameIsForeground`, a background game may be throttled by Windows), losing focus keeps what was counted, a beat clears it and re-arms. Nothing before the first beat.
- **Dump.** `crash_dump::startHangWatch()` (from `init.cpp`, right after the first `crash_dump::install`) polls once a second. On a Dump it suspends the beating thread just long enough to read its context and 16 stack words, resumes it (the dump writer suspends every thread itself, and a thread suspended while it holds the log's or the heap's lock would stall the writer), then writes `coop\hang-<date>-<time>.dmp` through the same `writeDump` as a crash with a synthetic exception record, code 0xE0484E47 at that thread's EIP, named as the exception thread (`ClientPointers` false). Log: `crash_dump: no frame for 20 s (thread N eip 0x.. = module+offset), dump written to ...` and `crash_dump: stack at 0x..: <16 words>`. `tools/re0/dump_stack.py` prints `hang at 0x..` instead of `exception ... at` for that code and then the same return addresses. The launcher's `crashFiles` list names `coop/crash-*.dmp` only, so a hang dump is not in a report bundle until `coop/hang-*.dmp` is added there.
- **Dev command.** adapter.ini `dev_commands=1` (default off, any partner mode) makes `dev_command::onNetTick` read and delete `coop\cmd.txt` about once a second: `stall <ms>` (1..60000) makes the next game tick sleep that long once (`dev_command: stall N ms`), `lock` and `rescue` are wired by the rescue chord (see "Rescue chord and orphan lock"), any other line is logged as refused. Parser `dev_command_rule.h`, unit tested.
- **Tests.** `hang_rule_test` (nothing before the first beat, a beating game never dumps, one dump per stall, a beat re-arms, a background stall never dumps (fails with the foreground guard removed), focus regained continues the count, clock wrap); `dev_command_rule_test`.
- **Live gate.** One PC, `dev_commands=1`: write `stall 25000` into `coop\cmd.txt` with the game in the foreground. About 20 s later `adapter.log` has `crash_dump: no frame for 20 s`, one `coop\hang-*.dmp` exists and `python tools\re0\dump_stack.py <dmp>` prints `hang at` with the sleeping game thread's stack; one dump per stall. The same stall with another window in front writes no dump. The first beat's line shows whether EndScene runs on the game thread (if it does not, the dump is of the render thread, which is the one that stopped drawing).

## The game's own UI art and font (static, 2026-10-25)

- **Message font**: `nativePC/arc/message/msg_<lang>.arc` holds `ui/00_font/font_<lang>` (GFD, type 0x2d462600) and its atlas `font_eng_00_ID` (1024 x 256 BC3, white glyphs on transparent; a clean sans, the font of item and examine messages). GFD: magic `GFD` + NUL, glyph count at +0x1c (0x12b for English), the atlas path string, then 16-byte glyphs {u32 code; u32 x = bits 8..19, y = bits 20..31; u32 width = bits 8..19, height = bits 20..31; u32 advance = bits 0..7, x offset = bits 16..23}; line height 27. Text can be set with it offline (scratch `gfd_render.py`); the overlay can draw from the same atlas once it is loaded.
- **Dialog panel**: `ui/resident.arc` `ui/14_dialog/tex/Dialog_00_ID_HQ` (1024 x 512, BC3 YCbCr): the dark leather panel (0,0)-(720,395) and the two metal rods with end caps (top (0,397)-(746,443), bottom (0,446)-(746,494)); the same archive has the pause texture `ui/24_pause/tex/pause_eng_ID`, the key guide and the loading art.
- **Out of the install, never committed**: the launcher (`UiArt.cs`, before each game start) writes `coop/ui/font_eng.png`, `font_eng.gfd`, `dialog_leather.png`, `dialog_rod_top.png`, `dialog_rod_bottom.png` and `outfits/<value>.png` from the player's own files; a part is written again only when its archive changed (`art.key`). Verified on the real files: the GFD count (299) is at +0x1c, a u32 path length (19, without its NUL) at +0x34, the path at +0x38 and the glyph records start after its NUL; the atlas rows are packed closer than the 27 line height, so neighbouring rects overlap and text still reads cleanly.
- **Options screen**: `ui/option.arc`: the machine frame `op_bg_02_win_ID` (1280 x 720), value slots and rods in `option00_ID`, the embossed label atlases `op_wd0x_<lang>_ID` (labels are pictures, not a font).

## Co-op HUD (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live: docs/TESTPLAN_RE0_STATE.md "T19")

The overlay (`debug_overlay`, the frame owner) draws the HUD into ImGui's foreground draw list; the parts are `hud_partner`, `hud_notice`, `hud_banner` and `hud_coop_scene` (`hud.h`). Every size and position below is written at 720 lines and multiplied by the backbuffer height / 720; text shadows are one reference pixel (at least one whole pixel).

**Assets.** Typefaces: `adapters/re0/fonts` (Cinzel Medium and Bold, Cormorant Garamond Medium Italic, each with its OFL text) is a profile folder entry that installs to `coop/fonts`. Google Fonts ships these as variable fonts and ImGui's loader cannot pick an instance, so the files are static instances (`fontTools.varLib.instancer`, wght 500 and 700; neither family declares a reserved font name). `hud_text::load` adds them after the default font (ImGui's dynamic fonts take any size per draw); a missing file leaves its face on the default font, logged once. The game's own art is extracted by the launcher (section "The game's own UI art and font"); `ui_textures` turns each `coop/ui/*.png` into a managed-pool A8R8G8B8 texture (WIC) on the render thread on first use and releases them with the ImGui context; a missing file is logged once and its part is drawn without it. `gfd_text` draws the game's message font from `font_eng.gfd` (pure reader `gfd_rule`) and `font_eng.png`, one image per glyph with a one pixel black shadow; without them it uses the default font at the same line height.

**Partner tag** (replaces the top-right status line; data `partner_hud` -> `debug_stats::setPartnerTag`). Bottom left, 36 from the left and 30 from the bottom, the whole tag at opacity 0.75, texts shadowed. Line 1: the partner's character name in Cinzel Medium 13, capitals, letter spacing 0.2 em, #E6DCC0; 10 on, a 44 x 14 heartbeat trace of 1.5 px in the condition's colour (Fine #5FBF74, Caution #D9A93A, Danger #D0503F). The trace is one, two or three beats of (flat, a small rise, a deep fall, back to flat) and sweeps left to right, only the part behind the sweep drawn, once per 1.6, 1.1 and 0.7 s (`partner_tag_rule`). Line 2, 2 under line 1, only while the partner is in another room or in a menu: Cormorant Garamond Italic 14, #BFB397 (`partner_status::Tag::detail`). Nothing without a partner.

**Wait notices** (`wait_notice`, the world hold's data, unchanged). A loading partner: bottom centre, baseline 56 above the bottom, "WAITING FOR PARTNER" in Cinzel Medium 14, spacing 0.32 em, #E0D6BA, the whole notice pulsing between opacity 0.55 and 0.9 over 2.4 s; 10 below, a 140 hairline (#E0D6BA at 0.18) with a 46 segment (0.85) sweeping left to right every 1.8 s. A partner's pause or message: a black veil of 0.55 over the screen and, centred, a 220 hairline (#B09660 at 0.55), 10, the text ("KESTREL HAS PAUSED", "WAITING FOR KESTREL") in Cinzel Bold 20, spacing 0.34 em, #ECE2C6, 10, a second hairline. No sub line.

**Banners** (`debug_overlay::toast(text, seconds, style)`, `toast_queue`; the game's font, sentence case; fade in 0.5 s, hold, fade out 0.6 s, `toast_rule`). A line: bottom centre, its bottom edge 70 above the bottom, text at the font's native 27 line height, on a band of black at 0.5 with 40 side padding whose edge is feathered by five larger and fainter rectangles. A panel (important messages): top centre, 36 from the top, 520 wide: the top rod (520 x 32), the leather (inset 6, 52 high, running 22 under each rod), the bottom rod (520 x 33), the text centred on the leather; a plain dark panel while the dialog art is missing. Messages: Line "<name> joined the game." / "<name> left the game."; "Team up."; "Split up."; "<character> stayed behind."; "Resumed."; "Resyncing." (a resync and a persisting room desync); "Rescue: freed."; "Room reloaded."; "Waiting for partner." (a door held for the partner); Panel "Quit and rejoin."; "Only the host can save the game."; "The session's save did not load."; "Host left, returning to main menu.". The three diagnostics that were toasts (a stopped game_tick callback, state mode incomplete, a partner on the other partner mode) are only in the F8 panel's error line (`debug_stats::setError`) and the log now.

**Beside the Co-op page** (`coop_scene`, published by `coop_menu` from the page's own refresh each frame, so it goes away 300 ms after the page does, drawn by `hud_coop_scene`). The host's character in its current outfit on the left (picture 470 high from y 150, x 40), the guest's on the right (ending 44 from the right edge); the saved outfit shows the character's default picture (Rebecca 02, Billy 05). Under each at y 468, inside the frame's window under the rows, x 294 to 990 (the host left-aligned under the labels, the guest right-aligned under the values): the character's name in the game's font and a dimmed (#7D7764) line, "You / Host" or "<name> / Host", "You / Ready" or "<name> / Ready" ("Not ready" while the guest's game is not waiting on the page with a matching save). With no guest the right picture is the guest character's default outfit as a black silhouette pulsing between 0.25 and 0.45 over 3 s, its name dimmed and the line "Open". Positions are scaled by height only; on a wider screen than 16:9 the captions stay in the 270 to 1010 box.

## Pause menu: the Co-op row (static, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

The Esc menu is `uGUIPause` (vtable 0xcdfe68; owned by sGamePause [0xdcbea8], whose +0x24 is 0 closed, 1 open, 2 closing). Its layout `ui\24_pause\pause` has two rows, i_exit (Back, commonmsg 0x8a, help 0x8c) and i_reset (Quit Game, 0x8b, help 0x8d), at Y 400 and 440, the arrow at 400, the help line iMes_message at 500 (scratch `MENUS.md` section 4). Its state +0x260: 1 open (0x5ccb40: cursor +0x268 = 0, labels, arrow 400), 3 input (0x5ccc60: two rows, wrap at 1, help from the 2-entry table 0xcdfe4c = {0x8c, 0x8d}, arrow moved by 40.0 at 0xcbdf74), 4 the quit dialog, 5 close, 6 closed.

- **Hooks through the vtable**, since the code bytes are encrypted on disk and can't be checked offline: `.rdata` is plain in `re0hd.exe`, and slot 8 = 0x5cc820 (move), slot 55 = 0x5cc8f0 (onLoaded), uGameGUI's slot 8 = 0x5efb50 were read from the file. `pause_coop::enable` patches the two slots only when they still hold those addresses.
- **The row**: after onLoaded, `uGUI::cloneInstance` 0x8836c0 (thiscall on the GUI, the source instance) copies i_reset, `cGUIInstance::addChild` 0x8758d0 (thiscall on i_reset's parent, +0x68) links it, 0x875030(1) as the engine does on its own clones (uGUIInGameMessage::v55 0x5be8a0), and the copy's label object (instance +0xe8 array, [1]) is set to "<CNTR>Co-op" with cGUIObjMessageGame::setString. Rows move to 400 / 440 / 480 (Back, Co-op, Quit Game), the help line to 540.
- **Input** in state 3 is ours (`pause_coop_rule::step`): uGameGUI::v8, then the merged menu bits, the pause's own cancel (the pad's cancel mask | 8, keyboard +0x160 bit 0x40000), up / down wrapping over three rows, mouse clicks on the rows' touch polygons. Back: decide sound, sGamePause resume 0x6016f0(1), state 5. Quit Game: decide sound, state 4 (the game's quit dialog). Cancel: cancel sound, state 5. The help line hides while the cursor is on Co-op.
- **Co-op**: closes the pause like Back; once sGamePause +0x24 is 0, `native_page::openNext("coop.ingame")`, stop reasons 7 and 8 set (the status screen's: they leave the menu line 0x16 running, while the pause's reason 6 (mask 0x9fefff7e from the table 0xdb5ef0 read from the file) stops it, so the Options screen cannot run under the pause), then `uOptionManager::open` 0x5cc350 on sSubMenu +0x68 (the manager the status screen's Options tab opens). The page (`coop_menu_rule::inGameView`: Pause in Menus, Back; cancel closes) shows the characters as on the title. When `takeClosedFlag` 0x5cb320 says it closed, reasons 7 and 8 are cleared.
- **Live fix (Build 196 -> next).** The first build logged `Co-op page opening` and nothing after: the Options manager's unit only moves and draws while its `+0xc` bits 0x400 / 0x800 are on, which sSubMenu sets when its own Options open (0x5d8040) and clears on close, so the router never ran. The open now sets both and the close clears them. And the open / close checks ran on game_tick, which is driven by the player's move (`uPlayerBase::move`): stop reasons 7 and 8 stop the player, so the tick stopped with them and the hold was never released (the host stood frozen). They now run as a debug_overlay frame step (EndScene, the same thread as the game tick). The same stall stopped the host's once-a-second OWNERSHIP resend, so a guest whose game restarted while its launcher stayed in the session never learned its character (it played as "unknown", like a spectator): the resend now runs on the net tick (`character_owner::onSession`).
- A fault in either detour gives the game's own two-row pause back for the session. Logs: `pause_coop: Co-op row added to the pause menu`, `Co-op page opening`, `Co-op page closed, back to the game`.

## Session gating: partner tag, game over, load-in lines (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

- **Partner tag** shows only while this game and the partner's are both in play (`lobby::inGame` for the partner's slot) and the partner's last state is under 2 s old (`partner_status::shows`). A partner who closed the game, left the session or went back to the title loses the tag within 2 s on both sides.
- **Game over**: the title starts at its main menu (state 0x1f) only when it came back from play (0xdcc131); the boot starts at 0 and a pause-menu quit at 6. In a co-op session such a title opens the Co-op page by itself once its main menu waits for input (0x20, fade done, no load running), by setting the Co-op row's own choice (0x2b). Both machines leave play on a game over (host_exit takes the guest out), so both land on the page.
- **Load-in lines** ("Are the faint sounds of footsteps those of survivors?") are commonmsg 0x58 to 0x5f, shown by the event script's `mes_common` op through `sGameGUI::showCommonMessage` 0x5d5060 (callers: mes_common, mes_commonSE, save_point, cRoomPhaseMain::v7). The hook leaves those ids unshown; the op's next step finds no message busy and goes on as after a closed message.

## TEAM doors: "stayed behind" (live fix, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Two faults behind the toast. (1) The runner's own door load stops its game tick about 8 s; the partner's DOOR_FOLLOW_RESULT arrived within half a second (guest `serial 1 accepted` 17:34:57.887) but the runner's tick, once back, expired the request before draining the results (host 17:35:06.065 `serial 1 timed out` then `result for serial 1 is not the pending request`). `door_follow::onTick` now reads the answers first and `FollowTracker` waits `kAnswerTimeoutMs`. (2) A follower mid-action, in a menu or leading a script answered Busy at once (guest serials 4, 5, 6 `rejected (busy)`); it now keeps the request and judges it again every tick for `kFollowDeferMs` (5 s, `room_move_rule::defers`, `door_follow: serial N busy here, waiting to follow` once) before refusing.

## Second save from the Co-op page: guest never placed (live fix, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Slot 9 loaded from the Co-op page after a game over: the host's save view had Billy in 0x40 and Rebecca in 0x24, the guest's game had Billy in the loaded 0x24 (`room_move: sent Billy -> scene 0x24`, triggers fired as Billy there). `join_sync::apply` saw the snapshot's 0x40 and waited for the game's switch, whose guard skipped every tick because Billy's record was not 0x40 ("a door is bringing it"), so the guest never placed. Unplaced, `pose_capture` sent no new poses and its held resend kept repeating the previous game's last pose (room 0x40): the host's `room_move` self-heal put the Billy puppet back in 0x40 a second after every ROOM_MOVE, so the host never saw the guest and the partner tag went with the stale state. Now a switch-mode wait places the guest where it stands once its own character is in the loaded room (`join_sync: own character already in the loaded scene ..., placed there`, its own position kept), and the held resend waits for placement.

## Observed enemies keep their hit shapes (live fix, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Build 202's shot probe on the partner (17:13): every missed wide scan tested the enemy's shapes (3 tested, 0 hit) and the nearest zombie had one shape whose base sat on its body, about 105 units below the ray. The zombie family's one hit point is a capsule from the feet up to a height taken from the joints, rebuilt every frame by 0x418710 (collision spheres 0 and 1, `setHitPoint(0, ..., mode 0, 10)`) inside `EnemyBaseUpdate` 0x420f70, the slot-41 update the observer skips under CharPause, so the copy's capsule kept the height it had when it was made. `enemy_observer_game::refreshHitShapes` runs the class's builder after each observed move, looked up by the class's slot 41 in `game::kHitShapeBuilders`. The table came from walking every enemy update's callees (decomp export) for setHitPoint 0x5293a0 / setCollisionSphere callers, keeping the ones that only read the enemy's state (record, flags, HP, joints) and write shapes, spheres and a few cached vectors: zombie family 0x418710, uEnemy2a 0x4d21a0, 2c 0x440790, 2f 0x44c1b0 (called from its think 0x445d60), 30 0x451710, 31 0x457900, 35 0x46bc00 (takes the enemy again, ret 4), 37/38 0x470720, 3a/3e 0x483290, 3c 0x48fa60, leeches 0x498e00 (clears +0x6a2c bits 0 and 1), 43 0x4a4060, 47 0x4c0650. Left out: uEnemy2b (only attack points per frame, 0x4cc520 / 0x4cc5c0), uEnemy34 (its builder 0x45cbb0 takes the AI field +0x6ae8 and writes AI state; needs its own look if its shots miss), and 39, 3bRebecca, 44, 45, 46 and uEnemyBase, whose updates set no shapes (set once at start, which the observer runs natively). The probe line now also gives the capsule's length.

## Shot probe and still frames (live, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

- **Shotgun misses are not the skip bits.** Build 201's trace on the partner (16:58 to 16:59): 10 shots, 4 hits sent, every one applied by the host, `enemy_drift` summaries with 0 skip onsets, 0 corrections, 0 anomalous shots. The pellets never touched the local copy's hit shapes. The wide scan 0x52fd10 (thiscall weapon, player, type, flag; ret 0xc) builds its ray with `calcHitscanRay` 0x50e9f0 (origin +0x30, direction +0x20), lists the same-room `uCharaBase` units' hit shapes (0x528d10: unit +0x10a0, 20 entries of 0x50 bytes, word 0 the owner, radius +0x30), tests enemy kinds (> 0xf, not skipping) with 0x5301a0 (stdcall shape, ray, radius, out; ret 0x10) and keeps a hit no nearer player shape blocks. A shape's centre (0x5267a0) is its offset through the unit's matrix at +0xb0 (mode 0), the unit position plus offset (mode 1) or absolute. `shot_probe` logs each scan that hit no enemy: the nearest living enemy's shape gap to the ray, its shape count, the shape's and the +0xb0 matrix's distance from the body, and how many enemy shapes the scan tested. A large matrix distance means the observer's matrix is stale (shapes left behind); a small gap with 0 tested means the enemy was not a candidate; a large gap means the aim.
- **Still frames.** A zombie lying in wait holds motion 0x1b at frame 0 on the simulator; the observer played the clip on (standing up) and the 60-move drift cooldown sought it back every ~1.4 s. `SeekTracker::update(..., holdsStillOwner)` (enemy observers only) holds an owner frame that stayed put for `kStillOwnerTicks` moves: a puppet more than a frame off it is set back at once (`Verdict::Hold`, not logged).

## Dying enemies posed at full rate (live fix, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

A zombie killed while getting up finished standing on the partner's screen, then snapped to the ground. The simulator dropped a dead enemy from 20 poses a second to one corpse sample a second at its terminal entry (partner log `poses 1/s` right after `slot 1 terminal`; the host's fall went 0x1a, 0x12, then lying 0x16 about 2.4 s later), so the copy played its own motions between samples. `enemy_observer_send_rule` now poses a dying enemy every round for 5 s (`kDyingRounds`) after the terminal entry, then once a second.

## Join: own character in another room (live fix, 2026-10-25)

A save that left the two characters apart (Rebecca in 0x24, Billy in 0x2c) loads on the guest with the camera on the saved focus. `join_sync::apply` compared the snapshot's room with the own character's room record, which right after the load still matched, logged `own room 0x2c unknown door, staying` and placed the guest in the wrong room: Billy never loaded, the guest sent no poses, the host refused SESSION_READY (`stale pose`) for good, and the guest looked like a spectator. Now any own room other than the loaded one keeps the snapshot pending (`g_travelling`) and asks `character_owner::switchTo` every tick until the game's own zap has loaded that room (`join_sync: own character in scene 0x.., switching to it`); a door from the snapshot still travels as before.

## Peer drops of a held weapon: the floor model (live report 2026-10-25; static, built and unit tested, not run live)

Rebecca dropped the handgun she held from the inventory: the host saw it on the floor, the guest saw nothing (other drops showed). The weapon set was not the cause: a floor item's model is its own sGameChara set keyed {kind 0x71, item id} (`makeResourceKey` 0x5202e0 keeps any kind other than 2/3/5/7 as is), never the weapon's {0x74, weapon type} that `equip_refresh` releases. `sItemPut::put` 0x4de500 creates the uItem through 0x51fa20(0x71, item id, record) -> 0x51fa40, whose bind 0x51f570 only attaches a set already in the map (node +0x18) or a matching entry of the dependency list +0x5c8; it never requests one. The drop menu (0x5e1d10) requests it first: state 9 calls `requestItemModel(0x71, item)` at 0x5e49b9 (0x5e6230: `RequestSet` 0x5203d0 with the controlled player's record +0xff4), state 10 polls `RequestsLoaded` 0x520240 at 0x5e4a09 and only then puts at 0x5e4b84. The room script does the same for map items (0x581870: `RequestSet(0x71, item, 0)` per item, wait, put). No `ReleaseSet(0x71, ..)` call exists (only the clear-all 0x520770), so the partner presumably showed every item it had once seen placed or dropped itself, and lacked only models it never loaded, such as a weapon carried since the start. `floor_items_sync` now repeats the drop's step for a peer's puts: the loaded room's events (arrived and pending) are held in order, each put item's set is requested once (`floor_pending::putItemIds`, `game::kItemUnitKind`), and the batch applies once `RequestsLoaded` is true (the same tick when the models were there). A room change sends held events back to their room's queue. One log line per batch that had to wait: `floor_items_sync: N floor changes applied after M ms of item model loading`. Retest: drop the weapon in hand on one machine; the partner sees it on the floor and can pick it up.

## Shots through a living observed enemy (live fix, 2026-10-25)

The weapon hit scans (0x52fd10, 0x52f520) skip an enemy whose `+0x6a0c` has bit 1 (guns) or 2 (other kinds); the classes set and clear them (0x4cbd90 / 0x4cbd20) for knock-downs, the critical kill, death falls and lying down, and the stream copied them onto the observer a render delay late, so shotgun pellets passed through a zombie that looked like it walked. A living copy now shows them clear (`enemy_observer_rule::shownFlags6a0c`): every shot that lands on the guest's screen goes to the simulator, whose damage handler still refuses a hit the enemy is immune to; a dead copy keeps them.

## Enemy drift trace (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Catches the shotgun-pellet kind of bug (state the engine changes on an observed enemy that the hit path reads) live, without a log line per frame. State mode only (`enemy drift` module, after the observer). Rules in `enemy_drift_rule.h`, engine side in `enemy_drift.cpp`.
- **Where the comparison hooks in.** `unitMoveDetour` runs `drive` first, which writes the shown sample (`enemy_observer_game::write`, HP, record, hit attributes and `+0x6a0c`), then the original unit move with the CharPause bit held, then releases the bit; the check runs last (`enemy_drift::afterMove`, only for an enemy `drive` wrote this call). So the write is before the move, the compare after it, and what the move (or a hit reaction inside it) changed is what is compared. Frozen, leased-here and not yet constructed enemies are never compared.
- **Fields.** Hp, Alive (hp above 0 and the stream's alive flag against hp above 0), ShotSkip (`+0x6a0c & kShotSkipBits`, expected 0 on a living enemy, the stream's on a corpse), RecordState (word 0, skipped while the simulator's enemy is in its setup state) and Motion (`motion0`). Hp, Alive and ShotSkip are put back at once (`enemy_observer_game::reassert`: setHP when different, the `+0x6a0c` write) so a hit test later in the frame sees them; record and motion are only watched.
- **Onset.** A field must differ in every post-move compare for `kSettleMs` (300 ms; motion `kMotionSettleMs` 600 ms) before one line is written, `enemy_drift: slot 3 (Zombie) shot-skip bits here 1 sim 0 for 312 ms, corrected`; one line when it matches again, `... matches the simulator again after 1840 ms`. A drift that goes on is never repeated; a short mismatch is never reported.
- **Per shot (the shooter).** A trigger pull is the local player's muzzle flash (`weapon_fx_capture::recordFlash`, a listed site; a throw counts too). At fire time the living, freshly compared observed enemies with shot-skip bits set are counted. Hits routed by `enemy_net::routeOwnHit` within `kHitWindowMs` (250 ms) of a shot are counted on the newest shot (sent or applied here: routed; no route or send failure: refused); each HIT_APPLIED for the local character counts as an answer for the oldest shot still waiting. `kAnswerWindowMs` (1000 ms) after the pull the shot is judged: one line only when skip bits were set on a living observed enemy or applied is below routed, `enemy_drift: shot hits routed 3 applied 1 refused 0 after 1000 ms` or `enemy_drift: shot with 1 living observed enemies skipping shots, first slot 3 (Zombie) flags 0x1; hits routed 0 applied 0 refused 0`. A normal shot only bumps the summary.
- **No spam.** Every line above goes through `LineBudget` (8 lines per 10 s, the rest counted as suppressed). One summary line every 30 s, only when something was counted: `enemy_drift: 30 s summary: onsets hp 0 alive 0 skip 1 record 0 motion 2, corrections 41, shots 12 (1 anomalous), 0 lines suppressed`.
- **To read a log.** A `skip` onset or a shot line with skip bits is the pellets-through-a-zombie bug again; `corrections` counting up with no onset means the engine sets a field every frame and the correction held; a shot with `applied < routed` and no skip bits points at the simulator (request dropped, `enemy_net: hit for slot N dropped`) or a lost answer.

## Wait notices (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

The world hold (`menu_mirror`'s sUnit::updateAll detour) now has two reasons and says which on screen (`wait_notice`, drawn by the HUD: section "Co-op HUD").
- **A partner's screen** that holds the world (menu_hold_rule, unchanged): a pause menu, save screen or held menu shows `<name> has paused` over a dimmed world; a message or cutscene (`menu_hold_rule::unlimited` phases) shows `Waiting for <name>`. The name is the lobby's (`lobby::nameOf`). "Resumed" is toasted when it ends. The old "Waiting for partner" / "Resumed" toast pair is gone.
- **A partner still loading the session**: a fresh LOBBY_PRESENCE with place Loading (`lobby::partnerLoading`) holds a game that has had a controlled character for less than `wait_notice_rule::kLoadWaitWindowMs` (60 s), with `Waiting for partner` at the bottom; a game running longer does not wait (a mid-game join). A partner that stops announcing (6 s stale) ends it.
- **Doors**: no hold (each player moves through doors on its own in state mode).

## State mode: enemy guards from RE1-Coop (2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Five guards RE1-Coop runs live, ported to our enemy sync. Nothing on the wire changes except one new lease end reason.

- **Stuck grab.** `contact_rule::finished` ends a lease whose hold runs `kStuckGrabMs` (15 s) as `End::Stuck` (wire reason 8, `kEndCount` 9); the holder puts the victim back to neutral through Player_Reset 0x516440 (`contact_game::freeVictim`) before the enemy goes back to its simulator, which carries on from the holder's state (`carriesState`).
