Log `lease: token N held 15 s, victim freed`.
- **No boss built from a stream.** `enemy_observer_rule::createWanted` refuses the boss classes (`enemy_policy::bossClass`, moved there from `boss_wire`): their room's script builds them on both machines, and RE1-Coop found a factory-built boss crashes.
- **Missing-enemy watch.** `MissingWatch`: a record the simulator streams alive with no enemy here is logged once after `kMissingReportMs` (3 s) of sightings (`enemy_observer: record R (class) alive on the simulator, no enemy here for N ms`), counted as F8 `enemies missing here`; the create keeps retrying once a second, a boss is only reported.
- **Hit reach.** `contact_net::apply` refuses a PLAYER_HIT whose contact point, rebuilt around the local enemy, lands over `kMaxHitReachUnits` (600, a guess) from the victim's real position: the simulator's enemy struck a copy of the victim that stood elsewhere. Log `contact: hit on X from local slot N refused, it landed D units from where X stands`; tune the limit from those lines.
- **Same enemy twice.** `duplicateOf`: a second pool enemy with the same spawn record gets no stream data and stays held (frozen); logged once (`enemy_observer: slot N is record R again (first in slot M), held`). The engine's own create refuses a second build of a record (`UnitObject`), so this is a watch, not an expected case; the second copy is not hidden (no verified hide call for enemies).

## State mode: enemy identity by spawn record (plan item 7, 2026-10-24; built and unit tested, not run live)

An enemy stream entry is bound to the local enemy by the engine's own identity of the enemy, the spawn record it was created from, not by the pool slot, which two machines do not have to agree on. State mode only; the wire does not change. Design: `coop-scratch\re1coop\IMPL_1_2_6_7.md` "Item 7". Ideas from the RE1-Coop study (`docs/re/PATTERNS.md`, host-owned enemies); nothing copied.

- **Finding: no flag is a per-enemy identity.** `0x612 + n` (n = the spawn id word) is the Act flag family, the enemy classes' "may act" gate: `getFlag(spawnId + 0x612)` in 0x457460, 0x429f90 and 0x433860 (phase 15 evidence ev_12367cab... and ev_1cc89c2f..., re-read in the decompile `0040ea70.c`, `004112b0.c` on 2026-10-24). Several records share one spawn id word and the uEnemy30 death handler clears it, so the bit says "may act", not "this enemy" and not "killed". `0x312 + n` (enemy family) is a spawn enable shared by a group of records: `EnemyCreate` 0x410bb0 refuses a record whose `+0x3c` flag number is non-zero while `getFlag(0x312 + it)` is 0, `createEnemyForFlag` 0x4109f0 creates every record of the scene carrying that number and `removeEnemyGroup` 0x411ec0 removes them. This corrects the "kill flag is 0x612 + it" of "Enemy creation seeded" in the enemy notes above: that bit is the Act gate, not a kill marker.
- **The identity the engine keeps.** The unit registry `[0xdcc010]` holds 1000 spawn records of 0x5c bytes (`game::kSpawnRecordCount`; the create accepts indices below 0x4e8, `game::kSpawnTableEntries`, and 1000 and up are a free list the registry's setup 0x410140 chains), global across rooms and written into the save (`saveToSave` 0x4115d0 copies all 1000 and the 0xaf scene heads). `EnemyCreate` stores its record index in the unit: the setter 0x5295c0 is `mov eax,[esp+4]; mov [ecx+0xff0],eax; ret 4` (REA ev_5369ecf4...), the sGameChara create 0x51fa40 sets -1 first, the getter 0x526510 returns `+0xff0` (`game::kUnitRecordOffset`). `UnitObject` 0x51fed0 (thiscall on sGameChara, walks the 90 entries comparing the getter; REA ev_65a2c13e...) is the engine's own record to unit lookup and `EnemyCreate` uses it to refuse a second build; `scene::unitObject` wraps it.
- **`enemy_spawn`.** `originOf(enemy)` reads `+0xff0` (SEH-safe; -1 or 1000 and up is nothing) and the spawn id (+0x10) from that record of the registry, the way the create reads it. The per-slot notes that only fed it are gone: this removes the live failure mode of an enemy whose create was never bound to a pool slot having no origin and so never streaming. The pending list stays for forgetting a reused slot's decision and follow state. `createOnDemand` asks `scene::unitObject(record)` instead of its own "already created" scan, which also covers an enemy not yet in the pool.
- **Receiver (`enemy_observer`).** `World::origin` holds each pool enemy's origin, read every tick. A pose entry is bound to the local enemy created from `entry.spawnRecord` (`enemy_observer_rule::slotOfRecord`), and applies only when class, spawn id and record all match (`bound`; another record in the same pool slot is refused). Each per-slot state (timeline, motions, hold) is indexed by the local pool slot; the stream's pool slot is kept per binding (`Slot::streamSlot`, a binding moves when the simulator reuses a slot) because ENEMY_MOTION entries carry no record and are routed through it, and because a hit names the simulator's slot. `createWanted` is "no local enemy has that record", so a script spawn that landed in another pool slot is bound and driven instead of created twice (the second half of review finding F9). A motion that arrives before its enemy's first pose is dropped; the layer-0 seek brings the motion back. Leases stay keyed by pool slot (`enemy_lease`), so they still assume the two pools agree; a divergent pool is not a case for them yet.
- **Hits.** `enemy_net::routeOwnHit` sends HIT_REQUEST with the simulator's slot, `enemy_observer::streamSlotOf(localSlot)`; an enemy with no binding keeps its own slot as before (Legacy classes are never bound), and the receiver still checks the class vtable. No wire change.
- **Wire.** `enemy_observer_wire::valid` refuses `spawnRecord >= kSpawnRecordCount`. The boss hand-over's member loses its zero pad for the same field (`boss_wire::Member::spawnRecord`, same 40 bytes, same bound) so the new authority finds each member by record: `boss_handover::localSlotOf` resolves it through `enemy_observer::slotOfRecord`, `rule::Local` and `fitOf` compare it, and holds, restarts and resumes go to the local slot.
- **Adopt on hand-over.** The previous simulator sends one reliable ENEMY_POSE round of every streamed enemy (`enemy_observer_capture::sendFinal`, same layout, alive or terminal) on the first tick of its own door out of a room it simulates with the peer in it: the predicate `boss_handover` already used for its prepare, now one shared rule `door_leave_rule::Departure` for both. The new simulator's `handOver` takes `SlotTimeline::newest()` (the last sample received, not the one rendered) when it arrived at most `kAdoptMaxAgeMs` (3000) ago and writes it through `enemy_observer_game::write` (position, rotation, HP through setHP when it differs, the record words) before the start rule (`handOverStart`, setAction at step 0); an older or missing sample, or a `removed` one, leaves the enemy's own state to be read as before. The RE1-Coop notion of adopting on reunion needs nothing here: the room authority keeps the room with the machine that was in it first.
- **Trace lines.** `enemy_observer: authority -> here (scene 0x%02x e%u), %u enemies resumed (%u at step 0, adopted %u)`; `enemy_observer: final round of %zu enemies sent`; `enemy_observer: slot %u entry refused, stream <class>/<spawn>/record <r>, here <class>/<spawn>/record <r>` (the slot is the local pool slot).
- **Tests.** `enemy_observer_rule_test` (binding by record with different pool slots, which fails with the equal-slot lookup back; another record in the same pool slot refused; `createWanted` false when the record is in another slot; `newest()` is the last pushed sample, not the rendered one; the adopt age limit and its clock wrap), `enemy_observer_wire_test` (record 999 accepted, 1000 refused), `boss_wire_test` and `boss_handover_test` (member records, same spawn id from another record refused, `Departure` latch).
- **Live gate (two PCs, X4 and X5).** LEAVE_BEHIND: the authority walks out of a room with live zombies: the other machine's `authority -> here` line has adopted equal to resumed, no teleport, HP equal on both afterwards. A script spawn created on demand in another pool slot than the simulator's moves with the stream (no second copy, no `entry refused`). Shots from the non-authority machine still land once (`hit for slot %u sent to slot %d` names the simulator's slot).

## Rescue chord and orphan lock (plan item 2, 2026-10-24; built and unit tested, not run live)

A held world has no way out today except quitting. The rescue is a local recovery in every partner mode (it sends nothing; the reload's own ROOM_MOVE is the ordinary door post-hook), and a watchdog frees pause bits no script holds. Design: `coop-scratch\re1coop\IMPL_1_2_6_7.md` "Item 2". Ideas from the RE1-Coop study (`docs/re/PATTERNS.md`, teleport and rescue through a forced door); nothing copied.

- **Engine facts.** `ScriptOp_EventPhaseOut` 0x56cd10 (the engine's end of an event) clears sGameInfo +0x50 bits 0x2 and 0x4000 (REA ev_8447d2fd..., bytes `83 20 fd` and `81 20 ff bf ff ff`) and calls `sRoomControl::popPhase` 0x610df0 (`add ecx,0xb8; jmp 0x60a330`), whose phase manager pop is `dec [ecx+0x3c]; requested(+0x18) = stack(+0x1c)[depth]; ret` with no depth guard (REA ev_cff2fc8b..., ev_fe2de14a...): a pop at depth 0 reads below the stack. Its pair `EventPhaseIn` 0x56cbe0 requests phase 13 (Event) and sets 0x2 and 0x4000. Phase manager: +0x14 current, +0x18 requested (-1 none), +0x3c stack depth. `sDoorLoad::start` 0x552b50 takes (room, entry, ...) and entries are at most 15.
- **Chord.** `command_input` (net thread) polls controller L3 and R3 together (`XINPUT_GAMEPAD_LEFT_THUMB | RIGHT_THUMB`, recorded by the `pad_commands` filter as `Buttons::rescue`, never hidden from the game) or keyboard F7 (`virtual_keys::realKeyDown`, foreground only like the other keys). Held `kHoldMs` (3000) it is one press (`rescue_rule::Chord`; release before the next). No peer or gameplay is needed. A press ends the peers' hold on this machine's world at once (`menu_mirror::releasePeerHold`, from the net thread): a held world does not run the game tick, so the ladder could not otherwise start.
- **Ladder (`rescue_rule::Ladder`, game tick).** Per scene: press 1 Release, press 2 Reload, later ones the toast `Quit and rejoin`. It starts over on a scene change (a room still loading is not one) or `kLadderResetMs` (30000) after the last press. A Reload is refused, and falls to the toast, with a door running, a menu open, a boss alive (a living pool enemy in `game::kBossRoomVtables`: `kBossVtables` plus uEnemy43 0xcc25d8 and uEnemy47 0xcc3710), no last door of the own character into the loaded scene, or the scene already reloaded since the player arrived in it (the second rescue in a scene only frees; fails with that guard removed in `rescue_rule_test`).
- **Release.** `event_sync::releaseFollowed()` (the release a lost peer does), the engine's end of event when `rescue_rule::releasePlan` allows it (`popEvent`: the current phase is Event, nothing requested, depth above 0, no script thread active; bits 0x2 and 0x4000 cleared, `popPhase` called under SEH, both pop sites' first bytes checked first and refused with a log line on a mismatch), `demo_skip::skipLocal()` (a demo in scheduler state 5 takes the engine's skip branch, the one a peer's skip takes; only exists in state mode, nothing is sent), the pause bits through `event_sync::releaseHeldPause(PauseRelease::Rescue)` (at the next script update, plain gameplay, no busy thread; clears the cutscene bits and the event phase bit; the resync key still clears the cutscene bits only) and the dropped peer hold. Log `rescue: released (followed %u, event phase popped|-, demo skipped|-, pause bits 0x%x, menu hold dropped|-)`, toast `Rescue: freed`.
- **Reload.** `door_sync::runRescue(change)` runs the own character's `lastDoor` whose room is the loaded scene through the local path `runFollow` uses (`split_rooms::beforeLocalDoor`, then the door; no DOOR_FOLLOW, the partner stays; the door is not marked shared). Logs `rescue: reloading scene 0x%02x through entry %u`, `rescue: reload refused (door active|menu open|boss alive|no door into this scene|already reloaded in this scene)`, `rescue: nothing left to try in scene 0x%02x`; toasts `Rescue: room reloaded`, `Quit and rejoin`. Unverified: that the room script replay after the reload leaves no stale state; the second press is what a live run must try.
- **Orphan lock (`rescue_rule::OrphanLock`, game tick).** Lock bits = sGameInfo +0x50 and (`kCutscenePauseBits` or `kEventPhaseBit` 0x4000). Set for `kOrphanMs` (4000) with no active script thread (`event_sync::scriptThreadActive`) in plain gameplay (`game_state::playing`) they are released through the same request; a thread or leaving plain gameplay restarts the count, and so does a release. Log `rescue: lock bits 0x%x held 4 s with no script, released`. Risk to measure: a vanilla script that sets pause bits and ends its thread legitimately for more than 4 s.
- **Dev commands.** `lock` sets `kTriggerPauseBit | kPlayerPauseBit` in plain gameplay with no script thread (`dev_command: lock, pause bits 0x%x set`, or `lock refused`), `rescue` is one chord press. The `hang watchdog` section's note that they were not wired is superseded.
- **Tests.** `rescue_rule_test` (chord timing and wrap, ladder order, reset by scene and by time, each refusal and their order, the second reload in a scene refused, orphan lock 4 s and its resets, `PopEvent` never at depth 0 and never with a requested phase or a running thread; the depth guard and the reload guard each fail their check when removed).
- **Live gate (one PC, `dev_commands=1`).** `lock` in plain gameplay: about 4 s later `rescue: lock bits 0xa held 4 s with no script, released` and the character moves again. Hold L3+R3 (or F7) 3 s in a room entered through a door: `rescue: released (...)` and `Rescue: freed`; hold again: `rescue: reloading scene` and the room loads again at the door, the partner stays where it is; a third time and a boss room: `Quit and rejoin`. Stuck Event phase (a cutscene cut short): the first press logs `event phase popped`. A peer's menu hold (Message or cutscene on the other machine) ends at the press and returns only if the peer's next MENU_STATE still says it holds.

## Wrong door and ladder prompts: the room message table (live fix, 2026-10-25; built and unit tested, not run live)

Build 204, both machines: a ladder said "The door is now unlocked", a door asked "Go up the ladder?", another door said it was blocked by swords. Not event_sync: every fired and followed thread names the same trigger and pc on both machines. The guest's Rebecca puppet left sh08 (scene 0x2c) for sh1c (0x40) at 17:37:26.740 (`scene: 0x2de626e0 now in scene 0x40 entry 0 (loaded scene 0x2c)`), and 1.4 s later Billy's own ladder, sh08 trigger 5 (`mes 0`, then the door to 0x40 on a yes), opened with sh1c's message 0, the unlock line of sh1c trigger 5 (`ResFlag 0x22`, 0: `mesSE 0,0,0xb33; FlagSet 0x22`). On the host the Billy puppet moved back to 0x2c at 17:37:34.917 while 0x40 was loaded, and Rebecca's door, sh1c trigger 5 at 17:39:40.811, asked sh08's message 0, the ladder's yes/no. Cause: `mes`/`mesSE` show `sGameGUI::showMessage(9, n)`, entry n of the room message table in sGameGUI slot 9 (+0x17c). `SceneRecord` 0x61e0f0 loads a record it has not got through 0x61b8e0, which releases slot 9 and loads that room's table (`message\scene\...`). The engine follows its record loads with 0x61dbe0(record), which puts that record's table in slot 9, passing the room it makes the loaded one (enterRoom 0x61e2c0, CamChangeArea 0x56ba20, the EventDemo phase 0x6081c0) or the player's record after loading another room (eventProc_2C 0x57fe40); `scene::move` did not, so every puppet move into a room not loaded (ROOM_MOVE leave, self-heal) left the other room's messages in place until the next real door. `scene::move` now calls 0x61dbe0 with the loaded record after `SceneRecord` returns another record. Check live: puppet leaves through a door, then examine a door or ladder with a message in the room still loaded.
